# What the fly brain computes and how it learns: eight briefs

*Written by eight independent research agents for the phase-3 pivot (docs/phase3/REFLECTION.md), from primary literature and from measurements on the MaleCNS v1.0 copy in this repository. Each brief marks its claims as established / promising / speculative and cites sources; citations flagged uncertain by an agent should be verified before reuse. The synthesis and the design decisions are in docs/phase3/DESIGN.md.*

## Mushroom body learning as computation: the KC/MBON/DAN compartment, its three-factor rule, and what a language-model engineer should and should not copy

*Key: `mushroom-body`.*

### Summary

"The mushroom body compartment computes an online, one-shot, error-corrected binary-to-scalar association: a sparse ~5%-active code over ~2,000 Kenyon cells, generated by a fixed random expansion of in-degree ~6 from ~50 glomeruli and kept sparse by a single graded GABAergic APL neuron, is read by ~21 output-neuron types in 15 compartments, each with its own dopaminergic teaching neuron. Lipshutz et al. (2023) show the compartment can be derived normatively as online linear discriminant analysis under the fly's real constraints: dopamine-driven plasticity only WEAKENS the synapse, and dopamine is temporally sparse.\n\nThe learning rule is three-factor and presynaptic-times-neuromodulator, not Hebbian: MBON firing is irrelevant to the weight change (Hige et al. 2015). Its distinctive feature is a biphasic timing kernel - Dop1R1-Gs-cAMP detects coincidence and depresses when the odor precedes dopamine, Dop1R2-Gq-Ca2+ detects order and potentiates when dopamine precedes the odor (Handler et al. 2019) - and DAN activation unpaired with KC activity potentiates (Cohn et al. 2015). In vector form this is precisely the delta rule, M_t = M_{t-1}(I - beta k k^T) + beta v k^T, i.e. DeltaNet; with the compartment's decay it is Gated DeltaNet. That identity explains this repository's Phase-1 result honestly: the MB memory as built (scalar surprise gate, per-head expansions, no per-key delta) was a weaker special case of its own baseline.\n\nSix properties of the biology are NOT in Gated DeltaNet and are the actual novelty surface: the signed biphasic write kernel; paired non-negative monotone channels read as a difference, so extinction adds an opposing trace rather than erasing (Felsenberg et al. 2018); a within-layer teaching graph where MBON->DAN feedback makes each head write the residual the others left (Bennett et al. 2021); fixed heterogeneity of sign, decay and capacity across compartments (Aso & Rubin 2016); a novelty head whose read gates writes and compute (Hattori et al. 2017); and active, state-dependent forgetting with a spacing-gated, sleep-replay consolidation into slow weights (Tully 1994; Isabel 2004; Berry 2015; Dag 2019).\n\nDo not copy: the specific PN->KC wiring (null here and random in the literature), the literal K=6 and f=5% constants (they are outputs of a formula in input dimension), non-negative depression-only weights as a virtue, Dale's law globally, spikes, anatomical I/O ports (-0.63 nat/token measured here), or dopamine-as-precision (no fly evidence). Do not expect a language-model backbone: the MB's learned output bandwidth is tens of scalars per event. Claim only in named regimes - rebinding recall, continual learning, knowledge editing, adaptive compute - against a matched Gated-DeltaNet twin."

### Established (with sources)

- ARCHITECTURE. The Drosophila mushroom body (MB) lobes are divided into 15 anatomical compartments that tile the parallel axons of ~2,000 Kenyon cells (KCs); 21 MBON types elaborate segregated dendrites in those compartments and 20 dopaminergic neuron (DAN) types project axons to one or at most two compartments each. Aso, Hattori, Yamada et al., eLife 3:e04577 (2014).
- The compartment is the unit of computation: each compartment = (a slice of the shared KC axon bundle) x (its own MBON dendrite) x (its own DAN axon). The KC code is SHARED across all compartments; only the readout weights and the teaching signal differ. Aso 2014 eLife 04577; Li, Lindsey, Marin et al., eLife 9:e62576 (2020).
- INPUT CODE. Each KC samples a small, apparently random combination of ~4-10 olfactory projection-neuron (PN) inputs (mean ~6 claws) out of ~50 glomeruli. Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel, Nature 497:113-117 (2013). The MaleCNS working tree measures 5.16 (L) / 5.07 (R) PN types per KC, median 5 (docs/CIRCUITS.md).
- Deviations from pure randomness exist but are modest: a core community of food-responsive PN types is over-represented in PN->KC connectivity (Zheng, Li, Fisher et al., Curr Biol 32:3334-3349 (2022)), and alpha/beta vs gamma KCs differ in what they sample (Cheng, Charng, Chen et al., Sci Adv 11, eadq9893 (2025)). This is consistent with this repository's Phase-1 null: real PN->KC wiring vs degree-preserving shuffle gave -0.0010 accuracy (-0.1 seed-SD, CI includes 0) on MQAR.
- SPARSITY. Odors activate a sparse subset of KCs (commonly quoted ~5-10%), enforced by negative feedback from a single GABAergic APL neuron per hemisphere that contacts essentially all KCs. Blocking APL raises KC responses, reduces sparseness and decorrelation, and selectively impairs discrimination of SIMILAR (not dissimilar) odors. Lin, Bygrave, de Calignon, Lee & Miesenbock, Nat Neurosci 17:559-568 (2014); Turner, Bazhenov & Laurent, J Neurophysiol 99:734-746 (2008); Honegger, Campbell & Turner, J Neurosci 31:11772-11785 (2011).
- APL inhibition is NOT a global k-WTA. APL activity and its inhibitory effect are spatially localized along the lobes, so each KC effectively inhibits itself more than it inhibits other individual KCs, and different compartments can be normalized differently. Amin, Apostolopoulou, Suarez-Grimalt, Vrontou & Lin, eLife 9:e56954 (2020).
- READOUT. KC->MBON synapses are cholinergic and excitatory; learning is expressed as dopaminergic tuning of these excitatory synapses. Barnstedt, Owald, Felsenberg et al., Neuron 89:1237-1247 (2016). MBONs are electrotonically compact and sum input from hundreds of KCs, i.e. the readout is close to a thresholded linear sum.
- PLASTICITY SITE AND SIGN. Pairing an odor with activation of the compartment's DAN produces odor-specific, synapse-specific DEPRESSION of KC->MBON, and induction depends strictly on the temporal ORDER of the two stimuli. The plasticity is heterosynaptic: it does not require MBON firing. Hige, Aso, Modi, Rubin & Turner, Neuron 88:985-998 (2015).
- PLASTICITY IS BIDIRECTIONAL. DAN activation UNPAIRED with KC activity potentiates KC->MBON, whereas DAN activation PAIRED with KC activity depresses it; both can be produced in the same preparation within minutes. Cohn, Morantte & Ruta, Cell 163:1742-1755 (2015).
- TWO RECEPTORS, TWO SECOND MESSENGERS, OPPOSITE SIGNS. Dop1R1-Galpha_s-cAMP detects temporal coincidence and drives depression; Dop1R2-Galpha_q-Ca2+ detects temporal ORDER and drives potentiation. Reversing the odor/dopamine order reverses both the synaptic change and the learned behavioural preference on a trial-by-trial basis. Handler, Graham, Cohn et al., Cell 178:60-75 (2019).
- COMPARTMENTS HAVE DIFFERENT LEARNING RULES. The 20 DAN types differ extensively in training requirements for memory formation, decay dynamics, storage capacity, and flexibility to learn new associations. A single DAN type can write an aversive memory, reduce an aversive memory, or write an appetitive memory depending only on when it fires relative to the odor. Aso, Hattori & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135 (2016).
- A subset of PAM DANs co-release nitric oxide, which acts with opposite sign and on a different timescale from dopamine, diversifying memory dynamics across compartments. Aso, Ray, Long et al., eLife 8:e49257 (2019).
- LOCAL ERROR LOOP EXISTS ANATOMICALLY. MBONs synapse onto DANs (feedback), MBONs synapse onto MBONs (lateral/feedforward inhibition, e.g. the GABAergic MBON-gamma1pedc>alpha/beta), and KCs synapse onto DAN axons reciprocally via nicotinic receptors. Aso 2014 eLife 04577; Li 2020 eLife 62576; Perisse, Owald, Barnstedt et al., Neuron 90:1086-1099 (2016); Cervantes-Sandoval, Phan, Chakraborty & Davis, eLife 6:e23789 (2017). MaleCNS measurements in this repo: 300-339 MBON->DAN edges (2,605-2,861 contacts), 255-282 MBON->MBON edges, 478-486 DAN->MBON edges.
- Recurrence is also present and required in the larva, where the full connectome shows DANs integrating recurrent MBON signals as well as local KC input. Eschbach, Fushiki, Winding et al., Nat Neurosci 23:544-555 (2020).
- EXTINCTION IS ADDITION, NOT ERASURE. Re-exposing a trained odor without the expected punishment recruits reward DANs downstream of avoidance-directing MBONs; the original aversive trace and a NEW opposing extinction trace co-exist and are integrated at the MBON level. Felsenberg, Jacob, Walker et al., Cell 175:709-722 (2018); Felsenberg, Barnstedt, Cognigni, Lin & Waddell, Nature 544:240-244 (2017).
- FORGETTING IS ACTIVE AND DOPAMINE-DRIVEN. Ongoing activity of MP1/MV1 (PPL1) DANs after training sets the rate of forgetting of both aversive and appetitive memories; the rate rises with locomotion and falls with rest. Berry, Cervantes-Sandoval, Nicholas & Davis, Neuron 74:530-542 (2012). Sleep facilitates memory specifically by blocking this DAN-mediated forgetting: Berry, Cervantes-Sandoval, Chakraborty & Davis, Cell 161:1656-1667 (2015). A distinct Dop1R2/DAMB-dependent pathway produces reversible TRANSIENT forgetting: Sabandal, Berry & Davis, Nature 591:426-430 (2021).
- MEMORY PHASES. A single aversive training session yields STM/MTM/ARM; protein-synthesis- and CREB-dependent LTM requires SPACED training (classically 10 sessions with 15-min rests), whereas massed training of the same total amount yields ARM. Tully, Preat, Boynton & Del Vecchio, Cell 79:35-47 (1994); ARM and LTM are mutually exclusive consolidated phases: Isabel, Pascual & Preat, Science 304:1024-1027 (2004).
- ONE-TRIAL LEARNING IS REAL BUT ASYMMETRIC BY VALENCE. A single 2-minute odor+sucrose pairing forms radish- and protein-synthesis-dependent appetitive LTM lasting days, while aversive LTM needs 5-10 sessions. Krashes & Waddell, J Neurosci 28:3103-3113 (2008).
- SLEEP CONSOLIDATION IS REPLAY OF THE SAME TEACHING NEURONS. Courtship LTM consolidation requires reactivation during post-learning sleep of the very DANs (DAN-aSP13) that were active during acquisition; learning induces sleep pressure through ventral fan-shaped body neurons that drive that reactivation. Dag, Lei, Le, Wong, Bushey & Keleman, eLife 8:e42786 (2019).
- A NOVELTY COMPARTMENT EXISTS. MBON-alpha'3 responds strongly to novel odors and is rapidly suppressed by repeated exposure; the suppression requires odor-evoked activity of the DAN innervating alpha'3, and the MBON drives an alerting behaviour to novel odors. Hattori, Aso, Swartz, Rubin, Abbott & Axel, Cell 169:956-969 (2017). This is an unsupervised, self-generated teaching signal with no external reinforcement.
- MBON POPULATION ACTIVITY BIASES ACTION. Individual MBON types carry innate valence and their summed, oppositely-signed activity steers approach vs avoidance; learning skews the balance. Aso, Sitaraman, Ichinose et al., eLife 3:e04580 (2014); Owald, Felsenberg, Talbot et al., Neuron 86:417-427 (2015).
- NORMATIVE ACCOUNT OF THE EXPANSION. The dimension of a random expansion layer is maximized at an INTERMEDIATE in-degree K that depends on the number of inputs N, the coding level f, and the weight distribution; the observed KC and cerebellar granule-cell in-degrees sit near their respective optima. Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott, Neuron 93:1153-1164 (2017).
- NORMATIVE ACCOUNT OF THE COMPARTMENT. A single MB compartment can be derived as an online linear-discriminant-analysis (LDA) classifier predicting the presence of an unconditioned stimulus from the KC code, under the biological constraints that DAN-induced plasticity only WEAKENS the synapse and that DAN activity is temporally sparse. Lipshutz, Kashalikar, Farashahi & Chklovskii, PLOS Comput Biol 19(2):e1010864 (2023).
- DAN SIGNALS ARE HETEROGENEOUS, NOT A GLOBAL SCALAR ERROR. DANs carry reward, punishment, locomotion, novelty, expectation and internal-state signals; models in which each DAN encodes a compartment-local error, with a single shared plasticity rule, account for this diversity and can solve several tasks simultaneously. Jiang & Litwin-Kumar, PLOS Comput Biol 17(5):e1009205 (2021).
- THE LEARNING RULE ITSELF IS STILL AN OPEN QUESTION IN THE FIELD. Twenty-five years of MB models disagree about the rule; the recurring assumption that its core function is prediction-error reduction is explicitly not established. Webb, Learn Mem 31:a053824 (2024).
- THE FLY'S EXPANSION-PLUS-WTA MOTIF IS A LOCALITY-SENSITIVE HASH. Random sparse projection followed by winner-take-all produces similarity-preserving sparse binary tags. Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha, Science 358:793-796 (2017). The same motif learns sparse binary word embeddings from raw text: Liang, Ryali, Hoover, Grinberg, Navlakha, Zaki & Krotov, 'Can a Fruit Fly Learn Word Embeddings?', ICLR 2021, arXiv:2101.06887.
- SPARSE CODE + FROZEN NON-ACTIVE WEIGHTS MITIGATES CATASTROPHIC FORGETTING. Only synapses between odor-activated KCs and the output neuron change; the rest are frozen. Shen, Dasgupta & Navlakha, arXiv:2107.07617 (2021), published in Neural Computation 35(11):1797-1819 (2023).
- SPARSE DISTRIBUTED MEMORY IS THE RIGHT ML ABSTRACTION FOR THIS MOTIF. Transformer attention approximates Kanerva's sparse distributed memory under conditions satisfied by pretrained GPT-2 (Bricken & Pehlevan, NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2111.05498), and SDM with top-k addressing is a continual learner (Bricken et al., ICLR 2023). Key-value memory networks with biologically plausible gated-Hebbian writes are an explicit MB model: Tyulmankov, Fang, Vadaparty & Yang, NeurIPS 34 (2021).

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The MB compartment is a one-shot, write-gated, key-value associative memory over a FIXED sparse random key code, read by a near-linear unit. The key code is shared by all compartments; the compartments are the heads. | PN -> KC: fixed sparse expansion, in-degree ~6 of ~50 glomeruli, ~2,000 KCs, ~5% active after APL feedback (Caron 2013; Lin 2014; Litwin-Kumar 2017). KC -> MBON: cholinergic, excitatory, hundreds-to-thousands of plastic inputs per MBON (Barnstedt 2016; Li 2020). Read: m_c = [w_c^T k - b_c]_+ ; value V = sum_c s_c m_c with s_c the compartment's innate sign (Aso 2014b; Owald 2015). | read v_hat = M_{t-1} k_t with k_t a sparse normalized code. Do NOT give each head its own expansion (this repo's v0.2 did: 'two compartments each have their own expansion... in the fly all compartments read one shared KC population', LIMITATIONS.md). One shared sparse key stream, H cheap per-head value matrices. This makes H heads nearly free: the k^T k Gram structure is computed once. | established |
| The write rule is three-factor and PRESYNAPTIC x NEUROMODULATOR, not Hebbian: the postsynaptic MBON's own firing is essentially irrelevant to the weight change. | Hige et al. 2015 show depression is heterosynaptic and odor-specific; it requires KC activity in the compartment plus DAN activity, not MBON depolarization. Formally dw_i/dt = -eta * d(t) * k_i(t) * g(w_i) with g(w)=w (multiplicative) or g(w)=1 (additive); the paired-vs-unpaired asymmetry adds a restoration term. | The write gate must be a function of (key activity) x (teaching signal), never of the read value. Concretely M_t = M_{t-1} * (I - beta_t k_t k_t^T / //k_t//^2) + beta_t v_t k_t^T / //k_t//^2, i.e. the DeltaNet update (Schlag et al., ICML 2021; Yang et al., arXiv:2406.06484). The erase term IS the Dop1R1 depression branch, and the write term IS the Dop1R2/unpaired-potentiation branch. This is why Gated DeltaNet beat this repo's MB memory 0.998 vs 0.981 in Phase 1: the MB memory as built was a weaker special case of the same object (scalar surprise gate, no per-key delta correction), not a different object. | established |
| The write kernel is BIPHASIC IN TIME with opposite signs, keyed on the ORDER of the sensory code and the teaching signal. This is the single most distinctive mechanism in the circuit and it has no counterpart in DeltaNet / Gated DeltaNet / Titans. | Dop1R1-Gs-cAMP detects coincidence -> depression when KC precedes DAN; Dop1R2-Gq-Ca2+ detects order -> potentiation when DAN precedes KC. Reversing order reverses the sign of both plasticity and behaviour (Handler et al., Cell 178:60-75, 2019; Aso & Rubin 2016). Write as a signed kernel: Delta W = integral kappa(tau) * d(t) * k(t-tau) dtau, kappa(tau) < 0 for tau > 0 and > 0 for tau < 0, zero crossing near tau = 0, width of order seconds. | Give each memory head a short learned causal kernel over the key stream, initialised biphasic: M_t = alpha_h M_{t-1} + sum_{tau=0}^{L-1} kappa_{h,tau} * beta_t * (v_t - M_{t-1} k_{t-tau}) k_{t-tau}^T, L ~ 4-8. Implemented as one depthwise causal conv on the key sequence, it costs almost nothing and gives the memory a native notion of 'which key preceded this teaching signal', which a single-step delta rule cannot express. This is a concrete, novel, pre-registerable H1 for Phase 3. | established |
| Revision is NON-DESTRUCTIVE: the circuit never erases a trace, it writes an opposing trace in a parallel compartment and sums at the readout. Each compartment's weights are non-negative and its DAN can only depress. | Extinction: the original aversive trace and the new extinction trace co-exist and are integrated by a pair of MBONs (Felsenberg et al., Cell 175:709-722, 2018). Compartments carry fixed opposite valences (Aso 2014b). Lipshutz et al. 2023 derive the compartment as LDA precisely under the constraint that DAN plasticity only weakens. | Replace one signed fast-weight matrix by a pair of non-negative matrices with monotone writes: M+_t = M+_{t-1} (I - beta+_t k k^T) + restore+, likewise M-, read v_hat = (M+ - M-) k. Rebinding a key writes to the OTHER channel rather than overwriting. Predictions: spontaneous recovery of the old binding when the new one decays; better continual learning; better non-destructive knowledge editing. This is genuinely different from Gated DeltaNet's single gated matrix and is directly testable on MQAR-with-rebinding, which this repo already runs. | promising |
| The teaching signal for each head is produced by the OTHER heads' readouts. MBON -> DAN (and MBON -> MBON) closes a local error loop inside the layer, so each head learns the residual the others left. | Anatomy: MBON->DAN feedback (300-339 edges in MaleCNS, this repo), MBON->MBON lateral inhibition, KC->DAN reciprocal synapses (Cervantes-Sandoval 2017), larval recurrent architecture (Eschbach 2020). Model: d_c(t) = [rho_c r(t) + sum_{c'} F_{c c'} m_{c'}(t) + ...]_+ ; with F_{c c'} = -a_{c'} this becomes d_c = [r - sum_{c'} a_{c'} m_{c'}]_+, a rectified reinforcement prediction error (Bennett, Philippides & Nowotny, Nat Commun 12:2569, 2021; Springer & Nawrot, eNeuro 8(3), 2021). | Make the memory bank sequential/boosting rather than parallel: eps_t^{(0)} = v_target,t; for each head h in a fixed order, write value = eps_t^{(h-1)}, gate beta_h = sigma(a_h log //eps^{(h-1)}//^2 + b_h), then eps^{(h)} = eps^{(h-1)} - M_h k. This is a within-layer residual allocation across heads, driven by a LOCAL error, with no backprop needed for the write. It is the correct reading of 'dopamine = prediction error' and is different from the scalar global surprise gate this repo used in v0.2 (which lost to a learned gate by 2.6 seed-SD in Phase 1, H1b). | promising |
| Compartments are deliberately heterogeneous in decay time, sign, capacity and flexibility. The heterogeneity is architectural, not learned. | Aso & Rubin, eLife 5:e16135 (2016): 20 DAN types differ in training requirements, decay dynamics, storage capacity and flexibility to learn new associations; some compartments overwrite prior learning, others do not. NO co-transmission in a PAM subset adds an opposite-sign slow channel (Aso et al., eLife 8:e49257, 2019). KC classes gamma / alpha'beta' / alphabeta carry short / intermediate / long memory phases. | Fix a decay ladder per head (this repo already does tau in {4,16,64,256}) AND give each head its own write SIGN and its own gate parameters, and crucially its own CAPACITY: some heads with large beta that overwrite (fast, flexible), some with small beta and long tau that accumulate (slow, stable). Do not tie beta and tau. Ablate: uniform heads vs the ladder. | established |
| The sparsity mechanism is an adaptive divisive/subtractive threshold implemented by ONE graded inhibitory unit with LOCAL, not global, reach - between k-WTA and per-block normalization. | k = [Ax - theta - alpha * (1^T rho(k)) ]_+ solved as a fixed point; APL is non-spiking, GABAergic, one per hemisphere, contacts ~100% of KCs (Lin 2014; this repo measures APL coverage 1.0 / 0.9995), but its activity and effect are spatially graded (Amin et al., eLife 9:e56954, 2020), so a KC self-inhibits more than it inhibits others. | Prefer a learned adaptive threshold with a running-quantile target (soft top-k) over a hard global k-WTA, and apply it per block of the code rather than globally. This repo's H1a already showed hard k-WTA beats no-normalization (+0.067, +1.1 seed-SD) but a DENSE LEARNED projection matched the MB (+0.041, CI includes 0) - i.e. the value is in the normalization, not in the fixed random expansion. | established |
| Consolidation is gated by SPACING, not by repetition count, and it runs offline as replay of the acquisition teaching signal. | Spaced training (10 sessions x 15 min rests) gives CREB/protein-synthesis-dependent LTM; the same amount massed gives only ARM, and ARM and LTM are mutually exclusive (Tully et al., Cell 79:35-47, 1994; Isabel et al., Science 304:1024-1027, 2004). Post-learning sleep reactivates the SAME DANs used at acquisition and this reactivation is required for LTM (Dag et al., eLife 8:e42786, 2019). Sleep also blocks dopamine-mediated forgetting (Berry et al., Cell 161:1656-1667, 2015). | Two-timescale memory with an explicit consolidation operator: fast weights M (per context), slow low-rank weights S (persistent). A key is promoted from M to S only when it RECURS after a gap Delta in [Delta_min, Delta_max] (spacing gate), and an offline 'sleep' pass between documents replays the top-//Delta M// keys into S with the same gate values that wrote them. Forgetting in M is suppressed during that pass. This gives a principled, biologically-grounded test-time-training schedule that is not in Titans/Gated DeltaNet. | promising |
| Forgetting is a driven, state-dependent process, not a passive constant decay. | PPL1 MP1/MV1 DAN tonic activity sets the forgetting rate for both valences; it rises with locomotion and falls with rest (Berry et al., Neuron 74:530-542, 2012); Dop1R2/DAMB mediates reversible transient forgetting (Sabandal et al., Nature 591:426-430, 2021); sleep blocks it (Berry 2015). | Make the decay gate alpha_t an explicit function of a scalar 'activity/interference' signal (e.g. recent write volume or running entropy), not a constant: alpha_t = sigma(-c1 * activity_t + c0). Suppress it during the consolidation pass. Predicts better retention in low-interference stretches and faster clearing on topic switches. Ablate against the constant-alpha Gated DeltaNet gate. | promising |
| One compartment computes essentially ONE scalar discriminant; the MB's expressive power is the small number of parallel compartments, not the 2,000 KCs. | Lipshutz et al. 2023 derive the compartment as online LDA: one discriminant direction predicting US presence from the KC code. The MB's entire learned output is ~15 compartment signals (21-34 MBON types; 48-49 MBON cells per side in MaleCNS). An unconstrained perceptron reading N=2,000 inputs could store O(2N)=~4,000 random dichotomies; non-negativity, depression-only updates and bounded dynamic range reduce this by a large and unquantified factor. | Do not expect the MB to be a language-model BACKBONE - its learned output bandwidth is tens of scalars per event. Use it as the memory/control module of a model whose backbone is something else. Correspondingly, do not bottleneck an LM through 'anatomical ports': this repo measured routing through 313 ALPN in / 97 MBON out at -0.63 nat/token vs random ports, 6/6 seeds. | established |
| The circuit has an unsupervised teaching signal (novelty) that needs no external reinforcement, and it is computed by reading the memory itself. | MBON-alpha'3 responds to novel odors and is suppressed by repetition; suppression requires odor-driven activity of the alpha'3 DAN (Hattori et al., Cell 169:956-969, 2017). The DAN is driven by the KC code itself, so the compartment self-supervises: familiarity = read(M_fam, k). | Add a familiarity head M_fam written with v = 1 for every key seen: nov_t = 1 - clip(M_fam k_t). Use nov_t to (i) gate writes in the other heads, (ii) allocate extra compute (more layers / more inference steps) on novel keys, (iii) trigger a chunk boundary. This is a cheap O(1) surprise signal that does not require the loss. This repo's v0.2 already exposes a `nov` MBON but never used it as a control signal. | promising |
| Optimal in-degree and coding level are OUTPUTS of a formula in (input dimension, coding level, weight distribution), not universal constants. | Litwin-Kumar et al., Neuron 93:1153-1164 (2017): representation dimension D = (sum lambda_i)^2 / sum lambda_i^2 is maximized at an intermediate K; e.g. with homogeneous excitatory weights, N = 1,000 and f = 0.1 the optimum is K = 9. K_opt grows with N and with input correlation. | Recompute K_opt and f for YOUR input statistics rather than copying K=6, f=0.05. For an LM key of dimension d ~ 512-1024 with strongly correlated token embeddings, K_opt will be far larger than 6. Sweep K and f and report the measured dimension D of the code as a diagnostic - that is the transferable quantity. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Kenyon cells: ~2,000 per hemisphere (Aso 2014, eLife 04577); hemibrain count 1,927 = 701 gamma + 337 alpha'beta' + 889 alphabeta (Li 2020, eLife 62576); MaleCNS v1.0 measured in this repo: 2,019 (L) / 2,045 (R).
- Compartments: 15 (Aso 2014); 15-16 in the hemibrain connectome (Li 2020). This repo's MaleCNS extraction annotates 32 MBON 'compartment' labels per side - a labelling difference, not 32 functional compartments.
- MBONs: 21 types (Aso 2014); 34 types including 14 atypical (Li 2020); MaleCNS measured here: 48 (L) / 49 (R) MBON cells, neurotransmitter split acetylcholine 25 / GABA 10-11 / glutamate 13.
- DANs: 20 types (Aso 2014); ~158 DANs, PAM ~15 types with 3-26 cells each, PPL1 6 types x 1 cell (Li 2020). MaleCNS measured here: 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 per side.
- PN -> KC in-degree: 4-10 PNs per KC, mean ~6 claws, from ~50 glomeruli (Caron et al., Nature 497:113-117, 2013). MaleCNS measured here: 5.30 (L) / 5.23 (R) PN neurons, 5.16 / 5.07 PN TYPES, median 5; PN fan-out 82.6 / 83.2 KCs; 92.5-92.7% of KCs have PN input.
- Coding level: ~5-10% of KCs respond to a given odor (Turner et al., J Neurophysiol 99:734-746, 2008; Honegger et al., J Neurosci 31:11772-11785, 2011; Lin et al., Nat Neurosci 17:559-568, 2014). Value is protocol- and odor-concentration-dependent; 5% is a convention, not a measured constant.
- APL: exactly 1 GABAergic neuron per hemisphere, non-spiking/graded, contacting ~100% of KCs (measured here: 1.000 L, 0.9995 R). Its inhibition is spatially graded along the lobes (Amin et al., eLife 9:e56954, 2020).
- KC -> MBON connectivity measured here (MaleCNS, >=3-contact threshold): 17,931 (L) / 17,985 (R) edges; mean 373.6 / 367.0 KCs per MBON. The >=3-contact threshold removes weak connections by construction, so this understates true fan-in.
- MBON -> DAN feedback measured here: 339 (L) / 300 (R) edges carrying 2,861 / 2,605 synaptic contacts. DAN -> MBON: 486 / 478 edges. MBON -> MBON: 282 / 255 edges. DAN -> KC: 7,212 / 6,177 edges.
- Optimal expansion in-degree (Litwin-Kumar et al., Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017): with homogeneous excitatory weights, N = 1,000 inputs and coding level f = 0.1, K_opt = 9. K_opt depends on N, f and the weight distribution; the fly and cerebellum both sit near their respective optima.
- Perceptron upper bound for one MBON: with ~2,000 plastic KC inputs, an unconstrained perceptron stores O(2N) ~ 4,000 random dichotomies. Under non-negativity + depression-only + bounded range the achievable number is much smaller and, to my knowledge, has not been computed for the fly - treat as UNKNOWN, not as 4,000.
- Aversive conditioning protocol (classical): 60 s odor paired with 12 electric shock pulses (1.25 s each, 60-90 V) at ~5 s ISI. LTM requires 10 spaced sessions with 15-min rest intervals; 10 massed sessions give ARM only (Tully et al., Cell 79:35-47, 1994).
- Appetitive one-trial learning: a single 2-minute odor+sucrose pairing yields protein-synthesis- and CREB-dependent LTM lasting days (Krashes & Waddell, J Neurosci 28:3103-3113, 2008). Aversive LTM needs 5-10 sessions. Valence asymmetry in trials-to-LTM is therefore ~5-10x.
- Plasticity timing window: sign of the KC->MBON change flips with the ORDER of odor and dopamine, with the crossover near zero offset and lobes of order seconds (Handler et al., Cell 178:60-75, 2019). I could not retrieve the exact kernel width from the abstract - treat the numeric width as UNCERTAIN.
- Memory decay: compartment-specific, from minutes to days; the MBON-alpha1 -> PAM-alpha1 positive-feedback loop operates over a ~1 h post-training window (Ichinose et al., eLife 4:e10719, 2015, as recorded in this repo's LITERATURE.md).
- Forgetting rate is set by tonic PPL1 MP1/MV1 activity and is bidirectionally modulated by behavioural state - up with locomotion, down with rest/sleep (Berry et al., Neuron 74:530-542, 2012; Berry et al., Cell 161:1656-1667, 2015).
- Gkanias et al., eLife 11:e75611 (2022): a single dopaminergic plasticity rule of the form Delta w_i proportional to D * [k_i + w_i - w_rest] with w_rest = 1, over a 6-MBON / 6-DAN 'incentive circuit', reproduces 92 published intervention experiments with r = 0.76 (p = 2.2e-18) and correctly predicts the ABSENCE of blocking.
- This repository's own baselines, for calibration: Phase-1 H1 MQAR (T=64, 8 pairs) MB memory 0.9814 +/- 0.0029 vs compute-matched Gated DeltaNet 0.998 (-0.0164 = -5.6 seed-SD) and DeltaNet 1.0000 +/- 0.0000; real vs shuffled PN->KC wiring -0.0010 (-0.1 seed-SD, CI includes 0); error gate vs learned gate -0.0151 (-2.6 seed-SD, learned gate WINS); hard k-WTA vs none +0.0666 (+1.1 seed-SD, k-WTA wins); sparse expansion vs dense learned projection +0.0413 (CI includes 0, no advantage). Phase-2 AIF-H1: K=3 free-energy steps beat K=0 by 0.026 bits/char = 0.7 seed-SD, below the pre-registered 2-SD bar.
- Data-quality flag from this repo's MaleCNS extraction: DPM_nt is predicted as 'dopamine'. DPM is established as serotonergic and amnesiac-peptide-expressing (and gap-junction coupled to APL); APL is GABAergic with reported octopamine co-transmission. The neurotransmitter prediction for DPM in the mirror is therefore almost certainly wrong and should not be used to wire a model.
- KC -> KC edges measured here: 56,963 (L) / 60,418 (R). Their functional role is unknown (largely axo-axonal contacts in the lobes); no established MB model uses them. Do not treat them as a recurrent excitatory network without independent evidence.

### Dead ends

- The SPECIFIC PN->KC wiring carries no usable information beyond its degree statistics. This repo's H1d measured real vs degree-preserving shuffle at -0.0010 accuracy (-0.1 seed-SD, CI includes 0). This is the expected result: Caron et al. (Nature 497:113-117, 2013) established the convergence is apparently random, and the later structure found by Zheng et al. (Curr Biol 32:3334-3349, 2022) and Cheng et al. (Sci Adv 11:eadq9893, 2025) is a modest food-odor community, i.e. a statement about the fly's ecological input priors, not about a general-purpose computation. Do not re-run wiring-as-prior experiments.
- The FIXED RANDOM sparse expansion itself gave no advantage over a dense LEARNED projection in this repo (H1a: +0.0413, CI includes 0). Its measured value was in the normalization (k-WTA: +0.0666, +1.1 seed-SD). Conclusion: keep the competitive normalization, drop the fixed random matrix, learn the projection.
- A SCALAR SURPRISE GATE on the write is worse than a learned input-dependent gate (H1b: -0.0151, -2.6 seed-SD). Do not repackage this as 'dopamine = precision'. There is no fly evidence that dopamine encodes precision (this repo's own literature sweep concluded 'Dopamine as precision has not been tested in flies'; FitzGerald et al. 2015 show it is behaviourally near-indistinguishable from RPE accounts). The fly evidence supports dopamine as a compartment-specific, timing-sensitive, valence-carrying teaching gate.
- A GLOBAL SCALAR REWARD PREDICTION ERROR is the wrong model of DAN activity. DAN signals are heterogeneous (reward, punishment, locomotion, novelty, expectation, internal state) and cannot be reduced to one scalar (Jiang & Litwin-Kumar, PLOS Comput Biol 17:e1009205, 2021). Kamin blocking, the classic signature of a global RPE, is not reliably observed in flies, and Gkanias et al. (eLife 11:e75611, 2022) reproduce 92 experiments with a purely LOCAL rule that predicts no blocking. Webb (Learn Mem 31:a053824, 2024) states explicitly that prediction-error reduction is an assumption, not a finding.
- Copying K = 6 and f = 5% as literal hyperparameters is a category error. Litwin-Kumar et al. (Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017) make K_opt a function of input dimension, coding level and weight distribution (e.g. K_opt = 9 at N = 1,000, f = 0.1). Copy the objective (maximize representation dimension), not the constant.
- DEPRESSION-ONLY, NON-NEGATIVE weights are a biophysical constraint the fly compensates for with compartment PAIRS, not a computational advantage. Lipshutz et al. (PLOS Comput Biol 19:e1010864, 2023) show that under this constraint a compartment reduces to a one-dimensional LDA. An engineer with signed weights should adopt the PAIR abstraction only if non-destructive revision is the goal, and otherwise use signed writes.
- Dale's law / sign constraints on the whole network. This repo's own literature review already flags Cornford et al. (2021) on naive sign constraints degrading performance, and the Phase-1 whole-graph masked-RNN experiment was null against both a degree-preserving shuffle and Erdos-Renyi. There is no MB result that requires sign constraints to obtain the computation.
- SPIKING for its own sake. The circuit's key normalizer, APL, is NON-SPIKING and graded (Lin et al. 2014; Amin et al. 2020), and every quantitative MB model that reproduces behaviour (Gkanias 2022; Bennett 2021; Springer & Nawrot 2021; Jiang & Litwin-Kumar 2021) is a rate model. Spiking MB models exist (Rapp & Nawrot, PNAS 117:28412-28421, 2020) but demonstrate energy/latency properties, not accuracy advantages on anything a language model measures. Copy graded normalization; do not adopt spikes hoping for capability.
- Routing an LM's inputs and outputs through ANATOMICAL PORTS. Measured in this repo at -0.63 nat/token (median, 6/6 seeds) for ALPN-in / MBON-out versus random ports of the same count. The cause is readout capacity: the MB's whole learned output is on the order of 15-49 scalars per event.
- Treating the MB as a SEQUENCE model. There is no order, composition or recursion in the KC->MBON mapping. Published 'MB sequence learning' models (Arena et al., Neural Netw 67:37-53, 2015; Arena et al., Int J Neural Syst 26:1650035, 2016) impose order through external state variables, not through MB structure. The fly gets its temporal structure from the central complex and from eligibility traces, not from the MB code.
- Expecting the MB alone to yield a state-of-the-art LANGUAGE MODEL. Its total learned output bandwidth per event is tens of scalars. The defensible claim surface is the MEMORY and CONTROL module of a model whose backbone is something else.
- Using the MaleCNS neurotransmitter predictions to wire modulatory neurons. DPM is predicted 'dopamine' in this repo's extraction but is established as serotonergic/peptidergic; dopaminergic and octopaminergic neurons are wired as ordinary signed edges in the mirror, which is physiologically wrong. Modulators must be modelled as gates, as this repo's LIMITATIONS.md already states.

### Open questions

- What IS the learning rule? Webb (Learn Mem 31:a053824, 2024) surveys 25 years of MB models and concludes the rule implemented by neural activity is still open. Any design that claims to 'implement the fly rule' is overclaiming; claim instead that it implements a specific published rule (Gkanias 2022, Bennett 2021, Lipshutz 2023) and test them against each other.
- Is depression MULTIPLICATIVE (Delta w proportional to w, which maps exactly onto the DeltaNet erase term W(I - beta k k^T)) or ADDITIVE (Delta w proportional to k alone, which maps onto plain Hebbian decrement)? I could not resolve this from the accessible literature. It changes the ML translation fundamentally and is worth a dedicated modelling ablation: run both, they are one line apart.
- What is the exact shape and width, in seconds, of the biphasic timing kernel kappa(tau), and is it the same in every compartment? Handler et al. 2019 establish the sign flip and the two receptor pathways; I do not have the numeric kernel. For an ML model this fixes the convolution length L and the initialisation of kappa.
- Do adult DANs actually compute a prediction error, i.e. is d_c = [r - sum a_c' m_c']_+ correct? Bennett et al. 2021 POSTULATE connectivity not yet observed; some imaging studies report no RPE coding. Contested.
- Does Drosophila show Kamin blocking? Evidence is limited and contested; Gkanias et al. 2022 predict its absence and fit 92 experiments without it. This matters: if there is no blocking, the teaching signal is compartment-local and the ML translation is residual allocation across heads, not a global error.
- How many associations can a fly hold simultaneously? No established number exists. Aso & Rubin (eLife 5:e16135, 2016) report compartment-specific differences in storage capacity and in whether new learning overwrites old, but do not give a count. Do not cite a number.
- What do the ~57,000-60,000 KC->KC contacts do? They are the largest un-modelled structure in the MB. Plausibly axo-axonal or reciprocal-synapse artefacts of the contact threshold; if functional, they would make the sparse code recurrent, which no MB model assumes. A cheap, novel Phase-3 probe.
- Is the gamma / alpha'beta' / alphabeta KC-class timescale ladder (short / intermediate / long memory) a principled timescale hierarchy or a developmental contingency? This determines whether a decay ladder over heads should be geometric and fixed, or learned.
- Does APL's LOCAL inhibition (Amin 2020) buy anything over global k-WTA in an engineered model? Untested. Concretely: block-wise adaptive thresholds versus one global top-k on the same key code.
- Does the spacing-gated consolidation and sleep replay (Tully 1994; Isabel 2004; Dag 2019; Berry 2015) translate into a measurable win for test-time training in LMs? Untested. This is the highest-upside open item: it is a schedule, not an architecture, so it can be tested cheaply.
- Do any of the six mechanisms that go BEYOND Gated DeltaNet (biphasic write kernel; paired monotone opposite-sign channels; inter-head teaching graph; per-head sign/tau/capacity heterogeneity; novelty head as compute allocator; state-dependent active forgetting) help at language-model scale? All six are individually ablatable and none has been tested in an autoregressive LM, to my knowledge. This is the novelty surface for Phase 3.
- Can the LDA-derived compartment objective (Lipshutz 2023) be generalised from a scalar US prediction to a vector-valued next-token prediction, and does the resulting online algorithm differ from the delta rule? This is the one place where a genuinely NEW derivation is available rather than a re-badged existing method.

### Relevance to language models

"Direct relevance is narrow but real, and it is not where the previous two phases looked.\n\nWhat the MB is NOT: it is not a sequence model, not a backbone, and not a source of useful wiring. It has no representation of order, no composition, no recursion, and its total learned output is on the order of 15-49 scalars per event. Both prior phases of this repository tested the MB as a wiring prior and as a surprise-gated fast-weight layer and got nulls; those nulls are consistent with the biology, not in tension with it.\n\nWhat the MB IS, in ML terms: a bank of parallel, one-shot, error-corrected key-value memories over a shared sparse key code, with per-head sign, per-head timescale, a biphasic temporal write kernel, non-destructive revision by opposing channels, an unsupervised novelty channel, active state-dependent forgetting, and a spacing-gated consolidation path into slow weights. In the vector limit, the core write is exactly the delta rule - M_t = M_{t-1} + beta_t (v_t - M_{t-1} k_t) k_t^T / ||k_t||^2 - which is DeltaNet (Schlag et al., ICML 2021) and, with the compartment decay, Gated DeltaNet (Yang et al., ICLR 2025). This is why the Phase-1 MB memory lost 0.981 to 0.998: it was a weaker special case of its own baseline, not a rival to it.\n\nThat identity is the good news, because it tells you precisely where the remaining novelty is. Six mechanisms in the biology are NOT in Gated DeltaNet: (1) the signed biphasic write kernel over the last few steps (Handler 2019), (2) paired non-negative monotone channels read as a difference, so revision never erases (Felsenberg 2018), (3) a within-layer teaching graph in which heads write the residual the other heads left (MBON->DAN; Bennett 2021, Eschbach 2020), (4) fixed heterogeneity of sign, decay and capacity across heads (Aso & Rubin 2016), (5) a familiarity head whose read is a compute-allocation and write-gating signal (Hattori 2017), (6) activity-dependent forgetting with a spacing-gated, replay-based consolidation into slow weights (Tully 1994; Isabel 2004; Berry 2015; Dag 2019).\n\nDefensible claim regimes, with matched controls against a Gated-DeltaNet twin: in-context associative recall with REBINDING (mechanisms 1-3), continual learning and knowledge editing without catastrophic interference (2, 6), long-context retention under topic switching (4, 6), and adaptive compute allocation (5). A state-of-the-art claim in one of those named regimes is achievable and honest; a general LM state-of-the-art claim from this circuit is not."


## The Central Complex as a Vector Computer: Phasor Codes, Rotation Algebra, Amplitude-as-Precision, and Hebbian Anchoring — and How to Build a Sequence Model Out of Them

*Key: `central-complex`.*

### Summary

"The fly's central complex is a vector computer whose instruction set is now known well enough to copy. A value is a phasor: a single-harmonic sinusoid across a small ring of columns, phase = angle, amplitude = length (Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022). Heading lives in an EPG ring attractor; I verified from this repository's own MaleCNS v1.0 data that the recurrent kernel the connectome encodes is, to 98.7% of its non-DC power, a single cosine harmonic at 180 degrees, W(dphi) = -902 + 944 cos(dphi), with amplitude and DC matched so the kernel just touches zero. That is the canonical amplitude-free ring kernel, and it means the operator is rank 3, not 148-dimensional. Kutschireiter et al. 2023 prove such a ring is exactly a circular Kalman filter: amplitude is the von Mises precision, cue fusion is vector addition of natural parameters; Basnak et al. 2025 measured it, informative cues giving narrower, higher bumps and more weight.\n\nArithmetic happens by anatomy. PFN axons write the heading phasor into fan-shaped-body columns at fixed offsets, with amplitude multiplied by a scalar drive; hDelta sums them. Because every contribution carries the same compass phase, sum_k a_k exp(i(phi + delta_k)) = exp(i phi) sum_k a_k exp(i delta_k): one shared rotation transforms an arbitrary body-frame vector into world coordinates. FC2 holds a goal phasor, stabilised by global divisive normalization rather than winner-take-all, on a ~50x slower timescale. PFL3 takes heading copies at +/-67.5 degrees, adds the goal, rectifies, and its left-minus-right difference is a precision-weighted sin(goal - heading) — a parameter-free complex inner product. ER neurons anchor the ring to arbitrary sensory features by local inhibitory Hebbian depression, in minutes.\n\nI measured the offsets directly from MaleCNS instance labels, which carry both PB glomerulus and FB column: PFL2 is exactly 180 degrees on both sides, PFL3 splits 175.5 degrees, PFN types 45-81 degrees, referenced to the unshifted PFGs/PFR_a. The whole FB apparatus — 456 PFN, 189 hDelta, 274 FC, 50 PFL, 601 FB tangential — sits unextracted in data/ while Phase 1 and 2 used only the 148-neuron compass.\n\nBuild the rotation, not the rate network. Claim state tracking and length extrapolation, not perplexity. Reproduce Selective RoPE and DeltaProduct first, then ablate the four fly-specific constraints."

### Established (with sources)

- EXPLICIT PHASOR CODE. In the Drosophila central complex (CX), a 2-D vector is represented by a sinusoidal activity profile across a columnar neuron population: the sinusoid's PHASE is the vector's angle, its AMPLITUDE is the vector's length. This is stated as the paper's central claim, not an interpretation: 'Each two-dimensional vector is explicitly represented by a sinusoidal activity pattern across a distinct neuronal population, with the sinusoid's amplitude representing the vector's length and its phase representing the vector's angle' (Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022, Nature 601:92-97, doi:10.1038/s41586-021-04067-0).
- HEADING RING ATTRACTOR. EPG ('compass') neurons tiling the ellipsoid body carry a single localized bump that tracks azimuth using visual landmarks and, in darkness, self-motion (angular path integration), and persists when both are absent (Seelig & Jayaraman 2015, Nature 521:186-191, doi:10.1038/nature14446). The network is a ring attractor enforced by LOCAL EXCITATION + GLOBAL INHIBITION, demonstrated with two-photon imaging plus optogenetics in flying flies (Kim, Rouault, Druckmann & Jayaraman 2017, Science 356:849-853, doi:10.1126/science.aal4835).
- VELOCITY INTEGRATION BY ASYMMETRIC SHIFTED COPIES. PEN (P-EN) neurons are conjunctively tuned to heading and angular velocity; mirror-symmetric left/right subpopulations increase during ipsilateral turns and project back to EPG with opposite angular offsets, moving the bump (Turner-Evans et al. 2017, eLife 6:e23496, doi:10.7554/eLife.23496). EM reconstruction plus RNA profiling confirmed the circuit-level ring-attractor architecture: EPG ring, PEN asymmetric projections, Delta7 long-range inhibition (Turner-Evans et al. 2020, Neuron 108:145-163, doi:10.1016/j.neuron.2020.08.006).
- DELTA7 MAKES THE BUMP SINUSOIDAL. Delta7 (Δ7) are glutamatergic (inhibitory in fly) protocerebral-bridge interneurons whose activity is yoked to but ~180° offset from the EPG bump; the anatomical EPG→Δ7 input strength is sinusoidally modulated across the bridge, and Δ7 broad lateral inhibition reshapes the EPG bump into a sinusoidal profile for PFN and other columnar types (Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022, Nature 601:92; Hulse et al. 2021, eLife 10:e66039, doi:10.7554/eLife.66039).
- I VERIFIED THE DELTA7 KERNEL DIRECTLY IN THIS REPOSITORY'S MaleCNS v1.0 DATA. Fourier-decomposing the already-extracted offset-binned connection profiles in docs/CIRCUITS.md: the disynaptic effective kernel EPG→Δ7→EPG has 98.7% of its non-DC power in the FIRST spatial harmonic, phase exactly 180°, DC 902.1 and first-harmonic amplitude 943.8 (ratio 1.046). The monosynaptic EPG→Δ7 anatomical profile is 98.5% first harmonic at phase -177.4° with DC 10.0 and amplitude 10.8 (ratio 1.078). By contrast the direct EPG→EPG profile is only 51% first harmonic (narrow, local). CONCLUSION: the measured recurrent kernel is, to within ~5%, W(Δφ) = -902 + 944·cos(Δφ) — a pure cosine whose DC offset is tuned so the kernel just touches zero. That is exactly the canonical amplitude-free ring-attractor kernel, and it means the EPG recurrent operator is RANK 3 in the heading basis (rank-2 cos/sin plus rank-1 DC).
- BAYESIAN SEMANTICS OF THE RING. A ring attractor whose bump amplitude is free (not normalised) implements, exactly, a circular Kalman filter over a von Mises posterior: phase = posterior mean μ, amplitude = concentration/precision κ. Cue fusion is literal 2-D vector addition of natural parameters; amplitude grows on confirmatory evidence and shrinks on conflicting or poor evidence (Kutschireiter, Basnak, Wilson & Drugowitsch 2023, PNAS 120(9):e2210622120, doi:10.1073/pnas.2210622120; code github.com/DrugowitschLab/BayesianRingAttractor).
- AMPLITUDE-AS-PRECISION IS MEASURED, NOT JUST MODELLED. Increasing cue informativeness improves encoding accuracy and produces a NARROWER AND HIGHER bump; under cue conflict the more informative cue is weighted more; a familiar cue is weighted more and guides remapping of a less familiar one; all explained by an attractor model with plastic sensory synapses (Basnak, Kutschireiter, Okubo, Chen, Gorelik, Drugowitsch & Wilson, Nature Neuroscience 28:1729-1740, 2025, doi:10.1038/s41593-024-01823-z).
- GAIN FIELDS. PFN neurons are cosine-tuned to heading, and the AMPLITUDE of that heading response is multiplicatively scaled by the non-negative component of the fly's translational velocity along that cell's preferred body-centric direction. PFNd prefer forward-ipsilateral, PFNv backward-contralateral motion; velocity arrives via noduli/LNO1 (Lu, Behbahani, Hamburg, Westeinde, Dawson, Lyu, Maimon, Dickinson, Druckmann & Wilson 2022, Nature 601:98-104, doi:10.1038/s41586-021-04191-x; Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022, Nature 601:92).
- VECTOR ADDITION BY ANATOMICAL PHASE SHIFT. PFN axons from the left and right protocerebral bridge reach fan-shaped-body columns offset by roughly ±45° relative to the EPG heading phase, producing a pair of near-orthogonal basis vectors; hDeltaB neurons sum the four phase-shifted, amplitude-gated sinusoids to yield an allocentric travelling-direction bump that is correct for sideways and backward walking. The circuit 'rotates, scales, and adds four vectors' (Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022, Nature 601:92; Lu et al. 2022, Nature 601:98). hDelta neurons project ~180° across the FB (to a column offset by half the FB width), giving negation/subtraction (Hulse et al. 2021, eLife 10:e66039).
- GOAL REGISTER AND STEERING COMPARATOR. FC2 neurons in the fan-shaped body hold an allocentric GOAL as a bump; optogenetically setting the FC2 bump sets the fly's goal direction. PFL3 cells are conjunctively tuned to heading and goal; PFL3L and PFL3R extract copies of the heading vector shifted by ±67.5°, add the common goal vector, pass the sum through a nonlinearity, and the left-minus-right population difference gives a signed steering command proportional to sin(goal - heading). PFL2 read heading at a 180° shift and encode unsigned distance-from-goal, setting turn gain (Mussells Pires, Zhang, Parache, Abbott & Maimon 2024, Nature 626:808-818, doi:10.1038/s41586-023-07006-3; Westeinde, Kellogg, Dawson, Lu, Hamburg, Midler, Druckmann & Wilson 2024, Nature 626:819-826, doi:10.1038/s41586-024-07039-2, with a 2024 author correction affecting only a PFL2 driver genotype).
- THE MEASURED PFL3 TUNING SET. The PFL3L population's preferred directions are (67.5, 22.5, -22.5, -22.5, -67.5, -112.5, -112.5, -157.5, 157.5, 157.5, 112.5, 67.5)° and PFL3R's are the mirror image (-67.5, -112.5, -157.5, -157.5, 157.5, 112.5, 112.5, 67.5, 22.5, 22.5, -22.5, -67.5)° — i.e. two 12-cell populations sampling the circle at 45° spacing with a ±67.5° global shift (reported for Westeinde et al. 2024; retrieved via search, not from the primary PDF, so treat the exact ordering as uncertain).
- HEADING-GOAL COMPARISON DRIVES BEHAVIOUR. Chemogenetically rotating the neural heading estimate makes flies slow down and turn so as to return the estimate to its pre-stimulation angle: the fly compares an internal heading with an internal goal and uses the SIGN AND MAGNITUDE of the difference to set turn direction, turn strength and speed (Green, Vijayan, Mussells Pires, Adachi & Maimon 2019, Nature Neuroscience 22:1460-1468, doi:10.1038/s41593-019-0444-x).
- HEBBIAN ANCHORING OF THE RING TO ARBITRARY VISUAL INPUT. Each EPG compass neuron is inhibited by visual cues at particular azimuths via R/ER ring neurons that form an ALL-TO-ALL matrix of potential connections onto the compass; sensorimotor experience remaps which visual position inhibits which compass neuron (Fisher, Lu, D'Alessandro & Wilson 2019, Nature 576:121-125, doi:10.1038/s41586-019-1772-4). Correlated activity of compass and visual neurons drives plasticity that flexibly transforms arbitrary 2-D visual scenes into a stable heading representation (Kim, Hermundstad, Romani, Abbott & Jayaraman 2019, Nature 576:126-131, doi:10.1038/s41586-019-1767-1; model code at github.com/ModelDBRepository/261585, MATLAB, with rules 'SOM inhib, post-synaptically gated' and 'Hebb inhib, pre-synaptically gated', learning rate epsilon_W_input = 0.5 (0.75 for 2-D input) and weight bound W_max = 0.33).
- NONLINEAR VECTOR OPERATIONS NEED SPECIAL MACHINERY. PFNa cells signal vectors in the positive direction with ordinary sodium spikes and in the negative direction with hyperpolarization-elicited CALCIUM spikes (T-type channel Ca-alpha1T), implementing a ~180° realignment relative to the compass — vector INVERSION is a distinct, nonlinear, cell-biological mechanism, not a linear sum (Ishida, Sethi, Mohren, Haraguchi, Abbott & Maimon 2025, Cell, doi:10.1016/j.cell.2025.11.xxx via S0092-8674(25)01375-3; code github.com/MaimonLab/cx-vector-inversion).
- A HANDFUL OF NEURONS SUFFICES. Networks with as few as ~4-8 units can maintain and velocity-update a genuinely CONTINUOUS internal representation of a circular variable provided connectivity is cosine-shaped and parameters are tuned; the cost is sensitivity to noise and tuning variation (Noorman, Hulse, Jayaraman, Romani & Hermundstad 2024, Nature Neuroscience 27:2207-2217, doi:10.1038/s41593-024-01766-5; code github.com/HermundstadLab/DiscreteRingAttractor).
- WHY SINUSOIDS. Mathematical analysis of the 8-column ring shows many activity profiles can encode heading, but the sinusoidal profile is the MOST RESILIENT to variation in neuronal activity, and that resilience holds only for a particular connectivity pattern — which both locusts and fruit flies exhibit (Vilimelis Aceituno, Dall'Osto & Pisokas 2024, eLife 13:e91533, doi:10.7554/eLife.91533).
- THE STEERING CIRCUIT IS A FAMILY, NOT A SPECIFIC WIRING. Five general rules generate a whole class of functionally identical steering circuits from compass set C, goal set G and steering sets S_L, S_R; because neural rates are non-negative a positive basis needs a third vector to span the plane. Insect species may differ in wiring while remaining functionally identical (Mitchell et al., 'A framework for constructing insect steering circuits', PLOS Computational Biology 2025, doi:10.1371/journal.pcbi.1014009; code refmitchell/framework-for-steering-circuits-software, Zenodo 14713161).
- GOAL SELECTION IS NORMALIZATION, NOT WINNER-TAKE-ALL. Connectome analysis indicates FC2 inhibition is almost entirely GLOBAL (four FB5A cells inhibit every FC2 roughly equally, with a smaller distance-dependent hDelta contribution and negligible direct FC2-FC2 inhibition); divisive normalization by total population activity favours a single clean bump without forcing it (Nanni & Lee, arXiv:2607.18969, 2026 — preprint, retrieved via search, UNCERTAIN).
- THE MALE CNS CONNECTOME IN THIS REPOSITORY ALREADY CONTAINS THE WHOLE FB VECTOR COMPUTER, UNEXTRACTED. Counting data/malecns/body-annotations-male-cns-v1.0-minconf-0.5.feather by cell type: PFN 456 neurons / 10 types; hDelta 189 / 13; vDelta 430 / 15; FC1-3 274 / 13; FS 308 / 14; FR 36 / 2; PFL 50 / 3 (PFL1, PFL2, PFL3); PFR 44 / 2; PFGs 18 / 1; FB tangential (FB1-9) 601 / 166; ER 282 / 26; ExR 26 / 8; EL 18; LNO 8 / 3; LAL 665 / 204; DNa 52 / 25. docs/CIRCUITS.md shows only EPG/PEN/PEG/Delta7/EL/ExR (148 neurons) were ever extracted; LIMITATIONS.md states 'the goal-comparison circuit (PFL3, FC2, hDelta) is not modelled at all'.
- PHASE OFFSETS ARE DIRECTLY READABLE FROM MaleCNS INSTANCE LABELS — I MEASURED THEM. Instance strings encode BOTH the protocerebral-bridge glomerulus and the fan-shaped-body column, e.g. 'PFNd(PB04)_L2_C1', 'PFL3(PB12c)_L4_C6', 'FC2B_C3_L'. Fitting col = a_L + glom on the left and col = a_R - glom on the right (excluding neurons labelled _irreg) gives a_L/a_R of: PFGs 0.00/10.00, PFR_a 0.00/10.00, PFNd -1.00/10.00, PFNa -0.90/10.89, PFNv -0.80/10.80, PFNm_a -0.89/10.92, PFNm_b -0.83/10.79, PFL1 +1.17/8.83, PFL2 +4.00/6.00, PFL3 +1.90/8.00. Taking the unshifted types (PFGs, PFR_a) as reference and 45° per column, the LEFT-versus-RIGHT phase split is: PFNd 45° (±22.5°/side), PFNv 72°, PFNa 80°, PFNm_a 81° (all ≈ ±36-40°/side), PFL1 105°, PFL3 175.5° (±88°/side), PFL2 360° ≡ 0 mod 360, i.e. BOTH sides shifted by exactly 180°. The exact 180° for PFL2 reproduces Hulse et al. 2021 exactly; PFN ±45° and PFL3 ±67.5° are reproduced in sign and rank order but quantised by the integer column labels (granularity 45°), so sub-column offsets require synapse-position-resolved FB columns.
- EPG INPUT TO COLUMNAR CELLS IS GLOMERULUS-MATCHED. Joining the MaleCNS graph to the annotation table, each PFN/PFL neuron's dominant EPG input is the EPG in its OWN protocerebral-bridge glomerulus (typically 20-70 contacts from that glomerulus, <5 from neighbours). The phase shift therefore arises ENTIRELY from the PB-glomerulus-to-FB-column projection, not from mixed sampling of the heading bump. (Measured in this session; session scripts fb_phase.py and offsets.py., not kept; reproducible from the graph and the annotation table)
- GOAL MEMORY IS SLOW AND PERSISTENT. Flies learn a safe heading under thermal punishment within a few trials; a circuit model stores the goal as reinforcement-updated weights over heading with learning rate alphaW = 0.001 versus heading learning rate alphaH = 0.05 — a 50x separation of timescales between the compass and the goal register (Dan, Hulse, Kappagantula, Jayaraman & Hermundstad 2024, Neuron 112:2581-2599, doi:10.1016/j.neuron.2024.04.036; code github.com/HermundstadLab/flyVisualLearning).
- THE ML SIDE ALREADY HAS THE SAME ALGEBRA UNDER OTHER NAMES. (a) Fractional power encoding in Vector Function Architectures maps a continuous value to a vector of unit-modulus phasors whose inner product is a shift-invariant kernel, generalising random Fourier features (Frady, Kleyko, Kymn, Olshausen & Sommer, arXiv:2109.03429, 2021). (b) RoPE partitions the embedding into d/2 two-dimensional subspaces, each rotated by position times a frequency theta_i = 10000^(-2i/d) — i.e. RoPE IS a fractional-power phasor code with position playing the role of heading (Su, Lu, Pan, Murtadha, Wen & Liu, arXiv:2104.09864, 2021). (c) The Linear Recurrent Unit parameterises its diagonal recurrence as lambda_j = exp(-exp(nu_log) + i·exp(theta_log)) with normaliser gamma_j = (1-|lambda_j|^2)^(1/2) — a bank of damped rotations with amplitude normalisation, i.e. a bank of leaky ring attractors (Orvieto, Smith, Gu, Fernando, Gulcehre, Pascanu & De, ICML 2023, arXiv:2303.06349).
- STATE TRACKING IS WHERE ROTATIONS PROVABLY MATTER. SSMs (S4, Mamba and relatives) cannot express computation outside TC0 and provably cannot solve permutation composition (Merrill, Petty & Sabharwal, ICML 2024, arXiv:2404.08819). Finite-precision linear RNNs whose state-transition matrices have only POSITIVE eigenvalues cannot solve parity, and non-triangular matrices are needed to count modulo 3; allowing negative (and by extension complex/rotational) eigenvalues unlocks state tracking (Grazzi, Siems, Franke, Zela, Hutter & Pontil, arXiv:2411.12537, NeurIPS 2024 M3L workshop / ICLR 2025). DeltaProduct extends this with products of n_h generalised Householder transformations, improving state tracking, language modelling AND length extrapolation (Siems, Carstensen, Zela, Hutter, Pontil & Grazzi, arXiv:2502.10297, NeurIPS 2025).
- THE OBVIOUS ML MOVE IS ALREADY PARTLY TAKEN — CHECK BEFORE CLAIMING NOVELTY. 'Selective RoPE' is described as an input-dependent, learnable rotary embedding that generalises RoPE to arbitrary angles and composes with decay gates, implemented as a complex gated linear attention layer, with the slogan 'strong recall = rotation + decay' and gains on copying, state tracking and retrieval (Movahedi et al., arXiv:2511.17388, reported as ICLR 2026; retrieved via search only, UNCERTAIN). Contextual Position Encoding (CoPE) already makes position a context-dependent CUMULATIVE SUM OF GATES — structurally the PEN angular-velocity integrator — and solves selective copy, counting and flip-flop (Golovneva, Wang, Weston & Sukhbaatar, arXiv:2405.18719, 2024).
- VECTOR-SYMBOLIC MACHINERY FOR DECODING SUPERPOSED PHASORS EXISTS. Resonator networks factor products of codevectors by 'searching in superposition' (Frady, Kent, Olshausen & Sommer, Neural Computation 32(12):2311-2331, 2020; Kent, Frady, Sommer & Olshausen, Neural Computation 32(12):2332-2388, 2020). Residue hyperdimensional computing represents and operates on numbers over a large dynamic range with resources scaling only LOGARITHMICALLY in that range, with strong noise robustness (Kymn, Kleyko, Frady, Bybee, Kanerva, Sommer & Olshausen, Neural Computation 37(1):1-37, 2025, arXiv:2311.04872). Holographic Reduced Representations have already been used to replace self-attention: Hrrformer achieves O(TH log H) time and O(TH) space and converges in ~10x fewer epochs on long sequences (Alam, Raff, Oates & Holt, ICML 2023, PMLR 202:490-507, arXiv:2305.19534).

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1. Represent every latent that has a natural circular or relational structure as an explicit PHASOR: a single first-harmonic sinusoid over a small ring of units, or equivalently one complex number. Phase carries the value; amplitude carries the precision. | Lyu/Abbott/Maimon 2022 state it as the code: amplitude = vector length, phase = vector angle. Aceituno/Dall'Osto/Pisokas 2024 prove the sinusoid is the maximally noise-resilient profile among all profiles that encode the angle, and that resilience requires a specific connectivity that both flies and locusts have. Mathematically, the set of single-harmonic profiles {A cos(x - phi)} over the circle is exactly a 2-D real vector space, closed under addition, so 'add two bumps' = 'add two vectors'. | A model state of 2R real numbers (R complex registers), not a dense N-dim hidden vector. Each register is z_r = a_r exp(i theta_r). Read out with a linear map from (Re z, Im z, /z/). This is the LRU/S5/Mamba-2 diagonal-complex state, but with the crucial extra commitment that /z/ is a CALIBRATED PRECISION, not a free activation. | established |
| P2. Build the recurrent operator as RANK-2 (cosine/sine) PLUS RANK-1 (global divisive inhibition), never as a full N x N matrix. The fly's 'ring' is a low-rank operator dressed as a dense circuit. | I measured this in the repository's MaleCNS v1.0 data: the disynaptic EPG->Delta7->EPG kernel is 98.7% first spatial harmonic, phase exactly 180 deg, W(dphi) = -902 + 944 cos(dphi), with amplitude/DC = 1.046 so the kernel just touches zero. The monosynaptic EPG->Delta7 profile is 98.5% first harmonic with amplitude/DC = 1.078. Direct EPG->EPG is only 51% first harmonic, so the sinusoidalisation is done by Delta7, exactly as Lyu 2022 and Hulse 2021 claim. | Per step: project the ring's activity onto (cos, sin) to get a 2-vector, operate on the 2-vector, project back, then divide by the population sum. Cost O(N) per register per step, not O(N^2). In a transformer/linear-RNN this is one complex multiply plus a normaliser — i.e. essentially free. It also explains why the repo's H2 run failed: it learned gains on the full 148x148 contact matrix instead of using the rank-3 structure the matrix actually encodes. | established |
| P3. Let the amplitude float and interpret it as precision. Do NOT normalise the bump to unit height and then bolt on a separate scalar 'confidence' or 'gate' head. | Kutschireiter et al. 2023 PNAS: an amplitude-free ring attractor implements a circular Kalman filter on a von Mises posterior, phase = mu, amplitude = kappa; fusing a cue z with reliability kappa_z is literally (a,b) <- (a,b) + kappa_z (cos z, sin z). Certainty decays under diffusion as dkappa = -(kappa^2 - kappa)/(kappa_phi + kappa_v) dt (quadratic approximation); velocity shifts the mean by dmu = kappa_v/(kappa_phi + kappa_v) dy. Basnak et al. 2025 Nat Neurosci measured it: more informative cues give a narrower, higher bump and are weighted more under conflict. | Replace the sigmoid input gate of a gated linear RNN with additive accumulation of natural parameters: z_{t} = lambda z_{t-1} + kappa_t exp(i u_t), where kappa_t >= 0 is an inferred reliability (a softplus head) and u_t is the phase the token asserts. Gating then emerges rather than being learned: a low-kappa token barely moves the register; a high-kappa token dominates. Forgetting is the decay term /lambda/ < 1 and has the closed-form Bayesian meaning above, so it can be SET rather than tuned. | established |
| P4. Update the latent by EXACT ROTATION, not by simulating a rate network. The rotation is the computation; the rate network is only the fly's implementation of it. | PEN neurons carry heading copies shifted by ~+/-45 deg (opposite sign on the two sides) whose gain is angular velocity; their asymmetric return projection moves the bump (Turner-Evans 2017, 2020). Noorman et al. 2024 show a cosine-tuned network of as few as 4-8 units can velocity-update a continuous variable exactly, but only under precise tuning and with noise sensitivity. | theta_t = theta_{t-1} + d_t with d_t = f(x_t) a per-token angular increment, applied as a complex multiply z_t = exp(i d_t) * lambda * z_{t-1} + input. This is exact at any sequence length and is O(1). It is structurally CoPE's gated cumulative sum (Golovneva 2024) lifted into the complex plane, and it is the single change most likely to fix the repo's failed H2: one Euler step of a tanh rate network per token cannot rotate a bump, so the connectome ring lost to an unstructured RNN at every length tested. | established |
| P5. Do vector arithmetic by ANATOMICALLY FIXED PHASE OFFSETS plus plain summation. Offsets are structure (free, untrained); amplitudes are content (learned/driven). | PFN axons from left and right PB land in FB columns offset by ~+/-45 deg, creating an orthogonal basis pair; hDeltaB sums the four amplitude-gated, phase-shifted sinusoids to yield allocentric travelling direction (Lyu 2022; Lu 2022). hDelta project ~180 deg for negation (Hulse 2021). In MaleCNS v1.0 I measured the left-right splits relative to the unshifted PFGs/PFR_a reference: PFNd 45 deg, PFNv 72 deg, PFNa 80 deg, PFL1 105 deg, PFL3 175.5 deg, PFL2 exactly 360 deg = 0 mod 360 (both sides 180 deg). | A 'vector ALU' layer with a FIXED, non-learned offset table delta_k (e.g. {0, +45, -45, 90, 180} deg), where each channel k contributes a_k(x_t) exp(i delta_k) and the register sums them. Only the non-negative amplitudes a_k are learned functions of the input. This is a strong, cheap inductive bias: it makes the layer exactly linear in the phasor algebra while still being nonlinear in the input, and it explains why non-negative rates need at least three basis directions to span the plane (Mitchell et al. 2025 PLOS CB rule). | established |
| P6. Multiplication is done as a GAIN FIELD: a scalar drive multiplies a phasor's amplitude without touching its phase. That is the CX's only multiplication primitive. | PFN cells are cosine-tuned to heading and their amplitude is multiplied by the rectified projection of translational velocity onto the cell's preferred body axis: r_k(column) ~ [v . u_k]_+ * (1 + cos(column - (phi + delta_k))) (Lu et al. 2022 Nature 601:98; Lyu et al. 2022 Nature 601:92). Velocity arrives from noduli/LNO1, outside the compass circuit. | A bilinear layer y = g(x_t) * R(theta_t) * v(x_t) where g is a non-negative scalar gate, R is the rotation by the register phase and v is a value vector. This is exactly 'RoPE applied to values with a data-dependent angle and a scalar gate' — cheap, and it keeps the operator norm controlled because rotations are isometries and g is the only magnitude knob. | established |
| P7. The compass acts as a GLOBAL ROTATION OPERATOR shared across all registers. This is the single most transferable idea in the whole circuit. | Because every PFN writes at its own fixed offset delta_k with amplitude a_k, the FB sum is sum_k a_k exp(i(phi + delta_k)) = exp(i phi) * sum_k a_k exp(i delta_k). The compass phase phi factors out: the FB computes an egocentric-to-allocentric COORDINATE TRANSFORM of an arbitrary body-frame vector by one shared multiplication. Confirmed for airflow direction (PFNa basis, Currier/Matheson/Nagel 2020 eLife 9:e61510) and travelling direction (Lyu 2022, Lu 2022) with the same hardware. | One inferred latent phase per head rotates ALL of that head's value registers simultaneously (rank-1 phase sharing), instead of RoPE's per-channel geometric frequency ladder or Selective RoPE's per-channel learned angles. This is a genuinely different constraint: it forces the model to maintain a single interpretable 'frame' per head that everything is expressed relative to, and it makes cross-register comparisons frame-invariant by construction (differences of phases are unchanged by a global rotation). | established |
| P8. Keep a small bank of PERSISTENT, WRITABLE goal registers, held stable by GLOBAL DIVISIVE NORMALIZATION rather than winner-take-all, and updated on a much slower timescale than the compass. | FC2 neurons hold the goal as a bump; optogenetic activation of FC2 columns sets the goal (Mussells Pires et al. 2024 Nature 626:808). Inhibition onto FC2 appears almost entirely global (FB5A), so a single bump is FAVOURED by normalization, not forced by selection (Nanni & Lee arXiv:2607.18969, 2026, uncertain). Goal learning rate alphaW = 0.001 vs compass alphaH = 0.05 — a 50x timescale separation (Dan et al. 2024 Neuron). | A small register file (R ~ 4-16 complex slots) with (i) a write head that adds kappa exp(i u) to a selected slot, (ii) a global normaliser s = sum_r /z_r/ and z_r <- z_r / (1 + s/s0), and (iii) a decay per slot that is 1-2 orders of magnitude slower than the fast compass. Normalization rather than softmax-argmax means multiple goals can coexist under low drive and collapse to one under high drive — a smooth, differentiable 'commitment' knob with no discrete branch. | promising |
| P9. Read out with a PHASE COMPARATOR: two shifted copies, a rectifying nonlinearity, and a left-minus-right difference gives a signed error signal; a 180-deg-shifted copy gives an unsigned magnitude that sets the gain. | PFL3L and PFL3R take copies of heading shifted by +/-67.5 deg, add the shared goal vector, apply a nonlinearity, and their population difference approximates A_heading * A_goal * sin(goal - heading); PFL2 use a 180-deg shift and encode unsigned distance-from-goal, boosting turn gain (Mussells Pires 2024; Westeinde 2024). Behaviourally the fly uses the sign AND magnitude of the difference to set turn direction, strength and speed (Green et al. 2019). In MaleCNS I measured PFL2's offset as exactly 180 deg on both sides and PFL3's left-right split as 175.5 deg (i.e. ~ +/-88 deg at 45-deg column granularity; hemibrain reports +/-67.5 deg). | An output head that, for each register pair (state z_s, target z_g), emits e = Im(conj(z_s) z_g) = /z_s//z_g/ sin(theta_g - theta_s) and m = Re(conj(z_s) z_g) = /z_s//z_g/ cos(theta_g - theta_s). e is a signed, precision-weighted error; m is a match score. Note this is EXACTLY a complex inner product, i.e. an attention logit in FHRR form — so the CX's steering circuit is a two-term attention readout whose 'query' and 'key' are the same registers at different fixed shifts. That gives a principled, parameter-free alternative to a learned attention projection for the comparison step. | established |
| P10. BIND the circular latent to arbitrary high-dimensional input ONLINE with a local, associative, mostly INHIBITORY plasticity rule. The ring does not come pre-anchored; it anchors itself to whatever the world offers, in minutes. | ER (ring) neurons make an all-to-all matrix of potential INHIBITORY connections onto EPG; correlated compass/visual activity depresses the active pairs, so each compass neuron ends up inhibited by the visual features present at its own heading. Sensorimotor experience remaps this within a single experiment (Fisher, Lu, D'Alessandro & Wilson 2019 Nature 576:121). Kim et al. 2019 Nature 576:126 show this makes stable heading maps in arbitrary 2-D scenes, with a model whose rules are post- or pre-synaptically gated inhibitory Hebbian learning, epsilon = 0.5 and W_max = 0.33. Basnak et al. 2025 show the more informative / more familiar cue drives remapping of the less familiar one. | A TEST-TIME, gradient-free, local write: M <- M - eta * outer(relu(context_features), bump_profile), clipped at W_max, where the bump_profile is the current register's cosine tuning. This is an anti-Hebbian fast-weight matrix that learns, during a single context, which content features should PULL the phase to which angle. It is the same family as DeltaNet's outer-product write but (a) inhibitory/subtractive, (b) targeted at binding an input to a CIRCULAR latent rather than to a value, and (c) with the precision-weighted remapping rule (more reliable register remaps the less reliable cue, not vice versa). Nothing in the LM literature does exactly this, and it is the strongest candidate for a genuinely new mechanism. | promising |
| P11. Negation/inversion is EXPENSIVE and special-cased; the rest of the algebra is linear-in-phasor plus rectification. Do not assume a free minus sign. | Rates are non-negative, so a positive basis needs a third vector to span the plane (Mitchell et al. 2025). The fly needs a dedicated cell-biological mechanism — hyperpolarization-elicited T-type calcium spikes in PFNa — to represent negative vector components, implementing a ~180-deg realignment (Ishida et al. 2025 Cell). hDelta's 180-deg cross-FB projection is the structural version of the same operation (Hulse 2021). | If you constrain amplitudes to be non-negative (which is what makes the precision interpretation valid), you must supply an explicit 180-deg-shifted channel to get subtraction. Concretely: every register gets a paired anti-register at delta = 180 deg, and the model chooses between them by a gate. This doubles register count but keeps every amplitude interpretable as a precision. It also predicts an ablation: removing the anti-register should specifically break tasks requiring subtraction/return-vector computation. | established |
| P12. Small is enough, if the tuning is right. Eight columns and ~50-150 neurons run the fly's entire compass. | EPG 46, PEN_a 20, PEN_b 22, PEG 18, Delta7 42 in MaleCNS v1.0 (verified in this repository's docs/CIRCUITS.md against hemibrain counts). Noorman et al. 2024 prove 4-8 units can hold a continuous circular variable with cosine connectivity, at the cost of noise sensitivity. | Register banks should be TINY (2 real dims per register, tens of registers), which means the phasor state costs almost nothing next to a 64-128 dim per-head linear-attention state. The design should therefore be additive to a strong linear-attention backbone, not a replacement for it — which is also the only way to run a fair compute-matched comparison against Gated DeltaNet. | established |
| P13. Context selection is MULTIPLICATIVE GATING of register pathways by slow, state-carrying tangential inputs — not a separate controller module. | The FB has ~166 tangential cell types (601 neurons) in MaleCNS v1.0 spanning 9 layers, many dopaminergic or state-related, positioned to gate specific columnar pathways; Hulse et al. 2021 propose exactly this as the substrate for context-dependent action selection. LNO/noduli neurons supply the velocity gain to PFNs from outside the compass (Lu 2022). | A small set of slow scalar 'state' variables (running surprise, novelty, recency) that multiply whole register pathways on and off, rather than a learned router with its own parameters. Cheap, ablatable, and it gives a clean experimental handle: each gate is one scalar you can clamp. | promising |
| P14. Separate the timescales explicitly and by a large factor: fast compass (ms / per token), slow goal (minutes to weeks / per document). | Compass learning rate alphaH = 0.05 vs goal alphaW = 0.001 in the Dan et al. 2024 model (50x). Flies reportedly maintain a consistent navigational goal angle for days to weeks (bioRxiv 2025.12.09.693277, uncertain) and the head-direction system itself is reported stable for months (Nature 2025, uncertain). | Three register banks with decay half-lives spaced ~1 : 30 : 1000 tokens. The fast bank tracks within-sentence relational state, the middle bank document-level frame, the slow bank persistent task goal. This is a principled, biology-derived choice of the multi-timescale structure that architectures like RWKV/Mamba parameterise but do not constrain. | promising |
| P15. The SOTA-claimable regime is STATE TRACKING AND LENGTH EXTRAPOLATION, not perplexity. Phase codes are provably privileged there. | Mathematics, not biology: exact rotation on the unit circle is a faithful representation of the cyclic group Z_n; composing rotations is exact at unbounded length. Merrill/Petty/Sabharwal 2024 prove SSMs cannot do permutation composition; Grazzi et al. 2024 prove positive-eigenvalue linear RNNs cannot do parity; Siems et al. 2025 show Householder products (rotations/reflections) fix both AND improve length extrapolation. A bank of rings with coprime periods is a residue number system, which represents a range with resources logarithmic in that range (Kymn et al. 2025 Neural Computation 37(1):1-37). | Pre-register the claim on: (a) modular counting / cycle navigation and parity from the Chomsky-hierarchy suite (Deletang et al., ICLR 2023, arXiv:2207.02098 — task list from memory, verify); (b) permutation-group word problems (S5/A5); (c) flip-flop and selective copy; (d) MQAR with mid-context REBINDING (the ER->EPG remapping analogue); (e) length extrapolation 8-32x beyond training. Controls that must all be beaten at matched parameters AND matched wall-clock: Gated DeltaNet (arXiv:2412.06464), DeltaProduct (arXiv:2502.10297), Mamba-2 (arXiv:2405.21060), an LRU/complex-diagonal model (arXiv:2303.06349), Transformer+RoPE, and — critically — Selective RoPE (arXiv:2511.17388) if it can be reproduced, since it is the closest prior art. | promising |

### Numbers an engineer can use
