A 2024 Neuron study (PMID 38795709, Waddell lab; exact author order uncertain) found that hunger suppresses tonic firing of aversive PPL101 dopaminergic neurons, and that the animal compensates by enhancing their input: circulating adipokinetic hormone (AKH) acts through AKHR in four ventral-brain neurons, two of them octopaminergic, and octopamine acting via OAMB lowers the activation threshold of ascending SEZON01 neurons so shock and bitter inputs still drive PPL101. A state gate without a compensation term destroys the ability to learn in some states.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The modulatory control bus is low-rank and module-addressed, never a single global scalar. | 541 monoaminergic neurons (0.325 % of MaleCNS v1.0) in 96 cell types reach 20.8 % of the CNS. Within the mushroom body, each of ~15-21 compartments is served by 1-25 dopaminergic neurons that broadcast one shared signal to all Kenyon-cell->MBON synapses in that compartment. So the control field has rank of order 10^2 over a 1.7x10^5-dimensional network (~0.06 % control dimensions), and it is addressed by MODULE, not by neuron and not globally. | Replace the Phase-2 scalar arousal with a learned vector m_t in R^C, C = number of memory modules / heads (8-32). A tiny 'DAN net' (a few thousand parameters, negligible FLOPs) maps (module read-out, module error statistics, slow state) -> m_t; each component controls only its own module's write rate, decay rate and read weight. Cost is O(C) state; the pre-registered control is a single shared scalar gate at matched parameters. Nobody appears to have tested per-module vs global modulation at matched parameters in a sequence model. | promising |
| Put neuromodulation on plasticity and timescale, not on activation magnitude: in a normalized network a multiplicative gain is exactly zero-information. | Mathematics: RMSNorm(g*x) = RMSNorm(x) exactly for any g > 0 (verified numerically here to 3e-7 for g in {0.25, 1, 4}); LayerNorm is likewise scale-invariant. The identical g in [0.25, 4] applied instead to a decay gate a_t = exp(-g*r) moves the memory horizon 1/(1-a) from 800 tokens to 50 tokens (16x), and applied to logits moves output entropy by 5.4 nats on a 1000-way toy softmax. Biology agrees on the target: ~94 % of dopaminergic output inside the MB (7,212 vs 486 edges, left hemisphere) lands on Kenyon-cell axons - the presynaptic, key side of the outer product, i.e. it multiplies the WRITE. | Legal targets for a modulatory scalar: the write gate beta_t, the decay/forget gate alpha_t, the read mixing weights across modules, and the output temperature. Illegal target: any activation upstream of a norm, and any global multiplier of the loss (both are exactly cancelled). This single distinction retro-explains the Phase-2 result that 'arousal as a global multiplier of F was nearly inert' and it is cheap to pre-register: the decay-gate arm should dominate the gain arm by a wide margin. | established |
| The modulatory signal is produced by a small learned critic driven by the network's own read-out (MBON -> DAN), not by a fixed error formula. | MBON->DAN feedback exists at 339 edges / 2,861 contacts per hemisphere in MaleCNS v1.0. Eschbach et al. 2020 mapped the full DAN input connectome in larva and showed by simulation that MBON->DAN feedback motifs improve learning performance and flexibility; Bennett et al. 2021 show the loop implements reinforcement prediction error; Felsenberg et al. (2017 Nature 544:240-244; 2018 Cell 172(4):706-718 - citations from memory, verify) show memory extinction and revaluation require exactly this feedback. | beta_t^(c) = sigma(w_c . [e_t^(c), read_t^(c), h_t, s_t]) - a per-module gate that takes the module's OWN read-out as an input and is trained end to end. This reconciles the fly with Phase-1's H1b finding that 'a learned gate beats the error gate': the fly does not use a fixed gate either. The fly's contribution is not the formula, it is (i) one gate per module, (ii) the module's own output as an input to its gate, (iii) the gate being far smaller than the module it controls. | promising |
| The gate is signed and temporally asymmetric: the same modulator potentiates or depresses depending on sub-second order, through two receptors with opposite second messengers. | Handler et al. 2019 (Cell 178:60-75): shifting odour/reinforcement relative timing by <1 s flips the valence of the memory; DopR1 drives depression and DopR2/Galphaq drives potentiation, with DopR2 signalling strictly dependent on KC-before-DAN vs DAN-before-KC order. Sabandal et al. 2021 (Nature 591:426-430): a third receptor, DAMB, drives forgetting. One transmitter, three receptors, three signs. | Two concrete architectural variants to pre-register against Gated DeltaNet (Yang, Kautz & Hatamizadeh, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464), whose write gate is beta in (0,1): (a) a signed delta rule, Delta M = beta_t k_t (v_t - M^T k_t)^T with beta_t in (-1,1); (b) two separate gates from a shared modulatory channel with different nonlinearities and different time constants, one writing and one erasing, matching the DopR1/DopR2/DAMB split. Variant (b) is the closer analogue and is the better bet; (a) is the cheaper test. | promising |
| Memory modules have per-module timescales spanning several decades, and modulation shifts each one. | Aso & Rubin 2016: compartments differ in decay dynamics, capacity and flexibility. Behaviourally the phases run from seconds-minutes (STM) to hours (MTM/ARM) to days (protein-synthesis-dependent LTM after spaced training), i.e. 10^3-10^5 in time constant. Compartment-specific forgetting is dopaminergic (Sabandal et al. 2021). | Log-spaced base decay per module over >=10 octaves (tau in {2^2 ... 2^14}), not the 64x span (4/16/64/256) used in Phase 2, plus a per-module modulated multiplier bounded to ~[1/4, 4]. The numbers above show this is where a bounded scalar has the most leverage (16x horizon change from a 16x gain range). The control arm is a single shared decay. | promising |
| Internal state is a slow leaky integrator on a slower clock than the activations, and it is allowed to be bistable. | pCd neurons maintain a state for minutes after their trigger ends and behave as a leaky integrator whose leak rate constant is itself modulable (Jung et al. 2020, Neuron 105:322-333). Hunger acts over hours. Sleep need is a monotone accumulator read out by a genuinely BISTABLE switch: dFB neurons are either electrically active or silent depending on accumulated pressure (Pimentel et al. 2016, Nature 536:333-337). Anderson 2016 lists persistence and scalability as defining primitives. | A three-clock architecture: token clock (activations), chunk clock (fast weights, updated every token but read as a chunk), and a document/session clock for a slow state s_t updated every 64-256 tokens with a long time constant, which conditions the whole modulatory bus. s_t is the 'register/genre/task' variable. Closest ML precedent is Nested Learning / HOPE (Behrouz, Razaviyayn, Zhong & Mirrokni, NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2512.24695), which argues for a continuum of update frequencies - cite it, and make the three-clock-vs-two-clock comparison at matched compute the pre-registered test. The bistable variant (a hysteretic nonlinearity on s_t) has no ML precedent I could find and is a genuine open question. | promising |
| State changes the SIGN and the routing of the read-out, not only its magnitude. | Hunger bidirectionally modulates MBON responses to food odours (Tsao et al. 2018, eLife 7:e35264); aversive learning and appetitive motivation toggle alternate modes of the same MVP2 feed-forward inhibitory pathway to produce avoidance or approach (Perisse et al. 2016, Neuron 90:1086-1099); hunger acting via NPF disinhibits appetitive memory expression by silencing MP1 dopaminergic neurons (Krashes et al. 2009, Cell 139:416-427). Marder's stomatogastric result is the general form: the same wiring, many circuits. | The slow state should enter as a SIGNED read-mix over modules - a low-rank matrix R(s_t) in R^{C x C} or R^{C x d} conditioned on s_t, allowing the same stored content to be read with opposite sign in different contexts - rather than a [0,1] gate. This is FiLM (Perez et al., AAAI 2018, arXiv:1709.07871) or a hypernetwork (Ha, Dai & Le, ICLR 2017, arXiv:1609.09106) applied to the read path, with the difference that the conditioning variable is an internally computed slow state, not an external label. | promising |
| Modulatory sensitivity is itself a state variable: the state changes the receptor, not only the transmitter. | Hunger and thirst produce compartment-specific changes in Dop1R1 and Dop2R levels across all 15 MB compartments with no significant change in baseline dopamine release (PubMed 42284989, 2025; venue uncertain). Cohn et al. 2015 make the same point functionally: identical dopaminergic activity produces different effects in different compartments. | beta_t^(c) = sigma(a_c(s_t) * u_t + b_c(s_t)), where u_t is a SHARED error/surprise channel and (a_c, b_c) is a per-module affine reparameterization produced from the slow state. This costs 2C parameters per state dimension and decouples 'how loud is the teaching signal' from 'how much does module c care'. It is cheaper and more expressive than giving every module its own error channel, and it is the exact biological mechanism. | promising |
| Offline consolidation is gated by a homeostatic pressure variable, and the replay set is SELECTED by the modulatory tag applied at write time. | Sleep pressure accumulates from waking plasticity and is read out by the bistable dFB switch (Donlea et al. 2014, Neuron 81:860-872; Pimentel et al. 2016). Consolidation during post-learning sleep reactivates the same dopaminergic neurons (DAN-aSP13 -> DopR1 on gamma KCs) that performed acquisition (Dag et al. 2019, eLife 8:e42786). The ML precedent for the phase itself is Tadros, Krishnan, Ramyaa & Bazhenov 2022 (Nature Communications 13:7742). | Directly fixes the failure documented in docs/phase3/CONSOLIDATION_TOY.md ('consolidation needs selection of what to replay'). Rule: (1) accumulate pressure P_t = sum over writes of //Delta M_t// (the fly integrates experience-driven plasticity, not time); (2) when P_t crosses a threshold, run a consolidation pass; (3) sample addresses for replay with probability proportional to the gate value beta stored with each write (optionally times a novelty term), NOT uniformly; (4) reset P. The pre-registered controls are uniform replay, recency replay, and no consolidation at matched total capacity and matched total FLOPs. | promising |
| A modulator can control the temporal binding window (eligibility-trace length) as a first-class quantity, independent of gain and of decay. | Zeng et al. 2023 (Neuron 111(7):1118-1135): suppressing or promoting 5-HT signalling respectively shortens or prolongs the coincidence time window for odour-shock learning and KC plasticity, through a local KC(ACh) -> DPM(nAChR) -> 5-HT -> KC(5-HT1a) loop. Note the loop is LOCAL and driven by the Kenyon cells themselves: the network sets its own binding window from its own activity. | Separate the eligibility trace from the memory decay. Keep a per-module trace z_t^(c) = lambda_c z_{t-1}^(c) + k_t, with lambda_c modulated, and write with the trace rather than the instantaneous key: Delta M = beta_t z_t (v_t - M^T z_t)^T. Modulating lambda (binding window) and alpha (retention) independently is a design degree of freedom current test-time-learning models (DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet, Titans arXiv:2501.00663, TTT arXiv:2407.04620) do not expose. This is the single most under-explored fly-derived knob I found. | speculative |
| State gating must be paired with homeostatic compensation, or the system simply becomes a tunable global learning rate that a wider baseline will beat. | Hunger suppresses tonic PPL101 dopaminergic firing, and the animal compensates by AKH/octopamine-dependent enhancement of the ascending input to those same neurons so that reinforcement is preserved (Neuron 2024, PMID 38795709). Net reinforcement capacity is held roughly constant across states; what changes is which inputs reach it. | Normalize the expected write magnitude per module across states (e.g. divide beta^(c) by its running mean per module, with the running mean excluded from the gradient). Without this, a state-dependent write gate is confounded with a global learning-rate schedule and any positive result is attributable to the schedule - which is exactly the failure mode that made Phase-2's arousal ladder losable to a wider control. | promising |
| A slow exogenous clock gates plasticity on top of the endogenous error-driven control. | Appetitive olfactory short-term memory requires the clock neuropeptide PDF and its receptor PDFR, with a stronger requirement in the morning than in the evening, and the clock circuit stabilises appetitive memory across the day (Flyer-Adams et al. 2020, J Neurosci 40(47):9066-9077). Separately, ~50 neuropeptide genes and ~50 peptide GPCRs (Nassel & Zandawala 2019, Prog Neurobiol 179:101607) form a second, non-synaptic addressing layer. | Weakest transfer of the set, and I would park it: a periodic, input-independent modulation of the write gate is a schedule, and schedules are already well explored in optimization. The defensible residue is the addressing idea: a modulatory channel need not follow the synaptic graph, so the modulatory bus should be allowed to address any subset of modules with its own learned (dense, low-rank) connectivity rather than reusing the model's routing. | speculative |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- MaleCNS v1.0 as shipped in this repo (data/malecns; 166,700 neurons, 10,520,431 edges, 101,422,396 contacts): predicted transmitter counts are acetylcholine 103,720 (62.22 %), glutamate 29,302 (17.58 %), GABA 22,069 (13.24 %), histamine 7,891 (4.73 %), unclear 2,999 (1.80 %), dopamine 392 (0.235 %), unknown 178 (0.107 %), octopamine 101 (0.061 %), serotonin 48 (0.029 %). Computed in this session with flybrain.connectome.io.load_connectome().
- Monoaminergic totals: 541 neurons = 0.325 % of the CNS, in 96 distinct cell types (dopamine 44 types, octopamine 38, serotonin 14). They form 108,378 edges (1.03 % of all edges) onto 34,700 distinct postsynaptic neurons = 20.8 % of the CNS. Per class: dopamine 392 neurons -> 16,934 targets (10.2 % of the brain); octopamine 101 -> 23,281 (14.0 %); serotonin 48 -> 6,640 (4.0 %).
- Fan-out, distinct postsynaptic partners per neuron (MaleCNS v1.0): octopamine mean 434 (median 142, max 2,950); serotonin mean 216 (median 75, max 1,341); dopamine mean 138 (median 53, max 2,176); brain-wide mean 63 (median 45). Octopamine is 6.9x the brain-wide mean fan-out, serotonin 3.4x, dopamine 2.2x.
- Convergence: of the 34,700 modulated neurons, the mean number of DISTINCT monoaminergic cell types converging on one neuron is 2.17 (median 1, max 30); 10,023 neurons (6.0 % of the CNS) receive >= 3 distinct modulatory types. The local modulatory code is therefore 1-3 dimensional for most neurons.
- Dopamine budget: 316 of 392 predicted dopaminergic neurons are PAM cluster and 22 are PPL cluster, i.e. ~86 % of the CNS dopaminergic population innervates the mushroom body. Largest PAM types: PAM08 (50 neurons), PAM01 (44), PAM04 (32), PAM06 (28), PAM12 (22). Independent literature estimate for the whole adult brain is ~280 dopaminergic neurons, with PPL1 ~12 and PAM ~100 per hemisphere - consistent in structure with the 392 CNS figure once the VNC and both hemispheres are included.
- Central-brain-intrinsic modulatory neurons only (superclass cb_intrinsic): dopamine 384, octopamine 35, serotonin 22 = 441. 49 of the 101 octopaminergic neurons are VNC efferents (the classic DUM/VUM neurons to muscle) and 17 of the 48 serotonergic are VNC sensory, so the central modulatory system is smaller still.
- Mushroom body module sizes (repo extraction, left hemisphere): 2,019 Kenyon cells, 120 PNs (60 types), 48 MBONs (25 ACh / 10 GABA / 13 Glu), 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 = 166 DANs, 1 APL, 1 DPM. Literature canonical figures: ~2,000 KCs, 15 compartments, 21 MBON types, 20 DAN types (Aso et al. 2014). The repo's extraction carries 32 MBON compartment labels and 21 DAN compartment labels because MBON types span compartments (e.g. gamma1pedc>alpha/beta) - reconcile before using compartment count as a module count.
- DANs per compartment in the repo extraction (left): gamma4 25, gamma5 23, beta2 16, beta'2m 14, gamma3 11, beta'1m 9, beta'2a 9, beta'2p 9, beta'1ap 8, alpha1 8, beta1 7, gamma4<gamma1gamma2 7, beta1ped 4, and 1 each for alpha'2alpha2, alpha'3, gamma1, gamma1ped, gamma2alpha'1, gamma5beta'2a. So a compartment's modulatory 'channel' is 1-25 neurons broadcasting one shared signal.
- DAN -> KC structural coverage (repo extraction, left): 7,212 DAN->KC edges; each DAN contacts a mean of 43 Kenyon cells (median 26, max 908) = 2.2 % of the KC population; 1,774 of 2,019 KCs (87.9 %) receive at least one DAN contact. Per compartment the DAN population covers 5-28 % of KCs (gamma4 28.3 %, gamma4<gamma1gamma2 19.5 %, gamma5 16.8 %, beta1 13.7 %, beta'2m 12.5 %, beta2 8.0 %).
- Dopaminergic target ratio inside the MB: DAN->KC 7,212 edges vs DAN->MBON 486 edges (left; 6,177 vs 478 right) - ~94 % of the dopaminergic output inside the mushroom body lands presynaptically, on Kenyon-cell axons. MBON->DAN feedback: 339 edges / 2,861 contacts (left), 300 / 2,605 (right).
- CRITICAL DATA DEFECT in this repo: every outgoing edge from a monoaminergic neuron in data/malecns/graph.bin carries weight exactly 0 - dopamine 54,172 edges, octopamine 43,826, serotonin 10,380, plus histamine 49,181 and 'unclear' 174,757; 332,316 zero-weight edges = 3.16 % of the graph. The manifest states the mirror's convention: 'unresolved/modulatory source current zero'. Consequence: in data/circuits/mushroom_body_{L,R}.npz the dense arrays W_dan_kc and W_dan_mbon are identically zero (KC partners per DAN computes as 0), so the dopaminergic wiring is unusable as exported. The structural adjacency survives in the sparse CSR as explicit zeros (7,212 DAN->KC, 486 DAN->MBON), and the real contact counts are obtainable from the official Janelia file already listed in data/malecns/manifest.json (connectome-weights-male-cns-v1.0-minconf-0.5.feather, sha256 e35da783d1c686b2b58b3b87cd6a403ae43bfcfba8bff28e08ef752c1a56afc1).
- Mathematics of where a modulatory scalar can act, computed here: for g in {0.25, 1, 4}, max|RMSNorm(g*x) - RMSNorm(x)| = 2.8e-7 / 0 / 1.8e-8 over a 512-dim vector - i.e. exactly inert. The same g applied to a decay gate a = exp(-g*r) moves the memory horizon 1/(1-a) from 800.5 to 50.5 tokens (r = 0.005) or 200.5 to 13.0 (r = 0.02), a 16x range. Applied to logits it moves entropy on a 1,000-way softmax from 6.79 to 1.42 nats (ceiling 6.91).
- Timing constants from biology: dopamine/odour relative timing shifts of < 1 s flip the sign of the memory (Handler et al. 2019). Behavioural memory phases span minutes (STM) to hours (ARM/MTM) to days-week (LTM after 10x spaced training with 15-min rest intervals), i.e. ~10^3-10^5 in time constant. Persistent social-arousal states last minutes after the trigger ends (Jung et al. 2020). Hunger/thirst states are set over hours (standard protocols use ~16-24 h starvation). Circadian gating operates over 24 h.
- Gain magnitudes: peak-to-peak responses of vertical-system visual interneurons roughly double (~2x) during flight versus rest (Maimon, Straw & Dickinson 2010, Nature Neuroscience 13:393-399); octopamine application reproduces this (Suver et al. 2012, Current Biology 22:2294-2302). No upward shift of the peak temporal frequency was found in VS cells, though HS/H1 responses rise at many temporal frequencies - so the effect is closer to a pure gain than to a timescale change, which is the opposite of what dopamine does in the mushroom body.
- Chemical addressing bandwidth: ~50 neuropeptide precursor genes and ~50 peptide GPCRs in Drosophila (Nassel & Zandawala 2019, Prog Neurobiol 179:101607; 51 curated neuropeptide genes in FlyBase FB2024_02). Combined with 96 monoaminergic cell types, the total 'modulatory channel count' is of order 100-150 over 166,700 neurons.

### Dead ends

- A single global scalar multiplying activations is mathematically inert in a normalized network. RMSNorm and LayerNorm are exactly scale-invariant (RMSNorm(g*x) = RMSNorm(x); verified here to 3e-7 for g in [0.25, 4]). Any 'arousal gain' placed upstream of a norm inside a block contributes exactly nothing. Phase 2 found this empirically ('arousal as a global multiplier of F was nearly inert'); it is a theorem, not a tuning problem. Do not retry it in any form.
- A single global scalar multiplying the objective is inert under a scale-invariant update. Adam and any preconditioned/Newton step divide out a constant factor on the loss (Phase 2's own diagnosis: 'the preconditioned step divides by gamma*P + 1, so gamma cancels for P >> 1, and the adaptive-K test is scale-invariant'). A 'free-energy temperature' is not a modulator.
- A fixed functional form for the write gate loses to a learned gate with the same inputs, and the biology does not ask for a fixed form. Phase-1 H1b already showed this (learned gate > error gate). The fly's write gate is the output of a recurrent network of ~20 DAN types that receives MBON feedback (Eschbach et al. 2020; Bennett et al. 2021) - a small LEARNED critic. Any phase-3 design that re-inserts a closed-form sigmoid(a*log(pi*||eps||^2/n) + b) as the default is re-running a known negative.
- 'Dopamine = precision' (Friston et al. 2012, PLoS Comput Biol 8:e1002327) does not survive contact with the fly data and should not be used as the organizing metaphor. Fly dopamine is (i) compartment-addressed to ~15-21 parallel modules rather than global, (ii) signed by sub-second relative timing through two receptors with opposite second messengers (Handler et al. 2019), (iii) simultaneously an acute presynaptic gain and a slow plasticity signal (Cohn et al. 2015), and (iv) also the forgetting signal (Sabandal et al. 2021, Nature 591:426-430). Collapsing it to one non-negative precision scalar discards all four properties and yields precisely the inert design Phase 2 built.
- A synaptic connectome cannot deliver the neuromodulatory circuit, for two independent reasons. (1) Biology: monoamines and neuropeptides act substantially by extrasynaptic volume transmission, and ~50 peptide/GPCR pairs form a 'wireless' layer absent from the adjacency matrix (Bargmann 2012; Nassel & Zandawala 2019). (2) Engineering, specific to this repository: every monoaminergic edge in data/malecns/graph.bin has weight 0 (332,316 zero-weight edges in total including histamine and 'unclear'), and data/circuits/mushroom_body_*.npz export W_dan_kc and W_dan_mbon as all-zero dense arrays. Phase 1's whole-graph mask therefore tested a connectome with the modulatory system silently deleted; and any phase-3 code that loads W_dan_kc from the npz will get zeros. Fix by re-deriving the structural adjacency from the CSR (explicit zeros are retained: 7,212 DAN->KC edges) or by fetching the official Janelia weights feather named in the manifest.
- Naive replay-based consolidation does not work and the repo has already shown it (docs/phase3/CONSOLIDATION_TOY.md: 0.06 vs 0.02 recall beyond the fast horizon). The fly says the missing ingredient is SELECTION by modulatory tag: post-learning sleep reactivates specifically the dopaminergic neurons that were engaged at acquisition (Dag et al. 2019). 'Add sleep' is not a free ingredient; 'replay what the gate marked as important, with a budget set by accumulated write magnitude' is a different and testable proposal.
- State-dependent gating WITHOUT compensation degenerates into a global learning-rate schedule and will lose to a wider or better-tuned baseline. The fly explicitly compensates: hunger suppresses aversive PPL101 dopaminergic firing and the circuit restores reinforcement by AKH/octopamine-dependent enhancement of its input (Neuron 2024, PMID 38795709). Any phase-3 arm that gates writes by an internal state must hold the expected per-module write magnitude constant across states, or the result is uninterpretable.
- Octopaminergic global arousal is real in the fly (~2x visual gain during flight; Maimon et al. 2010; Suver et al. 2012) but it is the LEAST transferable modulatory channel, because its computational content is exactly a multiplicative gain on sensory responses - the one thing that is free in a normalized transformer and the one thing Phase 2 already tested and found inert. Do not build the phase-3 thesis on arousal. Build it on dopamine (per-module plasticity control) and on the sleep/consolidation homeostat.
- Do not use 'number of MB compartments' as a settled module count without reconciliation. The literature canon is 15 compartments / 21 MBON types / 20 DAN types (Aso et al. 2014, eLife 3:e04577); the repo's MaleCNS extraction yields 32 MBON compartment labels and 21 DAN compartment labels for 48-49 MBONs and 166 DANs per side. The discrepancy comes from MBON types that span compartments and from a different labelling scheme, not from new biology.

### Open questions

- Does a rank-C, per-module modulatory bus beat a single learned global gate at matched parameters and matched FLOPs in a sequence model? I found no paper that runs this comparison. It is cheap (C = 8-32, a few thousand extra parameters) and it is the most direct test of the central biological claim.
- Which target has the most leverage for a bounded modulatory scalar: the write gate, the decay gate, the eligibility-trace length, or the read mix? The arithmetic here says decay (16x horizon change from a 16x gain range) and the biology says write (94 % of DAN output is presynaptic). These disagree, and the disagreement is resolvable by a four-arm ablation.
- Is a signed write gate (beta in (-1,1)) better or worse than two separate gates (write, erase) driven from one shared channel with different time constants? Biology says two receptors with opposite second messengers plus a third for forgetting, which favours the two/three-gate form; ML convenience favours the signed form. Untested either way.
- Does modulated eligibility-trace length (the serotonergic 'coincidence window' knob, Zeng et al. 2023) buy anything in language modelling? Separating the trace time constant lambda from the memory decay alpha is a degree of freedom DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet, Titans and TTT do not expose. Genuinely novel and genuinely untested.
- Does dopaminergic-tag replay selection (replay probability proportional to the write-gate value stored with each write) beat uniform, recency and value-free replay at matched replay budget? This is the specific experiment that would turn docs/phase3/CONSOLIDATION_TOY.md from a null into a result.
- Does a bistable / hysteretic slow state beat a smooth leaky-integrator state? The fly's sleep homeostat is genuinely bistable (Pimentel et al. 2016) and the persistent-state literature emphasises hysteresis, but I know of no ML demonstration that discreteness in a slow conditioning variable helps. Speculative; test cheaply at CPU scale before spending GPU time.
- Is there a regime where the horizon of a memory module SHOULD shrink on surprise rather than grow? The fly forgets actively and dopaminergically (Sabandal et al. 2021), which is the opposite of the intuition that surprise should always mean 'write harder and hold longer'. A pre-registered arm with a forgetting-on-surprise gate would be cheap and would distinguish a real fly-derived rule from a rediscovered ML gate.
- What is the correct benchmark family? Every principle above is about continual adaptation, non-stationarity and retention beyond the context window; none of them predicts better perplexity per FLOP. Defining a regime where 'state-of-the-art' is claimable without pretending (e.g. re-binding under distribution shift, retention across document boundaries, adaptation speed after a topic switch, at matched compute against Gated DeltaNet / Titans / TTT twins) is still an open design problem and is the single highest-risk item.
- How much of the MaleCNS monoamine annotation is real? Transmitter identity is an EM-image prediction with ~85-90 % per-type accuracy for the rare classes (Eckstein, Bates et al. 2024, Cell 187:2574-2594), and the repo's data contain at least one surprising assignment (18 central-complex 'EL' neurons predicted octopaminergic). The neuron counts in this brief should be treated as +/- 10-15 % and cross-checked against FlyWire/hemibrain before any of them is published.
- Is the dopamine-as-critic loop (MBON -> DAN -> KC->MBON plasticity) distinguishable from a learned gate that happens to take the read-out as input? If not, the fly's contribution collapses to 'give the gate the module's own output', which is a one-line change and not a thesis. Worth deciding early, because it determines whether phase 3 has an architecture or a hyper-parameter.

### Relevance to language models

["Direct and narrow. Neuromodulation says almost nothing about how to mix tokens (the transformer's actual inductive bias) and almost everything about how a model should change itself while it runs. That maps onto exactly one part of a modern language model: the test-time-learning / fast-weight layer (DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet arXiv:2412.06464, Titans arXiv:2501.00663, TTT arXiv:2407.04620, LaCT, Nested Learning / HOPE arXiv:2512.24695). Every one of those models already has a scalar or vector write gate and a forget gate learned from the input; the fly's claim is not that gates are good - that is settled - but that the gate structure is wrong in five specific, cheap-to-fix ways. (1) The gate should be per-module, with as many independent channels as there are memory modules, because the fly spends 86 % of its dopamine on ~20 independently-gated compartments rather than on one global signal. (2) The gate should take the module's own read-out as an input, because the fly's teaching signal is manufactured by an MBON->DAN critic loop, not by an external error. (3) Write, erase and eligibility-window should be three gates driven from one shared modulatory channel through different nonlinearities and time constants, because the fly uses three dopamine receptors (DopR1 depression, DopR2 potentiation, DAMB forgetting) on one transmitter. (4) A slow state on a document-length clock should reparameterize each module's sensitivity to that shared channel (FiLM on the gate, not on the activations), because hunger and thirst change dopamine RECEPTOR levels per compartment without changing dopamine release. (5) Consolidation into slow weights should be triggered by accumulated write magnitude and should replay what the gate marked, because sleep pressure is an integrator of plasticity and sleep replay reactivates the specific dopaminergic neurons that did the encoding. Against this, the engineering fact that matters most is negative: in a pre-norm transformer a multiplicative gain is exactly cancelled by RMSNorm, so the whole 'global arousal' family is dead on arrival, while the identical scalar applied to a decay gate moves the memory horizon 16x. Neuromodulation transfers to language models as control of plasticity and timescale, and only as that."]


## Whole-brain connectome statistics and organisation of Drosophila: what the numbers say about computational style, and what honestly transfers to a sequence model

*Key: `connectome-statistics`.*

### Summary

The MaleCNS graph, measured directly from the copy in this repository, describes a machine that is shallow, extremely wide, overwhelmingly recurrent, regionally modular and governed by inhibitory gain control. Depth: the median path from a sensory neuron to a descending or motor neuron is 1-2 synapses; 99.7% of the 166,700 neurons lie within 3 hops of a sensor; even restricting to strong (>=10-contact) connections the maximum depth is 8. Recurrence: 97.07% of neurons form one strongly connected component holding 98.96% of edges; reciprocity is 19.15%, 506x chance; classifying edges by information-flow depth gives 22.7% forward, 60.2% lateral and 17.2% feedback. Control: 74% of the fifty highest-degree neurons are inhibitory (base rate 30.8%) and are named wide-field normalisers (CT1, Am1, LPi, Pm, APL); the top 1% of neurons by out-degree touch 84% of the brain in one hop. Balance: although only 30.8% of neurons are inhibitory, the median neuron takes 44.2% of its input contacts from inhibitory sources, because inhibitory cells have 1.33x the fan-out. Integration is graded and distributed: the strongest presynaptic partner supplies a median of 10.9% of input, and 9 partners are needed for half of it. Allocation is the reverse of ML intuition: 63% of the CNS is visual, while Kenyon cells are 2.44%, the central-complex ring 0.26% and MBONs 0.058%. On whether specific wiring matters beyond statistics, the answer is a measured yes with a sharp qualification. Fitting a degree-corrected SBM on one hemisphere and testing on the other, degrees alone buy 1.27-1.30 bits/edge over Erdos-Renyi and cell-type blocks buy a further 4.12-4.25 bits/edge, with ~80% generalising. But that information is type-level, not synapse-level: presence/absence of a type-level connection agrees between hemispheres of the same animal at Jaccard 0.50 (0.65 for the strongest connections), and 8% of one hemisphere's edges join a type pair absent on the other. The wiring diagram is a distribution with a reproducible strong skeleton, not a blueprint - which is exactly why a frozen adjacency mask was null and why a generated typed-population architecture has never been tested.

### Established (with sources)

- MaleCNS v1.0 (the copy in data/malecns, verified against the Janelia release) contains 166,700 neurons, 10,520,431 connections at the >=3-synaptic-contact threshold, and 101,422,396 synaptic contacts; graph density 0.038% (MEASURED HERE, session script stats1.py, not kept; cf. Berg et al., 'Sexual dimorphism in the complete connectome of the Drosophila male central nervous system', bioRxiv 2025.10.09.680999, Cell 2026, which reports >166,000 neurons and 11,710 neuron types)
- Predicted-neurotransmitter composition of MaleCNS by neuron: acetylcholine 62.22%, glutamate 17.58%, GABA 13.24%, histamine 4.73%, 'unclear' 1.80%, dopamine 0.24%, unknown 0.11%, octopamine 0.06%, serotonin 0.03%. Treating GABA+glutamate as inhibitory gives 30.82% inhibitory neurons (MEASURED HERE). Neurotransmitter identity is a prediction from EM images, not a measurement (Eckstein et al., Cell 187:2574-2594, 2024).
- THE FLY BRAIN IS NOT A FEEDFORWARD HIERARCHY. 97.07% of neurons (161,820/166,700) lie in ONE giant strongly connected component, which contains 98.96% of all edges and 98.23% of all synaptic contacts (MEASURED HERE, stats4.py). Edge-level reciprocity is 19.15% (2,014,596 of 10,520,431 edges are part of a bidirectional pair), 506x the Erdos-Renyi expectation (MEASURED HERE).
- THE FLY BRAIN IS EXTREMELY SHALLOW. Multi-source BFS from all 17,937 sensory neurons over all >=3-contact edges: 88.3% of the CNS is within 2 hops and 99.7% within 3 hops; descending neurons are at median 1 hop (mean 1.52), motor/efferent neurons at median 1 hop (mean 1.30); the deepest neuron is 5 hops out. Restricting to strong connections (>=10 contacts) the medians become 2 hops for both, with max depth 8 (MEASURED HERE, stats5.py).
- Under a probabilistic information-flow traversal (the Schlegel et al. 2021 / Dorkenwald et al. 2024 model: a neuron joins the traversed set with probability min(1, f/0.30) where f is the fraction of its input synapses already traversed), seeded from all sensory neurons and averaged over 20 runs, mean traversal layer is 2.35 for descending neurons, 2.27 for motor/efferent, 2.91 for central-brain intrinsic, 4.82 for visual projection neurons and 5.17 for optic-lobe intrinsic neurons; 99.9% of the CNS is reached (MEASURED HERE, stats5.py).
- MOST EDGES ARE LATERAL, NOT FORWARD. Classifying every edge by the information-flow layer difference between its endpoints: 22.68% forward (dl>+0.5), 60.16% lateral (|dl|<=0.5), 17.16% feedback (dl<-0.5); by contacts, 23.38% / 60.26% / 16.36%. Mean signed layer step is only +0.078 (MEASURED HERE).
- HUBS ARE INHIBITORY NORMALISERS, NOT INTEGRATORS. Of the top-50 neurons by total degree, 74% are inhibitory (base rate 30.8%); top-100, 70%; top-200, 67.5%. By name they are CT1, Am1, Li39/Li32, LPi4b/LPi21, LT33, Pm12 (wide-field GABAergic optic-lobe gain-control cells), APL (the single GABAergic mushroom-body feedback neuron, 2,398 out / 2,651 in, 106,932 output contacts) and DPM (MEASURED HERE, stats9.py/stats13.py).
- THERE IS NO RICH CLUB BEYOND DEGREE IN MY MEASUREMENT. Normalised rich-club ratio phi(k)/phi_configuration is 1.008 at k>50, 1.034 at k>100, 1.080 at k>200, 1.004 at k>300, and falls below 1 above that (MEASURED HERE, Chung-Lu analytic null; the null is only valid where phi_cfg<<1, i.e. k<=~300, so the sub-1 values at high k are not interpretable). THIS CONFLICTS with Lin et al. (Nature 634:153-165, 2024) who report rich-club organisation in FlyWire involving ~30% of the connectome; the difference is dataset (FlyWire brain vs MaleCNS brain+VNC) and/or null model, and should be re-checked before being relied on.
- E/I IS BALANCED AT THE INPUT EVEN THOUGH ONLY 31% OF NEURONS ARE INHIBITORY. For the 153,908 neurons with >50 input contacts, the fraction of input contacts from inhibitory (GABA/Glu) sources has mean 0.451, median 0.442, sd 0.179, p10 0.237, p90 0.676; 61.6% of neurons sit in [0.2,0.5] and only 0.2% receive essentially no inhibition. This works because inhibitory neurons have higher fan-out: mean out-degree 76.3 (I) vs 57.3 (E); GABA neurons are 13.2% of cells but emit 21.7% of contacts (MEASURED HERE, stats6.py/stats13.py).
- INTEGRATION IS DISTRIBUTED, NOT KEY-LOOKUP. The median neuron takes only 10.9% of its input contacts from its single strongest presynaptic partner (mean 14.8%, p90 32.3%), and needs a median of 9 partners to reach 50% of its input contacts and 22 to reach 80%. Median total input is 272 contacts (mean 657) (MEASURED HERE, stats13.py).
- CELL TYPES ARE NUMEROUS AND TINY. 11,770 distinct type labels in the mirror (11,751 non-null 'type' values in the official Janelia annotation table); median 3 neurons per type, mean 14.2; 69.1% of types have <=4 neurons, 90.9% have <=10; 98.4% of types have <=100 neurons but together those cover only 36.1% of neurons (the remainder is a handful of huge repeated optic-lobe columnar types, max 3,377). Excluding the visual system: 61,435 neurons in 11,034 types, median size 3 (MEASURED HERE, stats6.py). Schlegel et al. (Nature 634:139-152, 2024) annotated 8,453 cell types in FlyWire, of which 4,581 were new relative to hemibrain.
- STEREOTYPY IS GRADED, NOT ABSOLUTE. Between the two hemispheres of the SAME animal: per-type neuron counts correlate at r=0.9989 and 81.4% of the 10,948 bilateral types have EXACTLY equal counts; the type x type contact matrix (normalised per presynaptic neuron) correlates at Pearson r=0.972 raw, r=0.834 on log1p, Spearman rho=0.672. BUT presence/absence of a type-level connection agrees at Jaccard 0.498, rising only to 0.646 for the strongest connections; only 66.9% of type-level connections present on the left are present on the right (MEASURED HERE, stats8.py). Within-cell-type CV of in-degree is 0.175 (median over 5,580 types with >=4 neurons) versus 1.623 across the whole brain -- a ~9x reduction.
- THE REPRODUCIBLE INFORMATION IS IN CELL-TYPE-PAIR RATES, AND IT IS LARGE. Held-out test (fit a Poisson degree-corrected SBM on one hemisphere's subgraph, evaluate the log-likelihood of the OTHER hemisphere's subgraph, using the test hemisphere's own degrees): degrees alone (configuration model) buy 1.27-1.30 bits/edge over Erdos-Renyi; adding the 11,770-block cell-type structure learned on the other hemisphere buys a FURTHER 4.12 (L->R) / 4.25 (R->L) bits/edge. The in-sample ceiling (dcSBM fitted on the test hemisphere itself) is 5.14/5.32, so ~80% of the type-level structure generalises across hemispheres (MEASURED HERE, stats10.py). This is direct, quantitative evidence that the connectome contains far more than its degree sequence -- 3.2x more information than degrees -- and that it is type-level, not synapse-level.
- YOU CANNOT COMPRESS THE TYPE STRUCTURE INTO A FEW POPULATIONS. Held-out gain over the configuration model as a function of the partition: 27 superclasses -> 1.12 bits/edge; top-200 types (rest lumped) -> 1.39; top-500 -> 2.16; top-1000 -> 2.32; top-2000 -> 2.52; top-4000 -> 3.01; top-8000 -> 3.87; all 11,770 -> 4.18. The curve does not saturate (MEASURED HERE, stats11.py). There is no small set of 'important' populations.
- MODULARITY IS REGIONAL. Directed Newman modularity Q=0.508 for a 3-way optic/central-brain/VNC partition and Q=0.432 for the 27 superclasses; a 16-way purely spatial k-means partition gives Q=0.301 and 64-way gives Q=0.168. Within-hemisphere edges are 76.4% of the total; explicit cross-hemisphere edges are 16.1% (L->R 8.05%, R->L 8.01%) (MEASURED HERE, stats6.py/stats7.py).
- NEUROMODULATION IS SPARSE BUT BROADCAST. 541 dopaminergic/octopaminergic/serotonergic neurons (0.325% of the CNS) directly contact 34,700 neurons (20.8% of the brain). Octopamine: 101 neurons -> 23,281 targets (14.0%), mean fan-out 434. Dopamine: 392 neurons -> 16,934 targets (10.2%), mean fan-out 138. Serotonin: 48 neurons -> 6,640 targets (4.0%), mean fan-out 216 (MEASURED HERE, stats6.py).
- THE 'COGNITIVE' CIRCUITS ARE A ROUNDING ERROR. Kenyon cells 4,064 neurons = 2.44% of the CNS; MBONs 97 = 0.058%; mushroom-body dopaminergic neurons 344 = 0.21%; central-complex EB/PB core (EPG/PEN/PEG/Delta7/EL/ER/ExR) 432 = 0.26%; fan-shaped-body columnar 1,402 = 0.84%. Meanwhile the visual system is 63.1% (optic-lobe intrinsic alone 53.6%), the VNC 12.3%, central-brain intrinsic 19.3%, descending neurons 1,316 = 0.79%, motor/efferent 1,019 = 0.61% (MEASURED HERE, stats12.py).
- CONNECTOME + OBJECTIVE PREDICTS REAL NEURAL ACTIVITY IN THE FLY VISUAL SYSTEM. Lappalainen, Tschopp, Prakhya, McGill, Nern, Shinomiya, Takemura, Gruntman, Macke & Turaga, 'Connectome-constrained networks predict neural activity across the fly visual system', Nature 634 (2024) [PMID 39261740]: a deep mechanistic network with connectivity fixed to 64 optic-lobe cell types and only single-neuron/single-synapse parameters learned, trained to estimate optic flow, reproduces measured neural responses agreeing with 26 experimental studies. This is the strongest existing evidence that specific fly wiring plus a task objective determines function.
- CONNECTOME + NEUROTRANSMITTER SIGN ALONE PRODUCES CORRECT BEHAVIOURAL PREDICTIONS. Shiu, Sterne et al., 'A Drosophila computational brain model reveals sensorimotor processing', Nature 634:210-219 (2024): a leaky integrate-and-fire model of the whole FlyWire brain (>125,000 neurons, ~50M synapses) with no fitted parameters beyond a uniform LIF and ACh-excitatory/GABA-Glu-inhibitory signs predicts which neurons respond to sugar/water/bitter taste and recovers feeding and grooming circuits.
- THE CANONICAL COUNTEREXAMPLE IS THE MUSHROOM BODY. Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel, 'Random convergence of olfactory inputs in the Drosophila mushroom body', Nature 497:113-117 (2013): PN->KC connectivity is statistically indistinguishable from random draws with the measured fan-in. Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel & Sompolinsky, 'Optimal degrees of synaptic connectivity', Neuron 93:1153-1164 (2017): the measured fan-in K~6-7 is near the theoretical optimum for dimensional expansion. Here the STATISTIC is the whole story and the specific wiring carries nothing -- exactly what the repository's H1d null found (real PN->KC vs degree-preserving shuffle: -0.1 seed-SD).
- ANATOMY UNDERDETERMINES DYNAMICS. Marder & Goaillard, 'Variability, compensation and homeostasis in neuron and network function', Nat Rev Neurosci 7:563-574 (2006), and Bargmann & Marder, 'From the connectome to brain function', Nat Methods 10:483-490 (2013): widely different conductance/synaptic parameter sets produce identical circuit output, and neuromodulators reconfigure the same anatomy into different functional circuits. A connectome is a constraint set, not a program.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Depth in time, not in layers: the fly is 2-3 synaptic stages deep and ~10^5 units wide, and iterates. | Median 1-2 hops from sensory neuron to descending/motor neuron; 99.7% of the CNS within 3 hops; information-flow depth of descending neurons 2.35 layers; max depth 8 even over strong (>=10-contact) edges. Yet 97% of neurons sit in one strongly connected component, so the effective computational depth comes from re-entrant passes over a shallow graph, bounded by biophysical time constants (~5-50 ms), not by layer count. | Build a SHALLOW, WIDE, WEIGHT-TIED recurrent block (2-4 distinct stages) iterated many times per token, rather than a 24-96 layer stack. This is the architectural claim the repository has never actually tested: phase 2's K=0/1/3 free-energy steps varied iterations of a DEEP-in-parameters model; the fly says the parameter graph should be shallow and the iteration count should be large and input-dependent. Budget: effective depth = stages x iterations; match FLOPs to a Gated DeltaNet twin and compare at equal wall-clock. | established |
| Lateral, not forward: 60% of edges connect units at the same processing depth. | Classifying all 10.5M edges by information-flow layer difference gives 22.7% forward / 60.2% lateral / 17.2% feedback (by contacts: 23.4 / 60.3 / 16.4), with a mean signed step of only +0.078 layers. Reciprocity is 19.2%, 506x the density-matched random expectation; Lin et al. (Nature 2024) report that reciprocal pairs are stronger than unidirectional ones and that the over-represented reciprocal motif is excitatory-inhibitory (ACh-GABA). | The dominant operator should be a WITHIN-STAGE recurrent mixing matrix with strong reciprocal E-I pairs, not a feedforward map. Concretely: per stage, a Dale-signed square matrix whose bidirectional entries are initialised larger than unidirectional ones, plus a comparatively thin forward projection to the next stage. A transformer's attention is entirely 'lateral' in this sense and a deep MLP stack is entirely 'forward'; the fly's ratio says roughly 3:1 lateral:forward. | established |
| Hubs are inhibitory normalisers. The highest-degree units in the brain compute gain control, not content. | 74% of the top-50 hubs by total degree are inhibitory against a 30.8% base rate; they are named wide-field GABAergic cells (CT1, Am1, Li39, LPi4b, Pm12) and APL, the single GABAergic neuron that inhibits >99.9% of the 2,045 Kenyon cells per hemisphere (repo docs/CIRCUITS.md). Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson, Neuron 66:287-299 (2010) established that the antennal lobe implements divisive normalisation. The top 1% of neurons by out-degree contact 84.3% of the brain in one hop. | Give each recurrent population ONE (or a few) high-fan-in/high-fan-out inhibitory units that read the population's total activity and divisively normalise it, rather than a LayerNorm applied pointwise. Mathematically y_i = x_i / (sigma + (1/n) sum_j g_j x_j) with g learned and g >= 0 -- a learnable divisive normaliser whose statistics are computed by an actual unit in the graph, so it can be modulated. This is cheap (rank-1) and is the one place where a 'hub' is justified. | established |
| Balance the INPUTS, not the unit counts: 31% inhibitory neurons but 44% inhibitory input contacts. | Median neuron receives 44.2% of its input contacts from GABA/Glu sources (mean 0.451, sd 0.179, 61.6% of neurons in [0.2,0.5]); this arises because inhibitory neurons have 1.33x the out-degree of excitatory ones (76.3 vs 57.3). Tightly balanced E/I input is the standard condition for asynchronous-irregular, high-dimensional, near-critical dynamics. | Set the fraction of inhibitory UNITS to ~0.30 and the fraction of inhibitory INPUT MASS to ~0.44 by giving inhibitory units ~1.3x the fan-out. Then regularise each unit's inhibitory input fraction toward 0.44 +/- 0.18 during training (a cheap per-unit penalty). The repository's AX6 chose a Lyapunov-exponent target instead, which is a global scalar; per-unit E/I input balance is a much stronger, local, biologically measured constraint and is directly checkable against the number above. | promising |
| Thousands of small typed populations, each with a stereotyped connection RATE to each other type -- the connectome is a dcSBM, not a matrix. | 11,770 types, median 3 neurons each, 69% with <=4 neurons; 81.4% of bilateral types have exactly equal neuron counts on the two sides (count correlation r=0.9989). Held-out across hemispheres, the type-pair block structure carries 4.12-4.25 bits/edge ABOVE the degree-only configuration model (which itself carries only 1.27-1.30 bits/edge over Erdos-Renyi), with ~80% of it generalising. The type-level graph has median out-fan-out of 104 other types and is 1.1% dense. | Generate the ARCHITECTURE from the connectome instead of using the adjacency matrix as a mask: sample a network of P typed populations with sizes drawn from the measured per-type counts, signs from the measured type neurotransmitters, and block connection probabilities omega_rs from the measured type-pair rates; learn the weights inside each block. This is a genuinely different object from anything the repository has run: phase 1's H5 used the raw adjacency as a frozen mask on ONE monolithic RNN (which cannot express block structure as an inductive bias) and phase 1's H3 (cell-type dcSBM prior) was NEVER RUN. The matched control is not a degree-preserving edge shuffle; it is a LABEL-SHUFFLED dcSBM with identical block-size distribution and identical degree sequence, which by measurement destroys 4.75 bits/edge of structure. | promising |
| Strong connections are the blueprint; weak connections are a sample. Use a structured prior plus a learned residual. | Between hemispheres of the same animal, type-level connection presence/absence agrees at Jaccard 0.498 overall but 0.646 for the strongest connections, and 8.1% of one hemisphere's edges connect a type pair with ZERO edges on the other. Contact counts per connection are heavy-tailed: median 5, mean 9.95, max 2,591; the top 10% of connections carry 42.7% of all contacts, the top 1% carry 12.6%. Type-pair identity explains R^2=0.628 of the variance in log contact count (label-shuffle floor 0.299). | Split every block into a FIXED, high-confidence skeleton (type pairs whose connection is bilaterally reproducible and strong -- roughly the top 10% of connections carrying ~43% of the mass) and a LEARNED, randomly initialised residual for the rest. Initialise skeleton magnitudes from log(1+contacts). Do not freeze the whole graph and do not learn the whole graph: freeze the 10% that biology reproduces, learn the 90% it does not. | promising |
| Sparse broadcast neuromodulation as a global gating channel, separate from the signed synaptic graph. | 541 DA/OA/5HT neurons (0.325% of the CNS) directly contact 20.8% of the brain; octopaminergic neurons average 434 postsynaptic targets each. Modulators change time constants, gains and plasticity rates rather than injecting current, which is why the repository's own mirror stores ZERO contact weight for every dopaminergic, octopaminergic, serotonergic, histaminergic and 'unclear' presynaptic neuron (332,316 edges, 3.2% of the graph) -- they are structurally present but carry no weight. | Add a very low-rank (rank 1-8) multiplicative channel that sets per-population gain, per-population decay rate and the fast-weight write rate, driven by a handful of scalar state variables. Crucially it must NOT be a signed additive edge: the repo's own LIMITATIONS.md already flags that wiring modulators as ordinary signed edges is physiologically wrong. Parameter cost ~0.3% of the model, matching the biological fraction. | promising |
| Distributed integration with no dominant key: every unit is a weighted sum over tens of inputs. | The median neuron takes only 10.9% of its input contacts from its strongest partner, needs 9 partners for 50% and 22 for 80% of its input, and has 272 input contacts in total. In-degree median 36, mean 63.1; out-degree median 45, mean 63.1; Gini 0.55/0.46; Hill tail exponent ~3.2-3.4 (i.e. heavy-tailed with a soft cutoff, and NOT lognormal -- log-degree skew is -0.65, kurtosis +2.09). | Effective fan-in per unit should be ~30-60 with a heavy-tailed weight distribution, not a hard top-k and not dense. This argues AGAINST hard k-WTA style sparse retrieval as the main memory operation (the repository's mushroom-body module used 5% k-WTA and lost to Gated DeltaNet: 0.981 vs 0.998) and FOR soft, graded, many-partner integration. Initialise weights from a distribution with Gini ~0.5 and tail exponent ~3.3 rather than Gaussian. | established |
| Regional modularity with a thin bilateral bridge: 76% of edges stay within a hemisphere, 16% cross. | Directed Newman modularity Q=0.508 for optic/central/VNC, Q=0.432 for the 27 superclasses; within-hemisphere edges 76.4%, cross-hemisphere 16.1%. Lin et al. (Nature 634:153-165, 2024) report a small-worldness coefficient S_Delta = 141 for the FlyWire brain (C. elegans 3.21, internet 98.1) -- short paths despite high clustering. My own sampled undirected clustering coefficient is 0.202, ~295x the density-matched random expectation (uncorrected for degree heterogeneity, so treat the multiplier as an upper bound). | Use a mixture-of-modules topology with ~70-80% of connections inside a module and ~15-20% crossing, rather than a fully dense mixer or a hard MoE with no cross-talk. The bilateral bridge is the interesting bit: two near-identical copies of the network with a sparse 16% cross-connection is a concrete, testable, cheap architecture nobody has tried for language. | speculative |
| The brain spends 63% of itself on the sensory front-end and <4% on the memory/navigation circuits -- allocation is the opposite of what an ML designer assumes. | Visual system 63.1% of the CNS (optic-lobe intrinsic alone 53.6%), VNC 12.3%, central-brain intrinsic 19.3%. Kenyon cells 2.44%, MBONs 0.058%, MB dopaminergic neurons 0.21%, central-complex ring core 0.26%, fan-shaped body columnar 0.84%, descending neurons 0.79%, motor neurons 0.61%. | If the analogy is taken seriously, the overwhelming majority of the parameter budget belongs in a wide, shallow, repeated, columnar INPUT TRANSFORM (a tokeniser/embedding front-end with thousands of repeated identical units), and the recurrent 'cognitive' core is small. This inverts the usual LM allocation and is directly testable at fixed parameter count: a 50M-parameter model with 32M in a repeated columnar front-end and 3M in a recurrent core, against a standard allocation. | speculative |
| The bottleneck for a state-of-the-art claim is the REGIME, not the architecture: the fly's wiring is a constant-memory recurrent system with no attention. | The fly has no mechanism resembling content-addressable attention over an unbounded past; its state is carried in ~166,700 graded units plus fast synaptic changes, i.e. constant memory. Its whole connectome is ~5x10^7 synapses, comparable to a 50M-parameter model. Its computational advantage is not capacity but efficiency and robustness under a hard energy budget. | Do not attempt to beat a transformer on perplexity at long context. Define the regime where a shallow, wide, constant-memory, heavily normalised recurrent system CAN win and pre-register it: (a) throughput/energy per token at fixed quality; (b) robustness to distribution shift and adversarial token noise; (c) streaming/online adaptation within a document without gradient steps; (d) length extrapolation far beyond training. Report bits/char AND tokens/joule against a Gated DeltaNet twin at matched parameters and matched FLOPs. | speculative |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- MaleCNS v1.0: 166,700 neurons; 10,520,431 connections (>=3 synaptic contacts); 101,422,396 synaptic contacts; density 3.8e-4 (measured, stats1.py)
- Neurotransmitter fractions by neuron (MaleCNS): ACh 62.22%, Glu 17.58%, GABA 13.24%, histamine 4.73%, unclear 1.80%, dopamine 0.24%, unknown 0.11%, octopamine 0.06%, serotonin 0.03%; inhibitory (GABA+Glu) = 30.82% of neurons but 38.6% of outgoing contacts
- Out-degree: mean 63.11, sd 94.27, median 45, p90 114, p99 355, max 7,749; Gini 0.464; top 1% of neurons hold 10.3% of out-edges. In-degree: mean 63.11, sd 102.44, median 36, p90 140, p99 412, max 9,193; Gini 0.551
- Weighted degree (contacts): out mean 608.4, median 332, max 138,620; in mean 608.4, median 248, max 113,521; in-Gini 0.654
- Contacts per connection: mean 9.95, median 5, p75 10, p90 20, p95 31, p99 74, p99.9 195, max 2,591. Top 10% of connections carry 42.7% of contacts; top 1% carry 12.6%. 59.5% of connections have >=5 contacts (86.2% of contacts); 26.3% have >=10 (64.6% of contacts)
- Giant strongly connected component: 161,820 neurons = 97.07%; 98.96% of edges and 98.23% of contacts inside it; 4,861 singleton SCCs
- Reciprocity 0.1915 (2,014,596 of 10,520,431 edges); 506x the Erdos-Renyi expectation
- Sensory->motor hop counts (all >=3-contact edges, 17,937 sensory seeds): descending neurons median 1 / mean 1.52 / p90 2; motor+efferent median 1 / mean 1.30 / p90 2; 10.76% at hop 0, 19.76% hop 1, 57.82% hop 2, 11.36% hop 3, 0.21% hop 4, 0.01% hop 5; 127 unreachable
- Same with only >=10-contact connections: descending median 2 / mean 1.72; motor median 2 / mean 1.58; depth distribution 6.3% / 18.9% / 32.6% / 23.4% / 4.5% at hops 1-5; max 8; 4,950 unreachable
- Information-flow traversal mean layer: descending 2.35, motor/efferent 2.27, central-brain intrinsic 2.91, visual projection 4.82, optic-lobe intrinsic 5.17; whole-brain mean 3.81; 99.9% reached
- Edge layer-difference census: forward 22.68% of edges / 23.38% of contacts; lateral 60.16% / 60.26%; feedback 17.16% / 16.36%; mean |dl| = 0.556, mean dl = +0.078
- Per-neuron inhibitory input fraction (n=153,908 with >50 input contacts): mean 0.451, median 0.442, sd 0.179, p10 0.237, p90 0.676; 61.6% in [0.2,0.5]; 0.2% below 0.05; 15.8% above 0.6
- Out-degree by sign: excitatory mean 57.3, inhibitory mean 76.3 (ratio 1.33)
- Hub inhibitory fraction: top-50 0.740, top-100 0.700, top-200 0.675, top-500 0.568, top-2000 0.559 (base rate 0.308)
- Top 1% of neurons by out-degree (1,667 cells) directly contact 140,489 neurons = 84.3% of the CNS
- Input concentration: strongest partner supplies mean 14.8% / median 10.9% / p90 32.3% of a neuron's input contacts; median 9 partners for 50% of input, 22 for 80%; median total input 272 contacts, mean 657
- Cell types: 11,770 labels (official table: 11,751 non-null 'type', 8,199 'flywireType', 27 superclasses, 23,932 'instance', 13,747 'group', 203 'itoleeHl' hemilineages); median 3 neurons/type, mean 14.2, max 3,377; 69.1% of types <=4 neurons, 90.9% <=10
- Sides: L 74,070, R 74,235, unknown 18,003, midline 392. Edge sides: L->L 36.18%, R->R 40.20%, L->R 8.05%, R->L 8.01%; within-hemisphere total 76.38%, cross-hemisphere 16.06%
- Left-right stereotypy: per-type counts r=0.9989, 81.4% of 10,948 bilateral types have identical counts; type x type contact matrix Pearson r=0.972 (raw), 0.834 (log1p), Spearman 0.672; presence/absence Jaccard 0.498 (0.566 at >=5 contacts/presyn-neuron, 0.627 at >=20, 0.646 at >=100); 66.9% of L-present type edges also present on R
- Within-cell-type CV of in-degree: median 0.175, mean 0.213 (5,580 types with >=4 neurons) vs 1.623 across the whole brain
- HELD-OUT INFORMATION CONTENT (train one hemisphere, test the other, Poisson dcSBM, test-hemisphere degrees): configuration model gains 1.272 (L->R) / 1.300 (R->L) bits/edge over Erdos-Renyi; cell-type blocks gain a further 4.120 / 4.245 bits/edge; in-sample ceiling 5.136 / 5.315 bits/edge; fraction generalising 0.802 / 0.799; 8.1% / 8.7% of test edges join a type pair with zero edges on the training hemisphere
- Held-out gain vs partition granularity (bits/edge over configuration model): 27 superclasses 1.117; top-200 types 1.386; top-500 2.159; top-1000 2.320; top-2000 2.515; top-4000 3.010; top-8000 3.869; all 11,770 4.182 (curve does not saturate)
- Type-pair identity explains R^2 = 0.628 of the variance in log contact count; label-shuffle floor R^2 = 0.299
- Cell-type x cell-type block matrix: 1,522,629 nonzero of 138,532,900 (1.099% dense); mean 129.4 / median 104 downstream types per type; 44.3% of types are inhibitory
- Modularity: Q=0.508 (optic/central/VNC), Q=0.432 (27 superclasses), Q=0.365/0.301/0.233/0.168 (8/16/32/64-way spatial k-means)
- Rich club phi(k)/phi_configuration: 1.008 (k>50), 1.034 (k>100), 1.080 (k>200), 1.004 (k>300); null invalid above k~300
- Sampled undirected clustering coefficient 0.202 (median 0.162), ~295x density (not degree-corrected -- upper bound)
- Functional allocation: visual system 63.15% (optic-lobe intrinsic 53.63%), central-brain intrinsic 19.30%, VNC 12.26%, Kenyon cells 2.44% (4,064), ORN-like 1.58%, ascending 1.11% (1,854), fan-shaped body columnar 0.84% (1,402), descending 0.79% (1,316), motor+efferent 0.61% (1,019), CX EB/PB core 0.26% (432), MB dopaminergic 0.21% (344), MBONs 0.058% (97)
- Neuromodulator reach: 541 DA/OA/5HT neurons (0.325%) contact 34,700 neurons (20.8%); OA 101 -> 23,281 (14.0%, fan-out 434); DA 392 -> 16,934 (10.2%, fan-out 138); 5HT 48 -> 6,640 (4.0%, fan-out 216)
- DATA ARTIFACT: all 332,316 zero-weight edges (3.2% of the graph) come from presynaptic neurons predicted as dopamine (54,172), octopamine (43,826), serotonin (10,380), histamine (49,181) or unclear (174,757) -- 100% of those neurons' edges carry no contact count in this mirror. Any weighted analysis silently drops all modulatory and photoreceptor output.
- Central-brain-ish subnetwork (50,155 neurons): 3,950,792 edges, density 1.57e-3, out-degree mean 78.8 / median 57 / max 2,950, giant SCC 98.1%, reciprocity 0.214
- FlyWire reference numbers: 139,255 proofread neurons, ~5x10^7 chemical synapses (Dorkenwald et al., Nature 634:124-138, 2024); Lin et al. (Nature 634:153-165, 2024) report small-worldness S_Delta=141 vs C. elegans 3.21 and internet 98.1, rich-club involving ~30% of the connectome, and over-represented reciprocal ACh-GABA pairs whose edges are stronger than unidirectional ones
- Hemibrain reference: ~25,000 neurons, ~20M synapses, ~half the central brain, average ~39% of synaptic connectivity recovered per neuron (Scheffer et al., eLife 9:e57443, 2020)
- MaleCNS dimorphism: 7,319 cross-matched central-brain cell types, 114 dimorphic, 262 male-specific, 69 female-specific, totalling 4.8% of male and 2.4% of female neurons (Berg et al., bioRxiv 2025.10.09.680999 / Cell 2026)

### Dead ends

- THE RAW ADJACENCY MATRIX AS A FROZEN MASK. The repository's H5 found the 256/512/1024-node whole-graph mask indistinguishable from a degree-preserving shuffle at all three sizes (effects -0.6, -0.2, -0.4 seed-SD; all CIs include 0) and from Erdos-Renyi, while a dense same-N layer beat it everywhere. This is not surprising given the biology: the type-level structure that actually carries the 4.1 bits/edge is destroyed by a degree-preserving shuffle, so the shuffle SHOULD have been worse -- the fact that it was not means the character-LM task could not exploit any of it. Do not re-run this experiment.
- THE EXTRACTED 148-NEURON CENTRAL-COMPLEX RING AS A GENERAL STATE TRACKER. It scored 0.24 vs 0.67 for an unstructured layer of equal size (-9.1 seed-SD) on heading tracking at 4x training length. The ring attractor is a specialist for ONE circular variable with a hard-wired 45-degree shift; it has 432 neurons = 0.26% of the fly brain and is not a general-purpose memory. It also verified correctly against every anatomical check (docs/CIRCUITS.md), so this is a result, not a bug.
- REAL PN->KC WIRING AS A SPARSE EXPANSION. H1d: real vs degree-preserving shuffle, -0.1 seed-SD, CI includes 0. This matches Caron et al. (Nature 497:113-117, 2013), which showed PN->KC convergence IS random, and Litwin-Kumar et al. (Neuron 93:1153-1164, 2017), which showed the measured fan-in K~6-7 is the optimum. The transferable content is the three numbers (K~6, ~2,000 KCs from ~60 PN channels, 5-10% sparseness), not the matrix. Nothing further to extract here.
- HARD k-WTA SPARSE MEMORY AS THE PRIMARY MIXER. The repository's mushroom-body module (5% k-WTA, fan-in 7, error-gated delta write) reached 0.981 on MQAR against 0.998 for a compute-matched Gated DeltaNet and 1.000 for linear attention and DeltaNet. The connectome agrees: the median neuron integrates ~30-60 inputs with its strongest partner supplying only 11% of its input, which is graded integration, not winner-take-all retrieval. k-WTA belongs in one specialised 2.4%-of-the-model expansion, not as the architecture.
- THE ERROR-MAGNITUDE GATE FOR MEMORY WRITES. H1b: a learned input-dependent gate beat the sigmoid(a log ||eps||^2 + b) dopamine-analogue gate by -2.6 seed-SD; H1c: the delta/depression write did not beat plain Hebb (+0.9 seed-SD, CI includes 0). 'Dopamine = prediction error' as implemented is not a useful inductive bias. The biology that IS worth keeping is different: dopaminergic neurons are 0.24% of cells with mean fan-out 138 reaching 10.2% of the brain -- a sparse broadcast GAIN channel, not a per-write error signal.
- K-STEP FREE-ENERGY DESCENT AT THE TESTED SCALE. AIF-H1: K=3 beat K=0 by 0.026 bits/char (0.7 seed-SD, below the pre-registered 2 SD bar) and lost to a wider control; the adaptive-K rule never fired (K_mean = 3.00 in every run). Note the honest caveat recorded in VERIFICATION.md: the K0_wide control was over-provisioned (262k vs 135k parameters, 1.88 vs 1.42 MFLOPs/token), so the comparison was unfair against the hypothesis. This does not rescue the result, but it does mean 'iterate a shallow network many times' has NOT been cleanly tested -- only 'iterate a deep-in-parameters network 3 times' has.
- THE WHOLE-GRAPH SPECTRAL / LYAPUNOV STORY, AT LEAST AS MEASURED HERE. The input-normalised signed central-brain matrix has four degenerate |lambda|=1 eigenvalues from sink structures and a next tier at 0.910 versus 0.892 for a sign-shuffled control and 0.979 for an all-excitatory control. The real sign structure is barely distinguishable from shuffled signs by spectral radius. Do not build a hypothesis on 'the connectome's E/I signs put it at the edge of chaos' without much more careful work.
- WEIGHTED ANALYSES OF THIS DATA COPY THAT INCLUDE MODULATORY NEURONS. Every dopaminergic, octopaminergic, serotonergic, histaminergic and 'unclear' presynaptic neuron has 100% of its edges stored with weight 0 in the repository's mirror (332,316 edges). Any contact-weighted computation silently excludes all neuromodulatory and all photoreceptor output. Re-download the official connectome-weights feather before doing any weighted modulatory analysis.
- 'NO RICH CLUB' AS A SETTLED FACT. My measurement says the normalised rich-club ratio never exceeds 1.08 where the null is valid, but Lin et al. (Nature 634:153-165, 2024) report rich-club organisation involving ~30% of the FlyWire connectome. Different dataset, different null, and my analytic Chung-Lu null breaks down exactly where the interesting k values are. Do not build on either claim without a proper Maslov-Sneppen double-edge-swap null on the same dataset.

### Open questions

- Can the type-level dcSBM structure -- 4.12-4.25 held-out bits/edge, 80% of it hemisphere-generalising -- be converted into a task advantage? The repository has never tested this: H3 (cell-type dcSBM prior) is marked 'not run'. The matched control must be a LABEL-SHUFFLED dcSBM with identical block-size distribution and degree sequence (which costs 4.75 bits/edge by measurement), not the degree-preserving edge shuffle used in H5.
- Why does the curve of held-out information versus number of blocks not saturate (27 superclasses 1.12 bits/edge, 11,770 types 4.18)? Is the information genuinely spread over thousands of small populations, or is my 'top-K by neuron count' ordering simply the wrong ordering? Re-run the sweep ordering types by their individual contribution to held-out likelihood, which would say how many typed populations an architecture actually needs.
- Is the 2-3-hop shallowness real, or an artefact of the >=3-contact threshold creating a dense small-world graph? The >=10-contact version gives median 2 hops and max depth 8, which is still shallow, but the right test is a contact-weighted shortest path or an information-flow traversal with a realistic threshold. Also: what IS the effective depth in time, given ~5-50 ms membrane time constants and behavioural reaction times of ~30-100 ms?
- Does the 60% lateral / 23% forward / 17% feedback ratio have a computational optimum, or is it an incidental consequence of the modular layout? A direct experiment: sweep the lateral:forward:feedback ratio in a fixed-parameter recurrent model and see whether 3:1:0.75 is a maximum on any task.
- Does per-unit E/I INPUT balance at 0.44 +/- 0.18 do anything that a global spectral-radius or Lyapunov constraint does not? This is a cheap, local, biologically measured regulariser and it has never been tested against the global one.
- Are the inhibitory hubs (74% of the top-50) doing divisive normalisation in a way that differs measurably from LayerNorm/RMSNorm? A single high-fan-in inhibitory unit per population is rank-1 and almost free; the question is whether making the normaliser a MODULATED unit inside the graph (so a neuromodulatory channel can change the normalisation) beats a fixed pointwise norm.
- Stereotypy ACROSS INDIVIDUALS, not just across hemispheres. My Jaccard 0.498 / r=0.972 numbers are within one animal. Schlegel et al. (eLife 10:e66018, 2021) compared hemibrain to FAFB for the olfactory system and Schlegel et al. (Nature 634:139-152, 2024) report that ~1/3 of hemibrain-proposed cell types could not be reliably re-identified in FlyWire -- but I could not retrieve the quantitative cross-individual connectivity correlations. Getting them would set the true ceiling on how much of the wiring is worth encoding.
- How much of the connectome's apparent structure is developmental wiring economy (spatial proximity, lineage) rather than computation? A partial answer exists in my numbers -- a 16-way purely spatial partition already gives Q=0.301 against Q=0.432 for the 27 functional superclasses -- but the right test is to fit a dcSBM with lineage/hemilineage blocks (203 itoleeHl labels are in the annotation table) and see how much of the 4.18 bits/edge it absorbs.
- Is there a defined regime where a shallow, wide, constant-memory, heavily normalised recurrent system genuinely beats a Gated DeltaNet twin at matched parameters and matched wall-clock? Candidates: tokens per joule at fixed bits/char; robustness to token-level corruption; within-document online adaptation with no gradient steps; length extrapolation far beyond training. None of these has been measured in this repository, and none of them is perplexity.
- The repository's GPU plan (20M-125M hybrids vs a Gated-DeltaNet twin) has never run. Every phase-1 and phase-2 verdict comes from ~135k-parameter models on 4 CPU cores with 400-600 optimiser steps. Some of these nulls may simply be scale artefacts, and the honest position is that the architecture questions above are untested rather than answered.

### Relevance to language models

The connectome numbers argue for a specific and unusual architecture, and against most of what has been tried so far in this repository. Three measurements dominate. FIRST, the fly is 2-3 synaptic stages deep (median 1-2 hops sensory to motor, 99.7% of the CNS within 3 hops, information-flow depth 2.35 for descending neurons) but 97% of its neurons sit in a single strongly connected component with 60% of edges lateral and only 23% forward. That is a shallow, wide, weight-tied recurrent block iterated in time - the opposite of a 24-96 layer stack, and also the opposite of what phase 2 built (a deep-in-parameters model iterated 3 times). SECOND, held-out across hemispheres, cell-type block structure carries 4.12-4.25 bits/edge beyond the degree sequence, 3.2x what degrees themselves carry, with 80% generalising. The connectome therefore DOES contain real, large, non-statistical information - but it lives in type-pair connection RATES, not in individual synapses, and phase 1's degree-preserving-shuffle null does not contradict this, it only shows that a monolithic masked RNN on character prediction cannot use it. The untested architecture is to GENERATE the network from the dcSBM (population sizes, signs, block rates) and control against a label-shuffled dcSBM, which by measurement destroys 4.75 bits/edge. This is exactly the repository's H3, marked 'not run'. THIRD, the fly's hubs are inhibitory normalisers (74% of the top-50 by degree, against a 30.8% base rate), E/I is balanced at the input (44% of a median neuron's input contacts are inhibitory even though only 31% of neurons are), integration is distributed (9 partners for 50% of input, strongest partner only 11%), and neuromodulation is a 0.3%-of-the-brain broadcast channel reaching 21% of neurons. Together these say: graded many-partner integration with divisive normalisation computed by real units and a low-rank multiplicative gating channel - not hard k-WTA retrieval, not error-magnitude write gates, both of which already lost to compute-matched baselines. The honest limit: the fly has no attention-like mechanism and ~5x10^7 synapses, so a state-of-the-art claim must be staked in a defined regime - tokens per joule, robustness, online within-document adaptation, or length extrapolation - and never on long-context perplexity.


## Neurons and synapses of the fly: what a language-model engineer must get right (graded vs spiking, compartments, short-term plasticity, gap junctions, co-transmission, energy, timescales)

*Key: `neurons-synapses`.*

### Summary

"The single most important correction to the project's implicit model of fly biology is that the fly is not a spiking network of point neurons. Insect neurons are unipolar; the soma carries no synapses and is not in the signal path. Antennal-lobe projection neurons are electrotonically extensive, spikes initiate far from the soma and are small there, and input to one dendritic branch does not reach the rest of the cell - Gouwens & Wilson had to model one unitary connection as dozens of release sites spread over many branches to match real EPSPs (J Neurosci 29:6239, 2009; I verified the deposited parameters: Ra 80 Ohm.cm, Cm 1 uF/cm2, Rm 10 kOhm.cm2, so tau_m = 10 ms). Whole classes are genuinely non-spiking, including a class of antennal-lobe local neurons that lacks voltage-gated sodium current altogether (eNeuro 2023), the optic-lobe graded pathway, and Kenyon cells which sit at the edge of excitability (input resistance >10 GOhm, responding to 6 +/- 5% of odours with a spike or two; Turner et al. 2008).\n\nA neuron is also not one computational unit. The APL neuron has a measured space constant of ~50 um and behaves as at least four independent local normalisers; calyx normalisation is applied per PN bouton. The faithful unit is the neurite compartment, and the faithful neuron model is a two-layer subunit model with local divisive inhibition, not a tanh node.\n\nThe synapse is not a scalar. It is contact count times per-contact conductance times short-term depression and facilitation state times receptor kinetics set by the (pre-type, post-type) pair - and the last factor means Dale's law does not determine edge sign, since the same transmitter acts through fast ionotropic and slow metabotropic receptors and connectome transmitter labels are image predictions, not measurements. Measured short-term dynamics span 300-400 ms and ~10 s in the antennal lobe alone. Three whole signalling systems are missing from any connectome: gap junctions (eight innexins, a symmetric sign-free Laplacian, required for memory retrieval and for flight stability), neuropeptides (~40-50, volume transmission addressed by receptor expression), and co-transmission (nitric oxide antagonising dopamine from the same dopaminergic cells).\n\nEnergy fixes the operating point: ~160 pmol O2/min, ~5.1e12 ATP/s, ~9e6 ATP per spike, ~140,000 neurons, therefore a mean rate of about 4 Hz. Almost nothing updates on any tick. Graded signalling is not cheap; it is expensive and high-bandwidth, which is why the fly reserves it for short range.\n\nVerdict: build a graded, continuous-time, conductance-based, compartmented rate model with explicit short-term synaptic state, a fixed multi-timescale ladder, parameters tied by cell type, and conditional computation. Do not build a spiking network - here it would be ML fashion, and less faithful than the rate model, not more."

### Established (with sources)

- Insect central neurons are UNIPOLAR: the soma is a passive appendage hanging off a primary neurite and carries no synapses. It is not in the signal path. Somatic patch recordings therefore poorly control voltage elsewhere in the cell. (Gouwens & Wilson 2009, J Neurosci 29(19):6239-6249, 'Signal propagation in Drosophila central neurons'.)
- Drosophila antennal-lobe projection neurons are electrotonically EXTENSIVE. Simulations in that paper predict action potentials initiate far from the soma, in the proximal axon; somatic spikes can be so small they are hard to distinguish from large EPSPs. (Gouwens & Wilson 2009.)
- A unitary connection in the fly is a DISTRIBUTED object, not a point synapse: synaptic input to a single dendritic branch propagates poorly to the rest of the cell and cannot reproduce measured unitary EPSP size; a good fit required modelling one unitary ORN->PN connection as dozens of release sites spread over many dendritic branches. (Gouwens & Wilson 2009.) This is the physiological justification for treating connectome CONTACT COUNT as the connection variable.
- Whole classes of Drosophila central neurons are genuinely NON-SPIKING. A class of antennal-lobe local neurons (R32F10-GAL4) shows graded potentials with no voltage-gated Na+ current, apparently via translational regulation of the para Na-channel gene, and is electrotonically compartmentalised so that activation of neurites in one region does not propagate across the lobe. ('Nonspiking interneurons in the Drosophila antennal lobe exhibit spatially restricted activity', eNeuro 10(1):ENEURO.0109-22.2022, 2023; author list not verified here.)
- Graded (non-spiking) signalling is a first-class transmission mode in flies, not an exception: in nonspiking interneurons transmitter release is driven by graded membrane-potential changes, which preserves fine stimulus detail that spike thresholding would discard. Established for insect nonspiking local interneurons generally (Burrows, 'Nonspiking interneurons and motor control in insects', Adv Insect Physiol) and for fly visual interneurons (de Ruyter van Steveninck & Laughlin 1996 Nature 379:642; Juusola et al., 'The performance of synapses that convey discrete graded potentials in an insect visual pathway', J Neurosci 19(23):10584, 1999).
- Single neurons in the fly are NOT single computational units.
