The GABAergic APL neuron of the mushroom body has a measured space constant of about 50 um: activity evoked at the vertical-lobe tip is undetectable at the lobe branch point, and activating alpha'/beta' Kenyon cells activates only the alpha' lobe of APL while neurites 2-3 um away stay silent. APL behaves as several independent local normalisers aligned with mushroom-body compartments. (Amin, Apostolopoulou, Suarez-Grimalt, Vrontou & Lin 2020, eLife 9:e56954, 'Localized inhibition in the Drosophila mushroom body'.)
- Normalisation in the fly is applied LOCALLY, per dendritic site. APL synapses with 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5%) and 126/136 PNs (92.6%); 84+/-2% of APL->PN synapses sit on PN boutons; inhibition is locally graded around active PN terminals, so KC claw responses stay homogeneous while PN bouton responses vary; silencing APL makes KC responses mirror PN input strength. (Prisco, Deimel, Yeliseyeva, Fiala & Tavosanis 2024, eLife 13:e74172.)
- Kenyon cells sit at the boundary between graded and spiking. They have extremely high somatic input resistance (>10 GOhm reported), respond to only ~6 +/- 5% of odours, and fire very few spikes per odour. (Turner, Bazhenov & Laurent 2008, J Neurophysiol 99:734-746, 'Olfactory representations by Drosophila mushroom body neurons'.) Forcing extra Na+ channel expression into KCs DECREASES their excitability, which shows their native excitability is finely tuned and low. (Greenin-Whitehead et al. 2025, J Physiol, 'Ectopic sodium channel expression decreases excitability of Drosophila Kenyon cells'.)
- Axo-dendritic polarity is weak in the fly. Kenyon cells carry presynapses in their calyx DENDRITES (Christiansen et al. 2011, J Neurosci 31(26):9696, 'Presynapses in Kenyon cell dendrites in the mushroom body calyx of Drosophila'). Input and output sites are intermingled on the same neurites throughout the central brain, so a neuron is not a feedforward in-degree/out-degree unit.
- KC dendritic architecture is claw-based: olfactory KCs have roughly 3-10 claw-shaped dendrites, each wrapping a single multisynaptic bouton from one cholinergic PN, inside a 'microglomerulus' where one PN bouton is enwrapped by on average ~13 KC claws from different KCs. (Reviewed in the mushroom-body connectome and calyx literature; see Li et al. 2020, eLife 9:e62576, and 'The cellular architecture of memory modules in Drosophila supports stochastic input integration', eLife 2022;11:e77578.)
- Short-term synaptic dynamics in the fly are strong and multi-timescale. In the antennal lobe, slow adaptation of PN responses is PRESYNAPTIC DEPRESSION, not lateral inhibition: PN calcium responses adapt to ~50% with timescales of 3-40 s (mean degree of adaptation 0.57 for PNs vs -0.06 +/- 0.1 for ORNs); vesicle-release depression has tau ~ 10 s with a fast component of 300-400 ms; GABA-A/GABA-B manipulations do not change the timescales. (Martelli & Fiala 2019, eLife 8:e43735.)
- Both depression and facilitation exist in Drosophila and are molecularly switchable: Synaptotagmin 7 switches short-term plasticity from depression to facilitation by suppressing release probability. (Sci Rep 11, 2021, 'Synaptotagmin 7 switches short-term synaptic plasticity from depression to facilitation by suppressing synaptic transmission'; author list not verified here.)
- KC->MBON plasticity is bidirectional and partly presynaptic, and is readable in paired-pulse ratio: dopamine pairing gives LTD with an INCREASE in paired-pulse ratio, soluble-guanylate-cyclase pairing gives LTP with a DECREASE in PPR. (Yamada et al. 2024, J Physiol, 'Cyclic nucleotide-induced bidirectional long-term synaptic plasticity in Drosophila mushroom body'.) There is also postsynaptic plasticity at the same synapse via nAChR alpha-subunit turnover (Pribbenow et al. 2022, eLife 11:e80445).
- Electrical synapses (innexin gap junctions) are present throughout the Drosophila CNS and are INVISIBLE to EM connectomics (no T-bar, no chemical synapse signature). ShakB (innexin 8) is the most widely expressed neuronal innexin; a CNS-wide anatomical map of all eight innexins exists. (Ammer, Vieira, Fendl & Borst 2022, Current Biology 32(9), 'Anatomical distribution and functional roles of electrical synapses in Drosophila'; author list moderately confident.)
- Gap junctions do real computation in the fly, and not only synchronisation: they DESYNCHRONISE a motor circuit to stabilise flight ('Gap junctions desynchronize a neural circuit to stabilize insect flight', Nature 2023); innexin 7 contributes to synchronised activity in the antennal lobe and to olfactory function (bioRxiv 2025.01.19.633780); electrical synapses between mushroom-body neurons are required for consolidated memory retrieval (2019, PMC6529013). Within the mushroom body, APL is gap-junction-coupled to DPM (Li et al. 2020, eLife 9:e62576).
- Co-transmission is common, so one neuron does not equal one transmitter. Nitric oxide is a co-transmitter in a subset of dopaminergic neurons, acts more slowly than dopamine, antagonistically to it, and shortens memory retention (Aso et al. 2019, eLife 8:e49257). The serotonergic CSD neuron co-releases acetylcholine. Three of 44 lateral-horn output-neuron types co-release two FAST transmitters (Dolan et al. 2019, eLife 8:e43079).
- Neuropeptide signalling is extensive and extrasynaptic ('wireless'): Drosophila has on the order of 40-50 neuropeptide precursor genes and a comparable number of GPCR receptors, and peptide action is volume transmission that does not follow the synaptic graph at all. (Nassel & Zandawala 2019, Prog Neurobiol 179:101607, 'Recent advances in neuropeptide signaling in Drosophila, from genes to physiology and behavior'.) Concrete instances: sNPF sensitises an attraction glomerulus while tachykinin suppresses an aversion glomerulus under starvation (Ko et al. 2015, eLife 4:e08298); NPF, sNPF, serotonin, insulin and AstA retune MBON/DAN gains at memory read-out (Tsao et al. 2018, eLife 7:e35264).
- The sign of a connectome edge is a property of the (pre, post) PAIR, not of the presynaptic neuron. Acetylcholine is excitatory via nAChR but also acts through muscarinic receptors; GABA acts through fast ionotropic Rdl (GABA-A) and slow metabotropic GABA-B (largely presynaptic); glutamate is inhibitory in most known fly CNS cases via GluCl-alpha but is not universally so. The repository already records this as a limitation (LIMITATIONS.md).
- Energy sets a hard activity budget. Measured resting Drosophila brain oxygen consumption is ~160 pmol O2/min, i.e. a resting ATP budget of ~5.1 x 10^12 ATP/s; with a cost of ~9 x 10^6 ATP per spike (blowfly axon estimate) spread over ~140,000 neurons this bounds the whole-brain MEAN firing rate at about 4 Hz. (Calculation given in 'State of Brain Emulation Report 2025', arXiv:2510.15745, using 'Direct quantification of the metabolic heat output of individual Drosophila brains', 2025, PubMed 40832252/42296970.)
- Graded signalling is NOT cheap. It costs ~10^4 ATP to transmit one bit at a chemical synapse, but ~10^6-10^7 ATP for graded (analog) signalling in an interneuron or photoreceptor, or for spike coding. (Laughlin, de Ruyter van Steveninck & Anderson 1998, Nat Neurosci 1:36-41, 'The metabolic cost of neural information'.) Blowfly photoreceptors transmit ~5x more information than Drosophila photoreceptors but use ~10x more energy per bit (Niven, Anderson & Laughlin 2007, PLoS Biol 5:e116).
- The best current connectome-constrained model that actually PREDICTS measured fly neural activity is a GRADED RATE model, not a spiking one: 64 cell types of the optic-lobe motion pathway, ~45,000 neurons over 721 columns, with unknown single-neuron and single-synapse parameters tied PER CELL TYPE and optimised by deep learning for motion detection; predictions agreed with measurements across 26 experimental studies. (Lappalainen et al. 2024, Nature 634:1132-1140, 'Connectome-constrained networks predict neural activity across the fly visual system'.)
- The whole-brain LIF model of the fly (Shiu, Sterne et al. 2024, Nature 634:210-219, 'A Drosophila computational brain model reveals sensorimotor processing') uses point LIF neurons with alpha synapses, a uniform per-synapse weight, no gap junctions, no neuromodulation and no learning, and validates qualitatively (activating sugar-sensing model neurons predicts which biological neurons respond). It is not fit to measured activity traces.
- Divisive normalisation is a measured, quantitative computation in the fly input stage: PN_i = Rmax * ORN_i^n / (sigma + m * sum_j ORN_j)^n with n ~ 1.5, implemented largely by GABA-B presynaptic inhibition of ORN terminals; total ORN drive spanning 57x across four concentration decades is compressed to 6.6x at the PN output. (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson 2010, Neuron 66:287-299.)

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The computational unit is the NEURITE COMPARTMENT, not the neuron. Build a two-layer (subunit) neuron: several semi-independent dendritic subunits, each with local nonlinear integration and local divisive inhibition, summed at one output site. | Gouwens & Wilson 2009: PNs are electrotonically extensive, single-branch input does not reach the rest of the cell. Amin et al. 2020: APL space constant ~50 um, so one neuron is several independent inhibitory units. Prisco et al. 2024: normalisation is graded around individual PN boutons. KCs have 3-10 claws, each fed by exactly one PN bouton. Mathematically this is the Poirazi & Mel (2003) two-layer model: y = f(sum_k g_k(w_k . x)) rather than y = f(w . x). | A 'neuron' becomes a group of d subunits: h_k = phi(W_k x) / (1 + sum_j A_kj phi(W_j x)) with LOCAL pooling, then y = psi(sum_k c_k h_k). Cost is the same as one wide layer with block-diagonal structure plus a local normaliser; it is fully differentiable and GPU-friendly. It is a genuinely different primitive from a transformer's global mixing and is the most defensible 'fly-derived' architectural claim available, because the wiring-level claim is already dead in this project. | established |
| Use CONDUCTANCE-based units, not current-based units. Division is then free and numerically stable, and matches what shunting inhibition actually does. | Membrane equation at steady state: V = (g_leak E_leak + sum_i g_i E_i) / (g_leak + sum_i g_i). Inhibitory conductance with E_i near rest divides rather than subtracts. This is exactly the measured AL/APL computation (Olsen et al. 2010: n ~ 1.5 divisive normalisation via GABA-B; Prisco et al. 2024: local divisive normalisation in the calyx). Jordan, Sacramento, Wybo, Petrovici & Senn (arXiv 2104.13238, 2021) show the same equation is Bayes-optimal cue combination, with PRECISION = total synaptic conductance, and that it is learnable. | Replace LayerNorm/softmax gating with y = (sum_i g_i(x) E_i) / (g0 + sum_i g_i(x)), g_i >= 0. This gives (a) built-in normalisation, (b) a principled precision/attention variable (conductance) that is a nonnegative learned quantity rather than a softmax, (c) no explicit 1/x blow-up. This is the single best candidate for a new, principled, fly-derived primitive that has not been tried in language modelling. | promising |
| Tie parameters by CELL TYPE, not by neuron. The genome specifies ~11,770 types for 166,700 neurons; that ratio is the fly's weight-sharing scheme. | The connectome carries stereotyped types across individuals; single-neuron and single-synapse biophysics are type properties. Lappalainen et al. 2024 (Nature 634:1132) made this work: unknown parameters were shared per cell type across 45,000 neurons and 721 columns, and the fitted model matched 26 independent experimental studies. | A type-tied model is a structured low-rank / factorised weight scheme: W = sum over (type_i, type_j) of alpha_{titj} * M_{titj}, where M is the connectome block and alpha is one learned scalar per type pair. Parameter count scales with types^2 (~10^8 at fly scale, far fewer for a chosen subgraph), not neurons^2. This is the only form in which connectome structure survived phase 1's null result, because phase 1 tested the raw MASK, never the type-tied parameterisation. It is a testable, pre-registerable hypothesis: type-tied initialisation vs shuffled-type-tied vs free. | promising |
| Model the connection as contact-count x per-contact conductance x short-term state x receptor kinetics. The connectome gives you the first factor only. | Gouwens & Wilson 2009 needed dozens of distributed release sites to match one unitary EPSP, i.e. contact count is the physiologically meaningful weight variable. The fly appears to arrange morphology so that response is LINEAR in synapse number ('Morphology and synapse topography optimize linear encoding of synapse numbers in Drosophila looming responsive descending neurons', eLife reviewed preprint 99277). | w_ij(t) = n_ij * g_type(pre_i, post_j) * D_ij(t) * F_ij(t), where D and F are per-edge depression and facilitation state variables with their own fixed time constants. This makes the weight matrix input-dependent and history-dependent at zero parameter cost per edge (the state is dynamic, not learned), which is precisely a fast-weight / linear-attention mechanism with a biologically fixed, rather than learned, gate. | established |
| Short-term plasticity is the fly's fast-weight memory and it is multi-timescale. Give every connection at least two depression time constants (~0.3 s and ~10 s) and one facilitation constant. | Martelli & Fiala 2019 (eLife 8:e43735): vesicle-release depression tau ~ 10 s with a fast 300-400 ms component; PN responses adapt to ~50%; adaptation timescales 3-40 s and are NOT abolished by GABA manipulation. Syt7 switches the same synapse between depression and facilitation. Tsodyks-Markram: dx/dt = (1-x)/tau_D - u x delta(t-t_sp), du/dt = (U-u)/tau_F + U(1-u) delta(t-t_sp). | A Tsodyks-Markram synapse is a rank-1 gated linear recurrence, i.e. exactly the algebra of modern linear-attention / state-space models (Mamba, RetNet, Gated DeltaNet). This is the one place where fly biology and current SOTA ML primitives genuinely coincide, so it is the highest-probability route to a competitive model. Concretely: replace a learned forget gate with a per-channel Tsodyks-Markram state whose tau ladder is fixed geometrically (0.3 s, 3 s, 10 s, 100 s in token-equivalents). Note the phase-1 warning: a LEARNED gate beat a fixed error gate, so the claim must be about robustness, continual adaptation or parameter cost, not about raw perplexity. | promising |
| Separate the FAST graph (chemical, signed, directed, in the connectome) from the SLOW field (neuromodulators and neuropeptides, unsigned, diffuse, absent from the connectome) and from the SYMMETRIC coupling (gap junctions, also absent from the connectome). | Three physically distinct signalling systems. Chemical: ms, point-to-point, sign set by the pre-transmitter x post-receptor pair. Electrical: instantaneous, symmetric (or rectifying), sign-free, a graph Laplacian; ShakB and seven other innexins; invisible to EM. Peptidergic/aminergic: seconds to hours, volume transmission, addressed by RECEPTOR EXPRESSION rather than by wiring; ~40-50 peptides (Nassel & Zandawala 2019); NO as a slow antagonistic co-transmitter of dopamine (Aso et al. 2019). | Three tensors with three update rates: (1) W_fast applied every token; (2) L_gap, a symmetric nonnegative Laplacian applied as x <- x + eps * L_gap x, a cheap diffusion/consensus step that costs one sparse matmul and acts as a learned low-pass smoother; (3) m_t, a low-dimensional (8-32) modulator vector updated every N tokens that multiplicatively sets per-type gains and per-type LEARNING RATES. The third is the only one with an existing ML analogue that works (FiLM / hypernetwork conditioning); the second is untried and cheap. Crucially, the peptide field is a BROADCAST, so it is O(types), not O(neurons^2). | established |
| The learning rule is three-factor and gated by a modulator that acts as an inferred LEARNING RATE, not as an error signal to be backpropagated. | Dopamine release onto KC->MBON synapses requires coincident postsynaptic activity plus nAChR/NMDAR/D1 and rutabaga adenylyl cyclase (Ueno et al. 2017, eLife 6:e21076); forward vs backward pairing flips plasticity sign via Dop1R1/Dop1R2 (Handler et al. 2019, Cell 178:60); each of ~15-16 compartments has its own DAN valence, decay constant and update rule (Aso & Rubin 2016, eLife 5:e16135); NO gives a compartment its own forgetting rate (Aso et al. 2019). Mathematically dW = eta(m_t) * pre * post with eta set by a modulator, which is Bayesian filtering of the weight: eta = posterior precision ratio. | Test-time training with a modulator-set learning rate. The repository's own numpy study (docs/phase3/ADAPTIVE_RATE.md) shows a surprise-gated rate matches a change-point Kalman ORACLE (recall 0.81 vs 0.81 at sigma=0.3) without knowing the noise or jump rate, while the best FIXED rate depends on the regime. So the honest claim is robustness and tuning-freedom under non-stationarity, not raw accuracy. Pre-register on distribution shift, not on perplexity. | established |
| Build an explicit ladder of fixed, geometrically spaced timescales spanning six orders of magnitude, because that is the fly's actual architecture. | Measured ladder: ~1 ms synaptic transmission; membrane tau_m = Rm*Cm = 10 ms (from the Gouwens & Wilson deposited model, Rm = 10,000 Ohm.cm^2, Cm = 1 uF/cm^2); ORN Weber-Fechner adaptation tau ~ 250 ms; fast vesicle depression 300-400 ms; slow presynaptic depression tau ~ 10 s (range 3-40 s); memory phases minutes (STM) / hours (ITM) / 24 h (LTM) across 15-16 MB compartments; spacing-effect integrators of 30-120 min (cytosolic PKC-delta), 3-4.5 h (mitochondrial), 8-9 h (pyruvate flux) (Comyn et al. 2024, eLife reviewed preprint 92860). | Multi-timescale state with FIXED, non-learned decay constants lambda_k = exp(-dt/tau_k), tau_k geometric over the ladder, one head per timescale, plus a modulator that reweights heads. This is a known-good ML design (multi-timescale RNNs, S4/S5 diagonal state matrices, Hyena) so it inherits their performance, and it is biologically warranted rather than chosen by search. | established |
| Sparsity in the fly is a TIME sparsity enforced by energy, not only a population sparsity. Almost nothing updates on any given tick. | Whole-brain mean firing bounded at ~4 Hz by the ~5.1 x 10^12 ATP/s budget and ~9 x 10^6 ATP/spike. With a ~10 ms membrane constant this means the average neuron emits an event roughly once per 25 membrane time constants. KCs respond to 6 +/- 5% of odours with very few spikes (Turner et al. 2008); the locust analogue gives ~10% of KCs responding with 0-2 spikes (Ray, Aldworth & Stopfer 2020, eLife 9:e53281). | This is the strongest argument for CONDITIONAL COMPUTATION, and it gives a defined regime in which a state-of-the-art claim is actually possible: FLOPs or joules per token at matched quality. Implement as an update gate per unit/block (skip the update when the change would be below a threshold), or as sparse MoE with a hard capacity derived from the 4 Hz analogue. Do NOT implement it as spiking on a GPU. Pre-register the metric as quality-per-FLOP and quality-per-joule, with a matched dense control. | promising |
| Keep the model GRADED. Do not go spiking. Spiking in the fly buys long-range transmission and a hard sparsity threshold; only the second matters for a language model and a k-WTA or top-k gate already delivers it. | Fly somatic spikes are small and initiate far from the soma; many classes do not spike at all; the fly's own high-bandwidth channels (photoreceptor->LMC, LPTCs, T4/T5, AL local neurons) are graded, and graded signalling carries MORE bits per second than spiking in the same animal (Juusola et al. 1999; de Ruyter van Steveninck & Laughlin 1996). The best predictive connectome-constrained fly model is a rate model (Lappalainen et al. 2024), and the whole-brain LIF model (Shiu et al. 2024) is validated only qualitatively. | Use a continuous-time graded model, discretised as an exponential-Euler recurrence: v_{t+1} = a v_t + (1-a) * I_t, a = exp(-dt/tau_m). Add a hard top-k for the expansion layer. A spiking implementation is warranted ONLY if the deliverable is energy on neuromorphic hardware (Loihi 2, SpiNNaker2), which is a legitimate but different claim, and it costs you surrogate gradients, dead-neuron pathologies and worse GPU throughput. On the evidence, spiking-for-fidelity in a fly-aligned language model is ML fashion, not biology. | established |
| Make the edge sign a RECEPTOR property of the (pre-type, post-type) pair, learned under a transmitter constraint, rather than a Dale sign on the presynaptic neuron. | Acetylcholine acts through nicotinic (fast excitatory) and muscarinic (slow, can be inhibitory) receptors; GABA through Rdl (fast ionotropic) and GABA-B (slow, presynaptic); glutamate through GluCl-alpha (inhibitory) but also NMDA/mGluR. One neuron can co-release two fast transmitters (3 of 44 LHON types, Dolan et al. 2019). Transmitter identity in connectomes is a PREDICTION from EM images, not a measurement (Eckstein et al. 2024, Cell, 'Neurotransmitter classification from electron microscopy images at synaptic sites in Drosophila melanogaster'; ~6 transmitters, per-synapse accuracy moderate - verify the exact figure before citing). | Parameterise sign and kinetics per (pre-type, post-type) pair: s_{titj} in [-1,1] learned, with a prior centred on the predicted transmitter and a learned time constant drawn from a small set {fast ionotropic ~5 ms, slow metabotropic ~200 ms}. This is strictly more expressive than the repository's current Dale-signed core and it is closer to the biology, not further from it. It also removes the '30.8% inhibitory' number as an axiom, which the repository currently treats as one. | established |
| Add the missing symmetric graph. Gap junctions are a real, sizeable part of the circuit that no connectome contains, so any model claiming connectome-completeness is wrong; treat the Laplacian coupling as a learned addition, not as a confound. | Eight innexins mapped across the CNS, ShakB the dominant neuronal one (Ammer et al. 2022, Curr Biol 32(9)); gap junctions desynchronise a flight circuit (Nature 2023); Inx7 synchronises antennal-lobe activity; APL-DPM are electrically coupled in the mushroom body. Electrical coupling is instantaneous, bidirectional and sign-free: dv_i/dt includes sum_j g_ij (v_j - v_i), a graph Laplacian. | x <- x - eps * L x, with L a learned sparse symmetric nonnegative Laplacian over the same node set. One extra sparse matmul. It gives cheap local consensus/smoothing and, with a negative eps on selected blocks, a decorrelating (desynchronising) operation. Untried in language modelling as far as I know; cheap to test as an ablation. | speculative |
| Input normalisation in two stages, copied quantitatively from the antennal lobe: per-channel Weber-Fechner adaptation followed by population divisive normalisation, then repeated at the expansion layer. | Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson 2010 (Neuron 66:287): n ~ 1.5, 57x input range compressed to 6.6x. Kadakia & Emonet 2019 (eLife 8:e45293): ORN adaptation tau ~ 250 ms; the two mechanisms are ADDITIVE (identity classification ~95%/~85% with both vs ~70%/~40% with neither at N_ID = 100/1000). Amin et al. 2020 and Prisco et al. 2024: at the KC layer the same computation is applied LOCALLY, not globally. | Stage 1: u_t = x_t / (eps + a_t), da/dt = (/x/ - a)/tau_adapt, tau_adapt ~ 250 ms-equivalent. Stage 2: y_i = u_i^1.5 / (sigma + k * sum_j u_j)^1.5 with sigma ~ 12, k ~ 0.1 in Olsen units. Stage 3 at the expansion layer: the same form but with the pool restricted to a local neighbourhood (an APL analogue with a ~50 um-equivalent radius), NOT a single global scalar. The repository's current 5% global k-WTA is explicitly contradicted by Amin 2020. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Passive biophysics of a Drosophila antennal-lobe PN (values read directly from the deposited NEURON model, ModelDB 118662, fit_params.hoc and LeakConductance.mod, for Gouwens & Wilson 2009): axial resistivity Ra = 80 Ohm.cm; specific capacitance Cm = 1 uF/cm^2; specific membrane resistance Rm = 10,000 Ohm.cm^2 (leak conductance 1e-4 S/cm^2); leak reversal e = -60 mV; electrode seal modelled at Re = 30 MOhm, Ce = 0.001 (NEURON units). Derived membrane time constant tau_m = Rm * Cm = 10 ms. UNCERTAIN: the paper's headline fitted values may differ from the deposited defaults; re-check the paper before quoting it as the fit.
- Kenyon cell somatic input resistance > 10 GOhm; mean fraction of KCs responding to a given odour 6 +/- 5% (Turner, Bazhenov & Laurent 2008, J Neurophysiol 99:734).
- Kenyon cell input resistance 1.36 GOhm and membrane capacitance 17.2 pF in an example control cell (Greenin-Whitehead et al. 2025, J Physiol). UNCERTAIN: the 17.2 pF figure is large for a 2-3 um soma and may include the whole arbor; treat the order of magnitude, not the digit.
- KC fan-in measured on MaleCNS v1.0 in this repository (docs/CIRCUITS.md): 5.16 (left) / 5.07 (right) PN TYPES per KC, 5.30 / 5.23 PN NEURONS per KC; PN fan-out 82.6 / 83.2 KCs; 2,019 / 2,045 KCs; 92.5-92.7% of KCs receive PN input; 183,709 / 189,838 PN->KC synaptic contacts.
- KC dendritic claws: 3-10 per olfactory KC, each wrapping one multisynaptic PN bouton; a microglomerulus has one central PN bouton enwrapped by on average ~13 claws from different KCs.
- APL electrotonic space constant ~ 50 um; 52.6 +/- 13.4 KC->APL and 49.6 +/- 17.9 APL->KC synapses per KC; 96,867 KC->APL and 15,656 APL->KC sites on the APL skeleton; distance-weighted connectivity predicts each KC inhibits itself ~40% more than it inhibits another KC; APL modelled as four independent regions (Amin et al. 2020, eLife 9:e56954).
- APL coverage and calyx normalisation: APL synapses with 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5%) and 126/136 PNs (92.6%); 84 +/- 2% of APL->PN synapses on PN boutons; PN->APL vs APL->PN synapse numbers correlate r^2 = 0.63 (Prisco et al. 2024, eLife 13:e74172).
- Locust giant GABAergic neuron (GGN) full-scale model as a calibration point for attenuation in a large insect interneuron: 5,283 compartments, ~2 mm path length, 10 mV depolarisation at the alpha lobe attenuates to 5-9 mV in the calyx; feedback inhibition range 400-500 um; ~10% of 50,000 KCs respond per odour with 0-2 spikes (Ray, Aldworth & Stopfer 2020, eLife 9:e53281; ModelDB 262670).
- Antennal-lobe divisive normalisation: exponent n ~ 1.5, semi-saturation sigma ~ 12, pool weight k ~ 0.1 (Olsen units); total ORN drive spans 57x over four concentration decades, total PN drive compressed to 6.6x (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson 2010, Neuron 66:287).
- Adaptation timescales: ORN Weber-Fechner adaptation tau ~ 250 ms, A0 ~ 0.1, 50 ORN types (Kadakia & Emonet 2019, eLife 8:e45293); PN slow presynaptic depression adapts to ~50% over 3-40 s, vesicle-release depression tau ~ 10 s with a fast component of 300-400 ms, mean degree of adaptation 0.57 for PNs vs -0.06 +/- 0.1 for ORNs (Martelli & Fiala 2019, eLife 8:e43735).
- Antennal-lobe local-neuron inventory (hemibrain): 196 ALLNs per hemisphere, 5 lineages, 4 morphological classes, 25 groups, 74 cell types, ~40% bilateral, ~half feed back onto ORN axons (presynaptic inhibition), with lineage-dependent output fractions to ORNs of 1.2-5.2% vs >16% (Schlegel et al. 2021, eLife 10:e66018).
- Energy budget: resting Drosophila brain O2 consumption ~160 pmol O2/min => ~5.1 x 10^12 ATP/s; cost per spike ~9 x 10^6 ATP (blowfly axon estimate); over ~140,000 neurons this bounds the MEAN firing rate at ~4 Hz (arXiv:2510.15745, 'State of Brain Emulation Report 2025', using the 2025 Drosophila brain calorimetry measurement, PubMed 40832252/42296970).
- Information-energy costs: ~10^4 ATP per bit transmitted at a chemical synapse; ~10^6-10^7 ATP for graded signalling in an interneuron or photoreceptor, or for spike coding (Laughlin, de Ruyter van Steveninck & Anderson 1998, Nat Neurosci 1:36-41). Blowfly photoreceptors carry ~5x the information of Drosophila photoreceptors at ~10x the energy per bit (Niven, Anderson & Laughlin 2007, PLoS Biol 5:e116).
- Graded vs spiking bandwidth in the fly visual pathway: graded large monopolar cells reported at up to ~2,000 bits/s, roughly 5x the spiking rate-coded channel. UNCERTAIN: the 2,000 bits/s figure came from a search summary attributed to the blowfly LMC literature (Juusola et al. 1999, J Neurosci 19:10584; de Ruyter van Steveninck & Laughlin 1996, Nature 379:642); verify the exact number before publishing it.
- Whole-brain LIF baseline (Shiu et al. 2024, Nature 634:210-219): a uniform per-synapse weight of 0.275 mV. A third-party whole-CNS reproduction on MaleCNS (165,122 neurons, ~90 M synapses, ~6.2 M connections at >= 5 synapses) had to retune this to 0.125 mV to avoid saturation, with point neurons, chemical synapses only, no gap junctions, no neuromodulation and no learning. UNCERTAIN/weak: the retuning figure is from third-party GitHub READMEs (sukoji/flyboard, davidvvliet/MaleCNS-Connectome), recorded in docs/aif/LITERATURE.md.
- Connectome-constrained RATE model scale (Lappalainen et al. 2024, Nature 634:1132-1140): 64 cell types, ~45,000 neurons, 721 columns of the central visual field, parameters tied per cell type, agreement with 26 experimental studies. UNCERTAIN: the exact free-parameter count was not verifiable here; the load-bearing fact is per-TYPE tying, not per-neuron.
- Mushroom body inventory (this repository, MaleCNS v1.0): 48/49 MBONs per side with 32 compartment labels, 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 DANs, 1 APL (GABA), 1 DPM (predicted dopaminergic in this mirror), MBON transmitters 25 ACh / 10-11 GABA / 13 glutamate, 339/300 MBON->DAN feedback edges (docs/CIRCUITS.md).
- Memory-phase timescales: 15-16 MB compartments each with its own decay; spacing-effect integrators of 30-120 min (cytosolic PKC-delta), 3-4.5 h (mitochondrial), 8-9 h (sustained pyruvate flux); sustained MP1/PPL1-gamma1pedc calcium for ~2 h after spaced training (Comyn, Preat, Pavlowsky & Placais 2024, eLife reviewed preprint 92860).
- Repository baseline to beat, for calibration: the sparse mushroom-body memory scored 0.981 against compute-matched Gated DeltaNet at 0.998, and the extracted 148-neuron central-complex ring scored 0.24 vs 0.67 for an unstructured layer of equal size (RESULTS.md, docs/phase3/REFLECTION.md).

### Dead ends

- Spiking for fidelity's sake. The fly's own high-bandwidth channels are graded; somatic spikes are small and initiate far from the soma (Gouwens & Wilson 2009); whole classes are non-spiking (eNeuro 2023 AL local neurons; LPTCs; T4/T5); and the only connectome-constrained fly model that actually predicts measured activity across 26 studies is a RATE model (Lappalainen et al. 2024, Nature 634:1132). On a GPU, spiking adds surrogate-gradient bias, dead-neuron pathology and worse throughput, and buys nothing a top-k gate does not. Spiking is warranted only if the deliverable is energy on neuromorphic hardware, which is a different, narrower claim.
- Treating a connectome node as one computational unit. APL has a ~50 um space constant and acts as at least four independent local normalisers (Amin et al. 2020, eLife 9:e56954); PN dendritic branches do not communicate well (Gouwens & Wilson 2009); a locust GGN attenuates 10 mV to 5-9 mV across its arbor (Ray et al. 2020). The 'one neuron = one tanh unit' assumption used in this repository's phase-1 masked RNN is therefore wrong in a way that could by itself explain the null result, and it was never tested.
- A single global sparsity scalar in the expansion layer. The repository's 5% global k-WTA (APL analogue) is directly contradicted by the measured locality of APL inhibition (Amin et al. 2020; Prisco et al. 2024). Any future MB-style layer should use several local pools, not one.
- Taking the presynaptic transmitter as the edge sign. The effective sign is set by the postsynaptic receptor: ACh via nAChR vs muscarinic, GABA via Rdl vs GABA-B, glutamate via GluCl vs NMDA/mGluR; some neurons co-release two fast transmitters (Dolan et al. 2019, eLife 8:e43079); and connectome transmitter labels are EM-image PREDICTIONS (Eckstein et al. 2024, Cell), not measurements. The '30.8% inhibitory' figure is therefore a prediction statistic, not a biological constant, and should not be an axiom (it currently is, in docs/aif/AXIOMS.md AX6).
- Wiring modulatory neurons as ordinary signed edges. Dopaminergic, octopaminergic, serotonergic and peptidergic neurons act by volume transmission onto receptors, on second-to-hour timescales, with sign depending on the receptor subtype present (Dop1R1 vs Dop2R vs DAMB), and NO acts antagonistically to dopamine from the same cell (Aso et al. 2019, eLife 8:e49257). The repository already records this (LIMITATIONS.md) but it means the modulatory fraction of the 10.5 M edges is physiologically meaningless as drawn.
- Believing that graded signalling is metabolically cheap. It costs ~10^6-10^7 ATP per bit-second-equivalent in an interneuron or photoreceptor versus ~10^4 ATP per bit at a chemical synapse (Laughlin et al. 1998), and blowfly photoreceptors pay ~10x the energy per bit of Drosophila ones for ~5x the information (Niven et al. 2007). 'Graded is efficient' is false; graded is high-bandwidth and short-range, spikes are low-bandwidth and long-range.
- Assuming the connectome specifies the circuit. Gap junctions carry no T-bar and are absent from every EM connectome, yet ShakB and seven other innexins are distributed across the CNS, gap junctions desynchronise a flight circuit (Nature 2023), Inx7 synchronises the antennal lobe, APL and DPM are electrically coupled, and MB electrical synapses are required for consolidated memory retrieval. Neuropeptide volume transmission (~40-50 peptides, Nassel & Zandawala 2019) is likewise entirely outside the graph. Any statement of the form 'the connectome contains the computation' is false on the biology.
- Expecting biological detail to improve next-token perplexity per FLOP. There is no precedent in the literature for a biologically detailed model beating a transformer on language perplexity at matched compute, and this repository's phases 1 and 2 add two more negative results (RESULTS.md). Any state-of-the-art claim must be made in a regime the fly is actually optimised for: energy or FLOPs per token, continual adaptation under distribution shift, one-shot association, sample efficiency, robustness to damage.
- Point LIF whole-brain simulation as a target to imitate. The published whole-brain fly LIF model uses a single uniform per-synapse weight and validates qualitatively; a third-party MaleCNS reproduction had to retune that weight by more than a factor of two to avoid saturation. This is a brittle regime with one global knob, and it has no learning, no gap junctions and no modulation.
- Modelling the soma as the integrator. Insect neurons are unipolar and the soma has no synapses; integration happens in the neurite tree and the spike initiation zone is in the proximal axon (Gouwens & Wilson 2009). A standard point-neuron 'soma' is not a simplification of the fly, it is a different object.

### Open questions

- What is the actual quantitative relation between synaptic CONTACT COUNT and functional connection strength in the fly central brain, per cell-type pair? The linear-encoding result for looming descending neurons (eLife reviewed preprint 99277) is suggestive but not general. Without this, every connectome-weighted model has an unmeasured scale factor per edge type.
- What fraction of Drosophila central-brain neurons are non-spiking? Non-spiking classes are documented in the antennal lobe, the optic lobe and among local interneurons, but I found no census. This directly decides whether an event-driven implementation is even biologically coherent at the whole-brain level.
- Membrane and synaptic time constants per CELL TYPE. The only well-documented passive parameter set is for antennal-lobe PNs (Ra = 80 Ohm.cm, Cm = 1 uF/cm^2, Rm = 10 kOhm.cm^2 in the deposited model). Kenyon cells, MBONs, EPG/Delta7 and descending neurons have no comparable published parameter table that I could verify.
- Synaptic kinetics: the decay time constants of nAChR EPSCs, Rdl (GABA-A) IPSCs, GluCl IPSCs and GABA-B effects in Drosophila central neurons. Specific numbers could not be verified against primary sources when this note was written. These four numbers are load-bearing for any continuous-time model and should be the first thing the next phase looks up.
- How large is the gap-junction graph relative to the chemical graph, and where? There is a CNS-wide innexin expression map but, to my knowledge, no quantitative electrical connectivity matrix for the central brain. Without it, the Laplacian coupling term has no prior.
- Do short-term plasticity parameters (U, tau_D, tau_F) vary systematically by cell-type pair in the fly, and can they be predicted from the connectome (e.g. from bouton size or active-zone count)? If yes, the connectome would supply the fast-weight dynamics, which is a far stronger claim than supplying the static mask and is the one thing phase 1 did not test.
- Is there any benchmark, in any regime, where a connectome-constrained or biophysically detailed model has beaten a matched conventional model on a sequence task? The visual-system result (Lappalainen et al. 2024) is a prediction-of-neural-activity claim, not a task-performance claim. If the answer is no anywhere, that should be stated explicitly in the next phase's pre-registration as the prior.
- What is the correct token-to-time mapping? The fly's ladder is in seconds; a language model's is in tokens. Every 'tau ~ 250 ms' or 'tau ~ 10 s' number above needs a declared, defended conversion before it becomes a hyperparameter, and the choice of conversion is itself a free parameter that must be pre-registered rather than tuned.
- Exact per-synapse accuracy of the EM neurotransmitter classifier (Eckstein et al. 2024, Cell) and its per-cell-type error structure. The whole sign structure of the model rests on it and the figure could not be verified when this note was written.
- Does the fly's ~4 Hz mean-rate bound have a direct analogue for a model trained on GPUs, or is it an artefact of the energy cost of ion pumping that has no digital counterpart? If the latter, the conditional-computation argument loses its biological warrant and becomes an ordinary efficiency argument.

### Relevance to language models

"Nothing in fly neuron or synapse biophysics predicts a better next-token model per FLOP than a transformer, and no published result supports such a claim. What the biology does give is four differentiable, GPU-friendly primitives with a real mathematical basis, plus one honest performance regime.\n\nThe primitives. (1) Conductance-based units, V = (g0 E0 + sum g_i E_i)/(g0 + sum g_i): division and normalisation are intrinsic, inhibition is shunting rather than subtractive, and the total conductance is a learnable precision variable. This is what the antennal lobe measurably computes (n ~ 1.5 divisive normalisation compressing a 57x input range to 6.6x) and it is Bayes-optimal cue combination. It is an unexplored replacement for LayerNorm-plus-softmax gating. (2) Subunit neurons: several semi-independent dendritic compartments with LOCAL normalisation pooled at one output, warranted by the 50 um APL space constant and by PN branch attenuation, and equivalent to a block-diagonal two-layer model. (3) Tsodyks-Markram short-term plasticity with a fixed ladder of time constants (300-400 ms fast depression, ~10 s slow depression, facilitation): algebraically this IS a gated linear recurrence, so it lands exactly on the primitive family that is currently state of the art in efficient sequence modelling (Mamba, RetNet, Gated DeltaNet) rather than fighting it. (4) Parameter tying by CELL TYPE rather than by neuron: 11,770 types for 166,700 neurons is the fly's own weight-sharing scheme, it is the form in which connectome structure survived in the visual system (Lappalainen et al. 2024), and it is the one connectome hypothesis this project has never tested - phase 1 tested the raw binary mask, not a type-tied parameterisation.\n\nThe regime. The fly's whole-brain mean firing rate is bounded at about 4 Hz by a ~5.1e12 ATP/s budget. The biological principle is that almost nothing updates on any tick. Translated honestly, that is conditional computation, and it defines a measurable regime in which a state-of-the-art claim is possible without pretending: quality per FLOP and quality per joule at matched quality, plus continual adaptation under distribution shift with a modulator-set learning rate. That is where to aim.\n\nAnd the negative: do not build it as a spiking network. On this evidence spiking would be less faithful to the fly than a graded model, and strictly worse to train."


## Genome, development and innateness in Drosophila: what ~13,000 genes can and cannot specify, with description-length and developmental-noise measurements on MaleCNS v1.0

*Key: `genome-development`.*

### Summary

The genomic-bottleneck story is true for mammals and much weaker for the fly. Measured on MaleCNS v1.0 (script committed at scripts/genome_bottleneck.py): the Shannon description length of the connectome's topology is 16.85 MB (1.348e8 bits, 808 bits/neuron), against a raw genome of 36-45 MB. The ratio is ~2-2.7x, not the ~10^5-10^6 Zador (2019, Nat Commun 10:3770) computes for humans. There is no extreme compression algorithm here to discover.

What the genome does supply is measurable. (a) REPETITION: 42% of neurons live in 43 cell types with >=800 members; the optic lobe is 53.6% of the brain in 272 types tiled over ~800 columns. This is weight sharing, which ML already exploits. (b) A FACTORISED TYPE CODE: 202 Ito-Lee hemilineages x ~31 types each reproduces the 5,634 observed types to 98%, at ~12.7 bits/neuron versus 13.5 for a free label - the saving is not in bits but in moving the cost from a table to a short developmental cascade (Isshiki 2001 Cell 106:511; Li 2013 Nature 498:456; Truman 2010 Development 137:53). (c) A BLOCK-STRUCTURED SIGN FIELD: hemilineage removes 84.7% of neurotransmitter entropy (H 1.446 -> 0.221 bits), confirming Lacin et al. 2019 (eLife 8:e43701) on new data. (d) SCALARS THAT ARE PROVABLY NEAR-OPTIMAL: KC fan-in 5.07-5.16 against the ~6-7 derived by Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 (Neuron 93:1153).

What the genome does NOT supply is the graph. Within one animal, left and right copies of the same cell type share only 52% of type->type edges, rising to 68% for strong ones (cosine of contact-weighted profiles 0.930, so the moments are tight and the realisation is not). PN->KC wiring differs between hemispheres of the same larva (Eichler 2017 Nature 548:175) and is random with a biased prior (Caron 2013 Nature 497:113). A type-level rule compresses the connectome only 1.6-2.4x and leaves 5.6-6.9 bits per edge; naive low-rank g-network fits did worse than the free table at every rank. Phase 1's nulls were the correct biological answer.

Three usable outputs: block Dale as a cheap untested prior; a measured <1% plasticity budget (KC->MBON = 0.34% of CNS edges) for the phase-3 lifetime-learning thesis; and description length, not FLOPs, as the benchmark axis where a fly-aligned design has a defensible SOTA target.

### Established (with sources)

- GENOME SIZE. Drosophila melanogaster has a ~180 Mb genome (~143.7 Mb assembled euchromatin, Release 6) encoding ~13,600-14,000 protein-coding genes (Adams et al. 2000, Science 287:2185). Read as raw sequence that is 2.9-3.6e8 bits = 36-45 MB. [current gene count from FlyBase; exact figure UNCERTAIN]
- THE GENOMIC BOTTLENECK ARGUMENT (Zador 2019, Nature Communications 10:3770, 'A critique of pure learning and what artificial neural networks can learn from animal brains'): animals are born with substantial innate competence; the genome cannot store a synapse-by-synapse blueprint (human ~10^9 bytes of genome vs ~10^14-10^15 synapses, ratio ~10^5-10^6); therefore the genome encodes wiring RULES, and the bottleneck acts as a regularizer. Sound for mammals. MEASURED HERE IT IS MUCH WEAKER FOR THE FLY: the Shannon description length of MaleCNS v1.0 topology is 1.348e8 bits = 16.85 MB, only ~2-2.7x smaller than the raw fly genome, versus ~10^5-10^6 for humans. The fly genome is NOT under severe pressure to compress its own connectome. (Script: scripts/genome_bottleneck.py, block 2.)
- COMPRESSION BY CELL TYPE IS MODEST (measured here, MaleCNS v1.0). Knowing both endpoints' cell type plus a free density per type pair (6,076 types with >=3 neurons, 93.4% of neurons, 7.06M edges) leaves 6.10 MB residual and costs 0.87 MB for the table: total 6.97 MB vs 16.85 MB raw = 2.42x compression, 6.91 bits/edge still unexplained. At full 11,752-type granularity: residual 7.37 MB + 3.02 MB table = 10.39 MB = 1.62x, 5.60 bits/edge unexplained. Degree sequence alone leaves 14.02 MB. So cell type explains far more than degree, but roughly two-thirds of the connectome's description length is NOT explained by a type-level rule.
- DEVELOPMENTAL NOISE FLOOR (measured here, the single most consequential number in this brief). Within ONE animal, comparing the left and right copies of the same cell type (5,188 types with >=2 neurons per side): the contact-weighted type-level output profile is well conserved (median cosine 0.930, IQR [0.860, 0.970]; mismatched-type control median 0.000; Pearson r of log1p(contacts per source neuron) = 0.753), but the BINARY type->type edge set is not: Jaccard(L,R) = 0.523 at >0 contacts per source neuron, rising only to 0.680 at >50. About a third of even the STRONG type-level connections are not reproduced on the other side of the same brain. The genome specifies a distribution over graphs whose type-level moments are tight; the edge-level realisation is substantially stochastic.
- HEMILINEAGE DETERMINES NEUROTRANSMITTER: DALE'S LAW IS BLOCK-STRUCTURED (measured here; confirms Lacin et al. 2019, eLife 8:e43701, 'Neurotransmitter identity is acquired in a lineage-restricted manner in the Drosophila CNS' [venue/number moderately confident]). On the 33,830 MaleCNS neurons with an Ito-Lee hemilineage label (202 hemilineages, excluding 'putative_primary'): H(NT) = 1.446 bits, H(NT | hemilineage) = 0.221 bits, so the hemilineage removes 84.7% of transmitter entropy; 164/202 (81%) hemilineages are >=90% one transmitter. Truman hemilineages (76): 78.1% removed, 61/76 (80%) pure. Cell types essentially never span hemilineages (2.2% Ito-Lee, 0.2% Truman). Types per hemilineage: mean 31.0 (Ito-Lee), 60.1 (Truman).
- CELL-TYPE IDENTITY IS A FACTORISED DEVELOPMENTAL CODE. Drosophila neurons arise from ~100 neuroblasts per central-brain hemisphere; each ganglion mother cell divides once into a Notch-ON and a Notch-OFF daughter, defining two hemilineages (Truman et al. 2010, Development 137:53; Harris, Pfeiffer, Rubin & Truman 2015, eLife 4:e04493, 'Neuron hemilineages provide the functional ground plan for the Drosophila ventral nervous system'). Within a lineage, neuroblasts sequentially express temporal transcription factors (VNC: Hunchback -> Kruppel -> Pdm -> Castor, Isshiki et al. 2001 Cell 106:511; medulla: Hth -> Ey -> Slp -> D -> Tll, Li et al. 2013 Nature 498:456). MEASURED HERE: 202 hemilineages x mean 31 types each = 6,262, against 5,634 distinct types observed among those same neurons, with 98% of types confined to one hemilineage. Type identity is (hemilineage, within-lineage slot) to ~98% accuracy: ~7.7 + ~5 = ~12.7 bits, versus log2(11,752) = 13.5 bits for a free label.
- WIRING SPECIFICITY IS COMBINATORIAL CELL-SURFACE CODE PLUS TIMING AND POSITION, NOT A UNIQUE MOLECULAR ADDRESS. The Dpr/DIP immunoglobulin-superfamily interactome (21 Dprs, 11 DIPs) was mapped by Ozkan et al. 2013 (Cell 154:228) and shown to control synaptic connectivity by Carrillo et al. 2015 (Cell 163:1770) and Tan et al. 2015 (Cell 163:1756); specificity determinants in Cosmanescu et al. 2018 (Neuron 100:1385). Teneurins instruct olfactory partner matching (Hong, Mosca & Luo 2012, Nature 484:201); Toll receptors do likewise (Ward, Hong, Favaloro & Luo 2015, Neuron 85:1013). Review: Sanes & Zipursky 2020, Cell 181:536. CRUCIAL COUNTING ARGUMENT: these families number in the tens, not the thousands, against ~11,750 cell types, so no molecule-per-type addressing is possible. [Dpr/DIP counts confident; specific paper page numbers moderately confident]
- DSCAM1 DIVERSITY IS SELF-AVOIDANCE, NOT ADDRESSING. Drosophila Dscam1 generates up to 38,016 isoforms by mutually exclusive alternative splicing (Schmucker et al. 2000, Cell 101:671). The isoform is chosen STOCHASTICALLY per neuron and functions as a self/non-self tag for neurite self-avoidance; thousands of isoforms are required for robust discrimination, but the identity of the isoform does not specify a target (Hattori et al. 2009, Nature; Zipursky & Sanes 2010, Cell 143:343, 'Chemoaffinity revisited: Dscams, protocadherins, and neural circuit assembly'). Anyone citing Dscam as evidence for a 'molecular address code' has the biology backwards.
- WIRING IS LARGELY ACTIVITY-INDEPENDENT IN THE FLY. Synaptic partner choice in the Drosophila visual map is prespecified without neural activity (Hiesinger et al. 2006, Current Biology 16:1835, 'Activity-independent prespecification of synaptic partners in the visual map of Drosophila'). Patterned but stimulus-INDEPENDENT activity does occur during synaptogenesis and shapes some connections (Akin, Bajar, Keles, Frye & Zipursky 2019, Neuron 101:894). Flies raised in the dark still build functional visual circuits. This is the opposite of the mammalian pattern and means the fly's 'innate' network is genuinely available at t=0. [Hiesinger 2006 details moderately confident]
- THE EXPANSION LAYER IS EXPLICITLY RANDOM, AND RANDOMNESS IS THE POINT. Adult PN->Kenyon-cell connectivity is consistent with random sampling of ~6 glomeruli per KC with unequal per-glomerulus probability and no stereotypy across animals (Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel 2013, Nature 497:113). In the larva, the complete MB connectome shows PN->KC assignment differing between the LEFT and RIGHT hemispheres of the SAME animal (Eichler et al. 2017, Nature 548:175, 'The complete connectome of a learning and memory centre in an insect brain'). CONTRARY EVIDENCE: Zheng et al. 2022 (Current Biology 32:3334, 'Structured sampling of olfactory input by the fly mushroom body') report reproducible departures from uniform randomness (food-related glomeruli over-represented, non-random co-occurrence). Both can be true: the sampling PRIOR is genetic, the SAMPLE is not. This repo's own H1d null (real PN->KC = degree-preserving shuffle, -0.1 seed-SD) is exactly what Caron/Eichler predict.
- THE GENOME SPECIFIES SCALARS THAT ARE PROVABLY NEAR-OPTIMAL. Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott 2017 (Neuron 93:1153, 'Optimal degrees of synaptic connectivity') derive the in-degree that maximises the dimension of a cerebellum-like expanded representation; the optimum is ~6-7 for the fly's parameters and is insensitive to the number of expansion units. MaleCNS measurement in this repo: KC fan-in over PN types = 5.16 (L) / 5.07 (R). The genome needs one small integer, not a 120 x 2,000 matrix. Related: Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha 2017 (Science 358:793) show the same circuit implements locality-sensitive hashing; Muscinelli, Wagner & Litwin-Kumar 2023 (Nature Neuroscience) show 'structured compression followed by random expansion' is the efficient architecture.
- LEARNING IS A SMALL, TARGETED MODIFICATION OF A FIXED INNATE CIRCUIT. Associative olfactory learning in the adult fly is implemented as dopamine-gated DEPRESSION of the KC->MBON synapses of the trained odour, in one compartment, leaving the rest of the circuit and the parallel innate lateral-horn pathway untouched (Hige, Aso, Modi, Rubin & Turner 2015, Neuron 88:985, 'Heterosynaptic plasticity underlies aversive olfactory learning in Drosophila'; architecture in Aso et al. 2014, eLife 3:e04577). MEASURED IN THIS REPO's verified circuit report: KC->MBON edges number 17,931 (L) + 17,985 (R) = 35,916 of 10,520,431 CNS edges = 0.34%. All MB-internal edges together are <2%. The canonical site of lifetime learning is ~0.3% of the connectome.
- DEVELOPMENTAL VARIABILITY PRODUCES BEHAVIOURAL INDIVIDUALITY, AND IT IS NOT GENETIC. Isogenic flies raised identically show stable individual visual-orientation biases traceable to asymmetric wiring of an identified optic-lobe microcircuit (Linneweber et al. 2020, Science 367:1112, 'A neurodevelopmental origin of behavioral individuality in the Drosophila visual system'). Kenyon-cell odour responses are idiosyncratic across individuals and predict individual odour preference (Honegger, Smith, Churgin, Turner & de Bivort 2020, PNAS 117:23292). The genome underdetermines the brain on purpose, or at least tolerably. [both moderately confident on exact volume/page]
- CRITICAL PERIODS EXIST IN FLIES AND CLOSE ACTIVELY. A larval motor circuit has a defined critical period that astrocytes actively close (Ackerman, Perez-Catalan, Freeman & Doe 2021, Nature 592:414). Early-adult odour exposure enlarges specific antennal-lobe glomeruli (Devaud, Acebes & Ferrus 2001, J Neuroscience 21:6274); dark rearing in the first days after eclosion changes lamina synapse numbers (Barth, Hirsch, Meinertzhagen & Heisenberg 1997, J Neuroscience 17:1493). [Ackerman 2021 confident; the two older ones moderately confident]
- STRUCTURAL PLASTICITY IN THE ADULT MUSHROOM BODY IS REAL BUT MODEST, AND THERE IS ESSENTIALLY NO ADULT NEUROGENESIS. MB volume/fibre number varies with age, sex and experience (Technau 1984/2007, J Neurogenetics; Heisenberg, Heusipp & Wanke 1995, J Neuroscience 15:1951, 'Structural plasticity in the Drosophila brain'). Adult neurogenesis in the central brain is absent or injury-induced and tiny (Fernandez-Hernandez et al. 2013, Cell Reports). So the adult fly's lifetime learning happens in a FIXED cell population by synaptic-strength and bouton-level change, not by growing new units. [all three moderately confident; the older MB-volume claims have been partly reinterpreted]
- ML PRECEDENTS FOR COMPRESSED/INDIRECT WEIGHT ENCODING. HyperNEAT (Stanley, D'Ambrosio & Gauci 2009, Artificial Life 15:185) generates connectivity from a small function of geometric coordinates. HyperNetworks (Ha, Dai & Le 2017, ICLR, arXiv:1609.09106) generate one network's weights from another, smaller one. Koulakov, Shuvaev, Lachi & Zador, 'Encoding innate ability through a genomic bottleneck' (bioRxiv 2021, doi 10.1101/2021.03.16.435261; a journal version appeared later [VENUE AND YEAR UNCERTAIN]) train a small 'genomic network' mapping (neuron-type i, neuron-type j) -> weight and report large compression with retained or improved generalisation. Barabasi & Barabasi 2020 (Neuron 105:435, 'A genetic model of the connectome') and Kovacs, Barabasi & Barabasi 2020 (PNAS 117:33570) fit C. elegans connectivity from gene-expression rules; Barabasi, Beynon, Katona & Perez-Nieves, 'Complex computation from developmental priors' (Nature Communications 2023 [UNCERTAIN]) evolve developmental encodings. Gaier & Ha 2019 (NeurIPS, 'Weight Agnostic Neural Networks', arXiv:1906.04358) is the purest 'innate architecture' result: searched topologies perform far above chance with a single shared random weight.
- THE COUNTER-LINEAGE IN ML: STRUCTURE FIXED AT INITIALISATION UNDERPERFORMS AT SCALE. Lottery-ticket masks identified at initialisation fail on ResNet-50/ImageNet; matching subnetworks exist only after rewinding to early training (Frankle & Carbin 2019 ICLR; Frankle et al. 2020, arXiv:1912.05671). Static random masks sit ~4.5-7 pp below RigL at equal FLOPs on ImageNet (Evci et al. 2020 ICML; verified in this repo's docs/aif/LITERATURE.md). Which connections exist must co-adapt with the weights. This is the strongest single argument against 'freeze a genome-derived structure and train inside it'.
- ALGORITHMIC GROWTH, NOT DECOMPRESSION. Hiesinger 2021 ('The Self-Assembling Brain: How Neural Networks Grow Smarter', Princeton University Press) argues the genome is not a compressed blueprint but a PROGRAM that must be run in time, with information generated during the run by local interactions; there is no shortcut from genome to connectome, and the genome/connectome bit ratio is therefore the wrong quantity. The MaleCNS measurements here support this: the type-level rule is cheap (0.87-3.02 MB) but leaves 6-7 MB of residual that the bilateral comparison shows is largely not reproducible, i.e. it is run-time noise, not stored information.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The genome's dominant compression trick is weight sharing over a repeated lattice, not a clever wiring code. | MEASURED on MaleCNS v1.0: 42.0% of all 166,700 neurons live in just 43 cell types with >=800 members; 58.4% in 115 types with >=200; 81.6% in 1,861 types with >=8. The optic lobe (ol_intrinsic, 89,403 neurons = 53.6% of the CNS) is only 272 types, mean 328.7 neurons/type, max 2,054 - i.e. ~270 types tiled over ~800 retinotopic columns. The central brain is the opposite: 32,164 cb_intrinsic neurons in 6,606 types (median 2 per type). A genome that specifies a motif plus a tiling rule pays O(types) not O(neurons). | This is convolution / parameter tying, and ML already exploits it fully (shared transformer blocks, position-invariant attention, tied embeddings). Re-deriving it from the fly buys nothing. The actionable inverse is the CENTRAL-BRAIN half: ~6,600 types at ~2-5 neurons each, i.e. thousands of singleton, non-shared, heterogeneous units. A fly-aligned LM would have a large tied/columnar trunk plus a small heterogeneous, untied 'central-brain' module of a few thousand distinct units. That asymmetry is not standard in transformers and is cheap to test. | established |
| Excitatory/inhibitory sign is a block-level developmental label, not a per-unit free parameter. | MEASURED: H(NT) = 1.446 bits over all neurons; H(NT / Ito-Lee hemilineage) = 0.221 bits, so a 202-way developmental label (~7.7 bits, paid once) removes 84.7% of the transmitter entropy across 33,830 neurons; 81% of hemilineages are >=90% one transmitter; cell types span hemilineages only 2.2% of the time. Confirms Lacin et al. 2019 (eLife 8:e43701) on a dataset they did not use. Description-length consequence: the sign field of 166,700 neurons costs ~202 x 2 bits, not 166,700 bits - a ~400x compression of that field alone. | BLOCK DALE: partition hidden units into G ~ 100-300 groups, assign one sign per group, share it. Compare against per-unit Dale (what phase 2 used) and against unconstrained signs, at matched parameters. Cheap, pre-registerable, and it changes the geometry of the recurrent Jacobian (block-signed matrices have different spectral structure from element-signed ones). Honest prior: a small effect, but it is the one structural fact in this brief that is measured, cheap, and untested. | promising |
| Cell-type identity is a factorised (lineage x birth-order x Notch) code, so the genome pays for a cascade, not a lookup table. | Neuroblast identity (spatial) x temporal transcription-factor cascade (Hunchback->Kruppel->Pdm->Castor, Isshiki 2001; Hth->Ey->Slp->D->Tll, Li 2013) x Notch ON/OFF (Truman 2010). MEASURED: 202 hemilineages x mean 31 types each = 6,262 vs 5,634 observed types among those neurons; 98% of types confined to one hemilineage. Type identity = (hemilineage ~7.7 bits, within-lineage slot ~5 bits) ~ 12.7 bits, against 13.5 bits for a free 11,752-way label - the factorisation saves almost no bits, but it moves the cost from STORAGE to a short PROGRAM that assigns labels as a by-product of division order. | Generate unit identities from a product code rather than a free embedding table: id(i) = (group g(i), slot s(i)), with the unit's parameters produced by a small function f(e_g, e_s) - a tensor-product / factorised hypernetwork. Concretely: replace a d x N per-unit parameter bank with e_G (G x r) and e_S (S x r) and a shared decoder. This is the one place where a hypernetwork is biologically warranted AND cheap. Caveat below: the measured bit saving is ~nil, so the case for it is inductive bias and reuse, not compression. | promising |
| The genome specifies a DISTRIBUTION over graphs; the realised graph is a sample, and a third of even the strong connections are not reproduced. | MEASURED within one animal (left vs right copies of 5,188 cell types): contact-weighted type-level output profiles agree (median cosine 0.930) but binary type->type edge sets agree at Jaccard 0.523 (>0 contacts/source neuron) rising only to 0.680 (>50). Pearson r of log1p(contacts/source neuron) = 0.753. Independently: PN->KC assignment differs between hemispheres of the same larva (Eichler 2017, Nature 548:175); isogenic flies have individual wiring and individual behaviour (Linneweber 2020, Science 367:1112; Honegger 2020, PNAS 117:23292). MATHEMATICALLY: the connectome is one draw from a low-parameter generative model whose sufficient statistics are type-pair densities and fan-ins. | (a) THIS EXPLAINS PHASE 1's NULLS AND RETIRES THE QUESTION: if half the fly's own type-level edges are not reproduced bilaterally, no result can depend on the specific edges, and 'real graph = degree-preserving shuffle' is the biologically PREDICTED outcome, not a disappointment. Any future claim of the form 'the specific wiring matters' is bounded above by Jaccard ~0.5-0.68. (b) CONSTRUCTIVE VERSION: extract a ~10^6-parameter generative model (type-pair densities, fan-in, sign-by-group) instead of a 10^7-edge matrix, and RESAMPLE the sparse mask from it during training so the weights must be robust to the sample. Controls that must be beaten: a fixed random mask of the same density, RigL (Evci 2020), dense. Honest prior: low - this is adjacent to DropConnect and dynamic sparse training, where static masks already lose. | established |
| Innate competence is delivered as a small number of NORMATIVELY OPTIMAL SCALARS, not as structure. | Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 (Neuron 93:1153) derive the expansion in-degree that maximises representational dimension; the optimum is ~6-7 for fly parameters and nearly independent of the number of expansion units. MaleCNS measurement in this repo: KC fan-in over PN types 5.16/5.07. Similar derivations exist for the 5% k-WTA coding level, the 30.8% inhibitory fraction, and the 8-column ring (Vilimelis Aceituno, Dall'Osto & Pisokas 2024, eLife 13:e91533, show the single-harmonic cosine ring is the most noise-resilient circulant integrator and that N=8 suffices). | Do not import matrices; DERIVE hyperparameters from an explicit objective. For a sparse memory: pick k, n, fan-in by maximising one-shot capacity at fixed per-token compute (this repo's own capacity study already gives the curve: sparse delta 100-of-2048 holds 0.70 recall at P=512 where dense delta with d_key=100 holds 0.07). This is exactly what phase 1 found - statistics transfer as hyperparameters, structure does not - and it is now explained rather than merely observed. | established |
| Lifetime learning is a targeted modification of ~0.3% of the network, in parallel with an untouched innate pathway. | KC->MBON depression is the learned change (Hige et al. 2015, Neuron 88:985); it is compartment-specific and dopamine-gated (Aso et al. 2014, eLife 3:e04577). MEASURED in this repo's verified circuit report: KC->MBON edges = 35,916 of 10,520,431 CNS edges = 0.34%; all MB-internal edges < 2%. The innate lateral-horn valence pathway runs in parallel and is not depressed by learning; ~30% of LH output neurons receive MB modulation axo-axonically, i.e. the learned signal GATES the innate one rather than replacing it (Dolan et al. 2019, eLife 43079, verified in this repo's literature digest). | Freeze the backbone; make the ONLY test-time-plastic parameters a memory of <1% of backbone size; combine its output with the frozen pathway MULTIPLICATIVELY (gain modulation), not additively. Pre-registerable hypothesis: at matched test-time FLOPs, restricting plasticity to <1% of parameters with a local modulated rule beats full test-time training (TTT / TTT-E2E) on continual adaptation and long-horizon retention while not regressing on standard perplexity. This gives the phase-3 'lifetime learning system' thesis a biological warrant AND a number to pre-register (the 1% budget). | promising |
| Wiring specificity comes from combinatorial surface codes read out in a spatiotemporal context, and the code alphabet is far too small for per-type addressing. | 21 Dprs x 11 DIPs (Ozkan 2013, Cell 154:228; Carrillo 2015, Cell 163:1770; Tan 2015, Cell 163:1756), ~14 Beat x ~8 Side, 2 Teneurins (Hong 2012, Nature 484:201), a handful of Tolls (Ward 2015, Neuron 85:1013) - tens of molecules against ~11,750 cell types. Dscam1's 38,016 isoforms are a stochastic SELF tag for self-avoidance, not an address (Schmucker 2000, Cell 101:671; Zipursky & Sanes 2010, Cell 143:343). Specificity therefore = (small combinatorial code) x (arbor position and timing) x (stochastic completion). | The ML analogue of a surface code is a LOW-DIMENSIONAL KEY per unit with connection probability a function of key similarity: p(i->j) = sigma(<u_{g(i)}, v_{g(j)}> + a + b). MEASURED HERE, THE NAIVE VERSION FAILS (see dead_ends): the 6,076 x 6,076 type-connectivity matrix has effective rank in the low hundreds (centred log-density Frobenius energy: rank 1 = 12.6%, rank 16 = 43.4%, rank 64 = 72.8%, rank 128 = 88.9%, rank 192 = 98.3%) and no rank-r SVD fit beat the free table or even reliably beat the global-density baseline. A key dimension of ~100-200 per type would be needed, which is not a bottleneck at all. | established |
| The right benchmark for a genomic-bottleneck design is description length, not FLOPs - and that is a defined regime where a SOTA claim is possible without pretending. | The genomic bottleneck IS a minimum-description-length principle: minimise /program/ subject to competence. Delet(a)ng et al. 2024 (ICLR, 'Language Modeling Is Compression', arXiv:2309.10668) showed Chinchilla 70B compresses enwik9 to ~8.3% of raw - far better than gzip/LZMA - but only when the model's own parameters are NOT counted; including them it loses badly. That gap is exactly what an innate-prior architecture is for. The Hutter Prize / Large Text Compression Benchmark (Mahoney) scores the SELF-EXTRACTING ARCHIVE: compressed data PLUS decompressor, i.e. total description length, on enwik8/enwik9. [Current record holders are neural (nncp by Bellard, cmix by Knoll); EXACT CURRENT NUMBERS UNCERTAIN - verify before pre-registering.] | Define the phase-3 claim in a regime the field does not contest hard and that follows from the biology: 'lowest bits/char on held-out text at a fixed TOTAL description length (compressed model program + optimizer/decoder needed to reproduce it)'. A design whose weights are generated by a small factorised program, tiled over a repeated trunk, with a <1% plastic memory, is structurally advantaged there and nowhere else. This is pre-registerable, adversarially benchmarked, and honest. It is the strongest single recommendation in this brief. | promising |
| Critical periods = a plasticity schedule that is actively closed, not passively annealed. | Astrocytes actively terminate a larval Drosophila motor-circuit critical period (Ackerman, Perez-Catalan, Freeman & Doe 2021, Nature 592:414); early-adult odour exposure enlarges specific glomeruli (Devaud 2001, J Neurosci 21:6274); early dark rearing changes lamina synapse counts (Barth 1997, J Neurosci 17:1493). In deep networks the phenomenon has an established analogue: Achille, Rovere & Soatto 2019 (ICLR, 'Critical learning periods in deep networks') show early data deficits cause permanent deficits, tied to the Fisher-information trajectory. | Schedule STRUCTURAL plasticity, not just the learning rate: allow mask rewiring and per-group gain changes for the first fraction of training, then lock them and train only weights; the lock should be triggered by a measured quantity (e.g. Fisher trace plateau), not a step count. Low novelty - this is close to dynamic-sparse-training schedules and LR annealing - but it is cheap and it is the correct biological reading. | promising |
| Evolution is the outer loop and it meta-learns the PLASTICITY RULE, not the weights. | The fly is born competent with no offline training set; what the genome supplies is a network plus a local, neuromodulator-gated learning rule (dopamine-gated depression at KC->MBON with compartment-specific valence and timescale). Nothing in the fly corresponds to gradient descent on a corpus at the lifetime scale. | Established ML lineage to treat as the CONTROL SET, not as novelty: differentiable plasticity (Miconi, Stanley & Clune 2018, ICML) and Backpropamine (Miconi et al. 2019, ICLR) - explicitly neuromodulated Hebbian fast weights trained by backprop, the direct ancestor of any 'dopamine-gated fast weight' claim; meta-learned update rules (Metz et al. 2019, ICLR); Hebbian plasticity in random networks (Najarro & Risi 2020, NeurIPS); OML (Javed & White 2019, NeurIPS) and ANML (Beaulieu et al. 2020, ECAI). A phase-3 design that does not name and beat Backpropamine and a learned gate will repeat phase 1's H1b result (a learned gate beat the error gate, -2.6 seed-SD). | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- MaleCNS v1.0 (measured, this repo): N = 166,700 neurons; E = 10,520,431 directed edges at >=3 synaptic contacts; density 3.786e-4; 11,751 distinct cell-type labels over 164,506 annotated neurons (2,194 untyped); neurons per type mean 14.2, median 3, max 3,377.
- Repetition / weight sharing (measured): neurons in types with >=8 members 135,979 (81.6%) in 1,861 types; >=50 members 114,654 (68.8%) in 295 types; >=200 members 97,314 (58.4%) in 115 types; >=500 members 79,736 (47.8%) in 59 types; >=800 members 70,026 (42.0%) in 43 types.
- Superclass asymmetry (measured): ol_intrinsic 89,403 neurons in 272 types (median 66, mean 328.7, max 2,054); visual_projection 9,201 in 346 types (median 3); cb_intrinsic 32,164 in 6,606 types (median 2, mean 4.9); vnc_intrinsic 13,161 in 2,800 types (median 4).
- Description length of the topology (measured): raw uniform-ER code at matched density = 1.348e8 bits = 16.85 MB = 808 bits/neuron. Residual given the degree sequence (Chung-Lu, 4e6-sample Monte Carlo) = 14.02 MB. Residual given a free density per type pair (T = 6,076 types with >=3 neurons, 93.4% of neurons, 7.06M edges, 439,012 non-empty blocks = 1.19% of T^2) = 6.10 MB (6.91 bits/edge) plus 0.87 MB for the table = 6.97 MB total = 2.42x compression. Single global density alone = 11.64 MB. Per-neuron type label = 13.5 bits = 0.28 MB for all 166,700.
- Description length at full type granularity (measured, T = 11,752): residual 7.37 MB (5.60 bits/edge), table cost 3.02 MB (145 bits/neuron), total 10.39 MB = 1.62x compression vs 16.85 MB raw; 1,517,066 non-empty type-pair blocks of 138,109,504 (1.0985%).
- Effective rank of the type x type connectivity (measured): cumulative Frobenius energy of the centred log-density matrix = 12.6% at rank 1, 23.0% at rank 4, 43.4% at rank 16, 57.5% at rank 32, 72.8% at rank 64, 88.9% at rank 128, 98.3% at rank 192. Best naive rank-r code lengths (log-density SVD with a fitted global scale): rank 64 -> 12.84 MB residual + 1.56 MB fp16 params = 14.39 MB; rank 128 -> 15.30 MB; rank 192 -> 16.36 MB. ALL WORSE than the free type-pair table (6.97 MB) and all worse than or comparable to the global-density baseline (11.64 MB).
- Hemilineage -> neurotransmitter (measured): Ito-Lee, 33,830 neurons, 202 hemilineages: H(NT) = 1.446 bits, H(NT|hemilineage) = 0.221 bits, information gain 1.224 bits = 84.7%; 164/202 (81%) hemilineages >=90% one transmitter; types per hemilineage mean 31.0, median 29, max 120; types spanning >1 hemilineage 126/5,634 (2.2%). Truman, 19,733 neurons, 76 hemilineages: gain 1.319 of 1.689 bits = 78.1%; 61/76 (80%) pure; types per hemilineage mean 60.1, median 42; types spanning >1 hemilineage 10/4,522 (0.2%).
- Neurotransmitter marginals (measured, MaleCNS predictions): acetylcholine 103,720; glutamate 29,302; GABA 22,069; histamine 7,891; unclear 2,999; dopamine 392; unknown 178; octopamine 101; serotonin 48. (Inhibitory fraction 30.8% follows from GABA + glutamate.)
- Bilateral reproducibility within one animal (measured): 5,188 cell types with >=2 neurons per side. Cosine(L, R) of the contact-weighted type-level output profile: median 0.930, IQR [0.860, 0.970], n = 4,954; mismatched-type control median 0.000. Jaccard(L, R) of the binary type->type edge set: 0.523 at >0 contacts per source neuron (|L| = 214,434 pairs), 0.589 at >2, 0.636 at >5, 0.658 at >10, 0.669 at >20, 0.680 at >50 (|L| = 7,159). Pearson r of log1p(contacts per source neuron) over union-present pairs = 0.753, n = 280,360.
- Plastic fraction (from this repo's verified circuit report, MaleCNS): KC->MBON edges 17,931 (L) + 17,985 (R) = 35,916 = 0.34% of 10,520,431 CNS edges. All MB-internal edges (PN->KC 19,815; KC->MBON 35,916; KC->KC 117,381; DAN->KC 13,389; DAN->MBON 964; MBON->DAN 639; MBON->MBON 537) ~ 188,641 = 1.79%.
- Mushroom-body constants (this repo's verified extraction, MaleCNS): 2,019 (L) / 2,045 (R) Kenyon cells per hemisphere; KC fan-in over PN types 5.16 (L) / 5.07 (R), median 5; 120/119 PNs in 60/61 types; PN fan-out to KCs 82.6/83.2; 48/49 MBONs over 32 compartment labels; 158 PAM + 8 PPL1 DANs; one GABAergic APL covering 100% / 99.95% of KCs. Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 predict optimal in-degree ~6-7 for these parameters.
- Genome vs connectome, fly: genome 180 Mb raw = 3.6e8 bits = 45 MB (or 143.7 Mb euchromatin = 2.9e8 bits = 36 MB); connectome topology description length 1.348e8 bits = 16.85 MB. Ratio ~2.1-2.7x. Human comparison (Zador 2019 framing): ~10^9 bytes genome vs ~10^14-10^15 synapses, ratio ~10^5-10^6. The fly's bottleneck is four to six orders of magnitude looser than the human's.
- Dscam1: up to 38,016 alternatively spliced isoforms (Schmucker et al. 2000, Cell 101:671) - used as a stochastic self/non-self tag, not as a target address.
- Cell-surface code alphabet: 21 Dprs x 11 DIPs (Ozkan et al. 2013, Cell 154:228); ~14 Beat x ~8 Side; 2 Teneurins; a handful of Tolls - tens of molecules against ~11,750 cell types. [Beat/Side counts moderately confident.]
- Developmental units: ~100 neuroblasts per central-brain hemisphere; each ganglion mother cell splits into Notch-ON / Notch-OFF daughters -> ~200 hemilineages; MaleCNS annotates 202 (Ito-Lee) and 76 (Truman). Temporal cascades: 4-5 factors (VNC Hb->Kr->Pdm->Cas, Isshiki 2001; medulla Hth->Ey->Slp->D->Tll, Li 2013). 202 x 31 types/hemilineage = 6,262, vs 5,634 observed types among lineage-annotated neurons.
- FlyWire comparison (for cross-checking; from this repo's literature digest and Dorkenwald et al. 2024 Nature 634:124 / Schlegel et al. 2024 Nature 634:139): adult female brain 139,255 neurons, 8,453 cell types. [Exact type count moderately confident.]
- This repo's own capacity study (docs/phase3/MEMORY_CAPACITY.md), relevant to 'the genome specifies scalars': at equal per-token compute (d_key = k), sparse delta 100-of-2048 recalls 0.97/0.95/0.92/0.82/0.70 at P = 32/64/128/256/512 stored pairs; dense delta with d_key = 100 recalls 0.88/0.72/0.40/0.17/0.07.

### Dead ends

- THE CONNECTOME AS A FROZEN WEIGHT MASK OR STRUCTURE PRIOR IS CLOSED, AND THE BIOLOGY NOW EXPLAINS WHY. This repo's H5 found real = degree-preserving shuffle = Erdos-Renyi at N = 256/512/1024 (0 of 3 sizes beyond threshold); H1d found the real PN->KC pattern = shuffle (-0.1 seed-SD). The reason measured here: within ONE fly, only 52-68% of type->type edges are reproduced between hemispheres (Jaccard 0.523 at >0 contacts, 0.680 at >50). Half the graph is developmental noise. Independent replication exists (cqw-acq/connectome-null-models: larval connectome vs degree-preserving null on CIFAR-10, +0.09 pp, 95% CI [-0.20, +0.38], n = 15, in this repo's literature digest). DO NOT RE-RUN THIS QUESTION IN ANY FORM.
- NAIVE LOW-RANK / BILINEAR 'g-NETWORK' FACTORISATION OF THE TYPE MATRIX DOES NOT COMPRESS THE FLY CONNECTOME. Measured here: randomized SVD of the 6,076 x 6,076 type connectivity in log-density space (and in logit space, with a fitted global scale) gives, at every rank tried, a total code length WORSE than the free type-pair table (6.97 MB) and no better than the single-global-density baseline (11.64 MB): rank 64 -> 14.39 MB, rank 128 -> 15.30 MB, rank 192 -> 16.36 MB. The Frobenius spectrum needs rank ~128-192 for 89-98% of energy, i.e. a ~150-dimensional embedding per type, which is not a bottleneck. CAVEAT, STATED HONESTLY: these are NAIVE SVD fits, not regularized binomial matrix factorisation and not a nonlinear g-network, so this REFUTES THE NAIVE METHOD, not the Koulakov hypothesis. But it removes the free lunch: do not assume the fly's type graph is low rank.
- MOLECULAR ADDRESS CODES. There is no per-type molecular address in the fly. The cell-surface families number in the tens (21 Dpr x 11 DIP, ~14 Beat x ~8 Side, 2 Teneurins) against ~11,750 types, and Dscam1's 38,016 isoforms are a stochastic self-avoidance tag, not a target label (Zipursky & Sanes 2010, Cell 143:343). Any design premised on 'each neuron type has a genetically specified partner list' is biologically wrong.
- STRUCTURE FIXED AT INITIALISATION UNDERPERFORMS LEARNED STRUCTURE AT SCALE. Lottery-ticket masks found at init fail on ResNet-50/ImageNet (Frankle et al. 2020, arXiv:1912.05671); static random masks are ~4.5-7 pp below RigL at equal FLOPs (Evci et al. 2020, ICML). Whatever the genome contributes, the ML lesson is that which connections exist must co-adapt with the weights. A 'genome-derived frozen architecture' claim starts from behind.
- ARCHITECTURE SEARCH UNDER A SMALL DESCRIPTION LENGTH AS A ROUTE TO A LANGUAGE-MODEL SOTA. Weight Agnostic Neural Networks (Gaier & Ha 2019, NeurIPS, arXiv:1906.04358) is the cleanest 'innate architecture' result and it operates on tiny RL tasks and MNIST; HyperNEAT (Stanley et al. 2009) likewise. No indirect-encoding / developmental-encoding method has produced a competitive language model. Budgeting GPU time for evolutionary architecture search here is a predictable loss.
- RE-DERIVING CONVOLUTION / PARAMETER TYING FROM THE OPTIC LOBE. The fly's largest compression is ~270 cell types tiled over ~800 retinotopic columns (53.6% of its neurons in 272 types). This is weight sharing, which transformers and convnets already exploit maximally. There is no win in rediscovering it, and a paper that presents it as a fly-derived insight will be correctly dismissed.
- TREATING A 'CRITICAL PERIOD' AS A NOVEL ML MECHANISM. Achille, Rovere & Soatto 2019 (ICLR) already established critical periods in deep networks, and the engineering translation (schedule the learning rate, then freeze) is standard practice. Use it as a schedule, do not claim it.
- EXPECTING THE FLY'S GENOMIC BOTTLENECK TO BE SEVERE. Measured: the fly's raw genome (36-45 MB) is only ~2-2.7x larger than the description length of its own connectome topology (16.85 MB), versus ~10^5-10^6 for humans. Arguments of the form 'the fly must have discovered an extreme compression algorithm' are not supported by the arithmetic.
