If you want the extreme-bottleneck story, the mammalian brain is the example, not the fly.

### Open questions

- Does a PROPERLY FITTED generative model of the type graph compress it? The naive SVD fits failed. The honest test is a regularized binomial/Poisson matrix factorisation (or a small nonlinear g-network in the Koulakov sense) fit by maximum likelihood with a held-out block split, scored in bits including parameters, against the free type-pair table (6.97 MB) and the global-density baseline (11.64 MB). This is a few hours of CPU and would settle the genomic-bottleneck question for this connectome definitively.
- How much of the 6.10 MB residual is explained by SPATIAL information (soma position, neuropil/ROI, arbor overlap) rather than type? Peters' rule predicts most of it. If arbor overlap plus type explains, say, 80% of the residual, then the 'genome' is a growth rule in space, and the right ML object is a geometric generative model, not a graph. The repo has 3-D soma positions for all 166,700 neurons and has never used them.
- Is the Jaccard ~0.5-0.68 bilateral floor a biological fact or a reconstruction/annotation artefact? Weak edges may be missed asymmetrically by proofreading, and the >=3-contact inclusion rule truncates. Cross-checking against FlyWire (left/right within FAFB) would separate the two. This number is load-bearing for the whole 'wiring does not transfer' conclusion, so it deserves a proper error bar.
- Does BLOCK DALE (one sign per ~200-unit group) differ measurably from per-unit Dale at matched parameters, in loss, in trainability, or in the spectrum of the recurrent Jacobian? Untested anywhere in this repo and, as far as I know, untested in the literature.
- What is the real current state of the art on the Hutter Prize / Large Text Compression Benchmark (enwik8/enwik9, self-extracting archive size), and by how much? If the leading neural compressors (nncp, cmix) are already within a few percent of what a small-program design could reach, the description-length regime is not a viable SOTA target either. THIS MUST BE VERIFIED before any such target is set.
- Which journal version of Koulakov, Shuvaev, Lachi & Zador 'Encoding innate ability through a genomic bottleneck' exists, and what compression ratio and task set does it actually report? I am confident about the bioRxiv preprint (2021, doi 10.1101/2021.03.16.435261) and the method, not about the published venue, year or numbers. Same for Barabasi, Beynon, Katona & Perez-Nieves 'Complex computation from developmental priors'.
- Is there a regime in which developmental STOCHASTICITY is functionally required rather than tolerated? Linneweber 2020 and Honegger 2020 show it produces individuality; population-level bet-hedging is the usual explanation. For a single language model there is no population, so the ML translation may be empty. Worth one theoretical pass before investing.
- Does restricting test-time plasticity to <1% of parameters (the fly's measured KC->MBON fraction) actually beat unrestricted test-time training at matched compute, or is 1% simply a constraint the fly is stuck with? This is the phase-3 thesis's crux and this brief supplies only the biological number, not the answer.
- How many bits does the lineage+temporal program actually cost? I measured that the factorised code (hemilineage, slot) reproduces type identity to 98% at ~12.7 bits versus 13.5 for a free label - almost no saving. But the genome does not store either; it stores a cascade of ~5 transcription factors and ~200 neuroblast identities. Estimating the description length of the PROGRAM rather than its output is the Hiesinger question and I do not know how to do it rigorously.
- Adult structural plasticity: what is the measured magnitude and timescale of KC->MBON bouton/active-zone remodelling with learning and with sleep in the adult fly, and does any of it exceed synaptic-weight change in functional consequence? My citations here (Technau; Heisenberg 1995) are old and partly reinterpreted; I could not verify current work.

### Relevance to language models

Four things in this brief bear directly on building a language model, and one of them is a strategic recommendation.

(1) IT CLOSES A QUESTION AND EXPLAINS A NULL. The measured bilateral reproducibility of the fly's own wiring (Jaccard 0.52 at any strength, 0.68 for strong type-level connections, within a single animal) means roughly a third to a half of the connectome is a developmental sample, not genomic information. Phase 1's finding that the real graph equals a degree-preserving shuffle is therefore the biologically PREDICTED result. No future experiment should test 'does the specific wiring help'; the ceiling is measured and it is low. What CAN transfer is the generative model: type-pair densities, fan-in scalars, sign-by-group, sparsity level. That is ~10^6 numbers, not 10^7 edges.

(2) IT GIVES ONE CHEAP, UNTESTED ARCHITECTURAL PRIOR. Dale's law in the fly is block-structured: a 202-way developmental label removes 84.7% of neurotransmitter entropy, and 81% of hemilineages are >=90% one transmitter. Every model in this repo so far assigned signs per unit. Block Dale (one sign per group of ~100-800 units, groups tied) is a one-line change with a measured biological warrant and a plausible effect on the recurrent spectrum. It is the best cost/benefit item here.

(3) IT WARRANTS THE PHASE-3 PLASTICITY BUDGET WITH A NUMBER. The fly's canonical site of associative learning is KC->MBON: 35,916 of 10,520,431 edges = 0.34% of the connectome, modified by depression, in parallel with an untouched innate pathway that the learned signal gates multiplicatively. That converts 'a frozen backbone plus a small fast memory' from a design taste into a pre-registerable constraint: plastic parameters < 1% of backbone, gain-modulatory combination, compared against unrestricted test-time training (TTT-E2E, Titans, Gated DeltaNet) at matched test-time FLOPs.

(4) IT IDENTIFIES A DEFINED REGIME WHERE A SOTA CLAIM IS POSSIBLE WITHOUT PRETENDING. The genomic bottleneck is a minimum-description-length principle. Delet(a)ng et al. 2024 (ICLR, arXiv:2309.10668) showed large LMs are superb data compressors but catastrophic once their own parameters are counted. The Hutter Prize / Large Text Compression Benchmark scores exactly the total: compressed data plus decompressor. A design whose weights are generated by a small factorised program, tiled over a repeated trunk, with a <1% plastic memory, is structurally advantaged in that metric and in no other. 'Lowest bits per character on held-out text at fixed total description length' is a real, adversarially judged, under-contested benchmark that follows from the biology rather than being retrofitted to it. VERIFY THE CURRENT RECORDS FIRST - I could not (web egress blocked).

What this brief does NOT support: hypernetworks or weight compression as a performance win (the fly's own type matrix is not low rank; effective rank ~150 over 6,076 types, and every naive rank-r code was worse than the free table); architecture search under a description-length budget as a route to a competitive LM (no indirect encoding has ever produced one); and any claim built on re-deriving weight sharing from the optic lobe, which is convolution.


## What flies can and cannot learn: the behavioural evidence and what it constrains in a fly-aligned sequence model

*Key: `behaviour-sequences`.*

### Summary

"Flies learn far more than their 140k-166k neurons suggest, but along axes almost orthogonal to language. Established: classical olfactory conditioning in one trial (Quinn 1974; Tully & Quinn 1985); appetitive learning from a single 2-minute pairing; durable memory only from SPACED training (10 cycles x 15 min rest -> 7-day protein-synthesis-dependent LTM; the same cycles massed give only ARM, Tully 1994); trace conditioning across seconds-scale gaps, not longer (Galili 2011); a sign flip in plasticity between forward and backward pairing carried by two dopamine receptors on the same synapse (Tanimoto 2004; Handler 2019); extinction as a NEW OPPOSING memory in a different compartment rather than erasure (Felsenberg 2017, 2018); reward-prediction-error learning with operant matching (Rajagopalan 2023); active, gated forgetting (Shuai 2010; Berry 2012); sleep-dependent consolidation with post-learning reactivation (Donlea 2011; Dag 2019); place learning in the central complex, not the mushroom body (Ofstad 2011); visual pattern memory outside the MB with the MB required only for CONTEXT GENERALISATION (Wolf 1998; Liu 1999); operant self-learning doubly dissociated from classical learning (Brembs & Plendl 2008); courtship conditioning and narrow social learning (Siegel & Hall 1979; Danchin 2018; Kacsoh 2015); vector navigation memory with a ring attractor whose visual anchoring is learned Hebbianly in minutes (Seelig 2015; Kim 2017; Fisher 2019; Lyu 2022); and explicit efference-copy prediction of self-generated sensory consequences (Kim 2015, 2017).\n\nThe limits are as informative. Working memory is ONE target for ~4 s (Neuser 2008). Serial order in grooming comes from a suppression hierarchy with no sequence memory (Seeds 2014); courtship is a ~3-state sticky HMM (Calhoun 2019). Chaining stops at depth-2 second-order conditioning. Nothing in the repertoire requires more than a finite-state description; there is no recursion, no symbol reuse, no compositional syntax.\n\nSo: the biology does not offer a sequence model, and phase 1 already measured that the wiring offers nothing beyond its statistics. What it offers, concretely and with mathematics attached, is a LIFETIME LEARNING SYSTEM - sparse random hashing at fan-in ~6 (provably near dimension-optimal, Litwin-Kumar 2017), a biphasic signed eligibility kernel, additive opposing compartments at multiple timescales, per-compartment local errors with no cue competition, inferred learning and forgetting rates, and spacing-gated offline consolidation. Those are the components to build, on a conventional backbone, and to test on continual adaptation, re-binding and retention against compute-matched Gated DeltaNet / Titans controls - never on perplexity per FLOP."

### Established (with sources)

- Classical olfactory conditioning is the workhorse paradigm and is robust: one training cycle (one odour paired with ~12 x 60-90 V shock pulses over 60 s in a T-maze) produces an immediate performance index up to ~0.8-0.9 that decays to near zero within ~24 h (Quinn, Harris & Benzer 1974 PNAS 71:708; Tully & Quinn 1985 J Comp Physiol A 157:263). ESTABLISHED.
- Durable memory requires SPACED repetition, not more repetition: 10 training cycles with ~15 min rest intervals produce protein-synthesis-dependent long-term memory lasting ~7 days, while the same 10 cycles massed back-to-back produce anaesthesia-resistant memory (ARM) that is cycloheximide-insensitive and shorter-lived (Tully, Preat, Boynton & Del Vecchio 1994 Cell 79:35). Spacing is gated by sustained slow oscillations in two pairs of dopaminergic neurons (Placais et al. 2012 Nat Neurosci 15:592). ESTABLISHED.
- One-trial (single-episode) learning is real: a single ~2 min odour+sugar pairing yields appetitive memory lasting hours (Tempel, Bonini, Dawson & Quinn 1983 PNAS 80:1482; Krashes & Waddell 2008 J Neurosci 28:3103). So the fly writes usable associations in ONE episode but needs spaced repetition to move them into durable storage. ESTABLISHED.
- Memory expression is gated by internal state, separately from memory storage: appetitive memory is only expressed when the fly is hungry, via NPF/dopaminergic gating of MBON output (Krashes, DasGupta, Vreede, White, Armstrong & Waddell 2009 Cell 139:416). Storage and retrieval are decoupled. ESTABLISHED.
- Trace conditioning works but only over seconds: flies form odour-shock associations across a CS-US gap, with performance falling steeply and vanishing by roughly 15-20 s of gap (Galili, Ludke, Galizia, Szyszka & Tanimoto 2011 J Neurosci 31:17383; review Dylla, Galili, Szyszka & Ludke 2013 Front Physiol 4:67). The 'trace' is carried by slow odour-evoked calcium in Kenyon cells, not by a working-memory buffer. ESTABLISHED (exact cutoff UNCERTAIN).
- The sign of plasticity flips with the sign of the CS-US interval: forward pairing (odour then shock) makes the odour aversive; backward pairing (shock then odour) makes the odour appetitive ('relief learning') (Tanimoto, Heisenberg & Gerber 2004 Nature 430:983; Yarali et al. 2008 J Exp Biol 211:2417). Mechanism: two dopamine receptors with different kinetics on the same KC->MBON synapse, Dop1R1/cAMP giving depression for forward pairing and Dop1R2/Rac-dependent signalling giving potentiation for backward pairing (Handler, Graham, Cohn, Morantte, Siliciano, Zeng, Li & Ruta 2019 Cell 178:60). ESTABLISHED. This is a biphasic, signed eligibility kernel in time.
- Extinction is NOT erasure: re-exposure to the CS without the US recruits a DIFFERENT dopaminergic compartment and writes a new, opposing memory that is summed at readout with the original, which remains intact (Felsenberg, Barnstedt, Cognigni, Lin & Waddell 2017 Nature 544:240; Felsenberg et al. 2018 Cell 175:709). Parallel opposing traces, never overwrite. ESTABLISHED.
- Memories are written and updated with cell-type-specific rules across ~20 anatomically separate mushroom-body compartments, each with its own dopaminergic neuron, its own MBON, its own valence and its own forgetting rate (Aso et al. 2014 eLife 3:e04577; Aso et al. 2014 eLife 3:e04580; Aso & Rubin 2016 eLife 5:e16135). Plasticity is heterosynaptic depression of KC->MBON (Hige, Aso, Modi, Rubin & Turner 2015 Neuron 88:985; Cohn, Morantte & Ruta 2015 Cell 163:1742). ESTABLISHED.
- Flies show reward-prediction-error learning and obey Herrnstein's matching law in a two-choice operant task: dopaminergic reward responses are reduced by expected reward via MBON->DAN feedback, and choice allocation matches relative reinforcement (Rajagopalan, Darshan, Hibbard, Fitzgerald & Turner 2023 PNAS 120:e2221415120). Novelty/familiarity is explicitly represented in one compartment (Hattori, Aso, Swartz, Rubin, Abbott & Axel 2017 Cell 169:956). The larval connectome shows the MBON->DAN recurrent loop as the dominant recurrent motif (Eschbach et al. 2020 Nat Neurosci 23:544; Winding et al. 2023 Science 379:eadd9330). ESTABLISHED.
- Operant learning is a SEPARATE system from classical learning, doubly dissociated pharmacologically: operant 'self-learning' (changing a motor bias) depends on PKC and FoxP in motor circuits and is mushroom-body-independent, while 'world-learning' (predictive stimulus value) depends on rutabaga adenylyl cyclase in the mushroom body (Wolf & Heisenberg 1991 J Comp Physiol A 169:699; Brembs & Heisenberg 2000 Learn Mem 7:104; Brembs & Plendl 2008 Curr Biol 18:1168; Mendoza et al. 2014 PLoS ONE 9:e100648). Two learning systems with different addresses. ESTABLISHED.
- Place learning exists and is central-complex-dependent, not mushroom-body-dependent: in a hot arena with one cool tile, flies learn the tile's location from distal visual landmarks, and this requires ellipsoid-body ring neurons (Ofstad, Zuker & Reiser 2011 Nature 474:204). A separate operant place paradigm (the heat box) shows spatial avoidance learning in minutes (Wustmann, Rein, Wolf & Heisenberg 1996 J Comp Physiol A 179:429). ESTABLISHED.
- Visual pattern memory in the flight simulator is acquired WITHOUT the mushroom body (Wolf et al. 1998 Learn Mem 5:166), but the mushroom body is required to GENERALISE that memory across contexts (Liu, Wolf, Ernst & Heisenberg 1999 Nature 400:753). The MB is a context/generalisation gate, not the visual memory store. ESTABLISHED (direction of the Liu 1999 effect: high confidence, not re-verified this session).
- Sequential grooming is produced by a SUPPRESSION HIERARCHY, not by a learned or chained sequence: dust simultaneously activates all body-part grooming programs; they are ranked by mutual inhibition, so the anterior-to-posterior order emerges as higher-ranked programs lose drive and release the next (Seeds, Ravbar, Chung, Hampel, Midgley, Mensh & Simpson 2014 eLife 3:e02951; command circuitry in Hampel, Franconville, Simpson & Seeds 2015 eLife 4:e08758; nested CPG control in Ravbar, Zhang & Simpson 2021 eLife, UNCERTAIN on exact reference). This is serial order with NO sequence memory. ESTABLISHED.
- Courtship song is not a stored sequence either: pulse-vs-sine mode is chosen moment-by-moment from feedback about the female (male forward velocity, distance) (Coen, Clemens, Weinstein, Pacheco, Deng & Murthy 2014 Nature 507:233), and the whole courtship behaviour is best described as a small number (~3) of persistent hidden internal states, each imposing a different sensorimotor mapping, that switch on a tens-of-seconds timescale (Calhoun, Pillow & Murthy 2019 Nat Neurosci 22:2040). ESTABLISHED. This is a sticky-HMM-gated policy, the closest thing in the fly to 'syntax'.
- Flies learn from a social/sexual experience: courtship conditioning (males rejected by mated females subsequently court less) is a well-established associative paradigm requiring the mushroom body, Orb2 for long-term courtship memory, and specific dopaminergic neurons (Siegel & Hall 1979 PNAS 76:3430; Keleman, Kruttner, Alenius & Dickson 2007 Nat Neurosci 10:1587; Keleman et al. 2012 Nature 489:145). ESTABLISHED.
- Social learning between individuals exists but is narrow: mate-choice copying with conformity that persists across chains of observers (Danchin et al. 2018 Science 362:1025) and mushroom-body-dependent social transmission of wasp-threat information (Kacsoh, Bozler, Ramaswami & Bosco 2015 eLife 4:e07423). Information transfer is about a single scalar (preference / threat), with no symbolic or combinatorial content. ESTABLISHED for the phenomena; PROMISING/contested for their generality.
- Navigation memory is vector-based and lives in the central complex: a ring attractor of EPG neurons holds heading and integrates angular velocity (Seelig & Jayaraman 2015 Nature 521:186; Kim, Rouault, Druckmann & Jayaraman 2017 Science 356:849); the mapping from an arbitrary visual scene to that heading is learned by Hebbian plasticity within MINUTES in a novel arena (Fisher, Lu, D'Alessandro & Wilson 2019 Nature 576:121; Kim, Hermundstad, Romani, Abbott & Jayaraman 2019 Nature 576:126); the fan-shaped body performs explicit vector arithmetic on sinusoidal (phasor) codes to produce allocentric travelling direction and goal vectors (Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022 Nature 601:92; Lu et al. 2022 Nature 601:98; Hulse et al. 2021 eLife 10:e66039). Flies also path-integrate idiothetically back to a food site (Kim & Dickinson 2017 Curr Biol 27:2227). ESTABLISHED.
- Flies make explicit predictions about their own sensory consequences: efference copy / corollary discharge silences or compensates visual responses during self-generated turns, quantitatively matched to the expected optic flow (Kim, Fitzgerald & Maimon 2015 Nat Neurosci 18:1247; Kim, Fenk, Lyu & Maimon 2017 Cell 168:280; Fujiwara, Cruz, Bohnslav & Chiappe 2017 Nat Neurosci 20:72). ESTABLISHED. Note this is a FORWARD MODEL of action consequences, not iterative free-energy descent on a latent state.
- Forgetting is an ACTIVE, gated process, not passive decay: Rac1 activity sets the forgetting rate (Shuai, Lu, Hu, Wang, Sun & Zhong 2010 Cell 140:579) and ongoing dopaminergic activity through the DAMB receptor actively erases memory (Berry, Cervantes-Sandoval, Nicholas & Davis 2012 Neuron 74:530). ESTABLISHED.
- Sleep consolidates memory and post-learning reactivation is the mechanism: inducing sleep after training converts weak training into long-term memory (Donlea, Thimgan, Suzuki, Gottschalk & Shaw 2011 Science 332:1571), and learning-related neurons reactivate during post-training sleep to consolidate courtship LTM (Dag, Lei, Le, Wong, Bushey & Keleman 2019 eLife 8:e42786). ESTABLISHED. This is the direct biological warrant for an offline replay/consolidation stage.
- Second-order conditioning exists: an odour associated with an already-conditioned odour acquires its valence, supported by a hierarchical dopaminergic architecture in which MBON output drives DANs of downstream compartments (Tabone & de Belle 2011 Learn Mem 18:641; Yamada, Bushey, Li, Hibbard, Sammons, Funke, Litwin-Kumar, Hige & Aso 2023 eLife 12:e79042, UNCERTAIN on exact eLife article number). This is the fly's only demonstrated CHAINING of representations. ESTABLISHED for the phenomenon.
- Visual/spatial working memory is one item for a few seconds: in the detour paradigm a fly that starts toward a target that then disappears continues toward its remembered position for about 4 s, and this requires ellipsoid-body ring neurons and rutabaga (Neuser, Triphan, Mronz, Poeck & Strauss 2008 Nature 453:1244; Kuntz, Poeck & Strauss 2017 Cell Rep, UNCERTAIN on exact volume). There is no evidence of a multi-item, order-preserving buffer anywhere in the fly. ESTABLISHED.
- There is NO evidence of compositional language, recursion, symbol reuse, or productive syntax in any fly behaviour. Every documented fly behaviour is describable by a finite-state controller: a small set of persistent internal states, a ranked action competition, continuous vector state in the central complex, and a content-addressable associative table in the mushroom body. ESTABLISHED (as an absence of evidence across the whole behavioural literature).
- Mathematics that does transfer, and is confirmed in this repository's own extraction: random, sparse PN->KC convergence with fan-in ~5-7 (Caron, Ruta, Abbott & Axel 2013 Nature 497:113; measured here at 5.16 / 5.07 PN types per KC) is near the theoretical optimum that maximises the dimension of the KC representation for the fly's parameters (Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky & Abbott 2017 Neuron 93:1153, optimum K* ~6). ESTABLISHED. The same motif as an algorithm (sparse random projection + winner-take-all = locality-sensitive hash) beats classical LSH on nearest-neighbour search (Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha 2017 Science 358:793) and produces competitive word embeddings at very short hash lengths (Liang, Ryali, Hoover, Grinberg, Navlakha, Zaki & Krotov, ICLR 2021, arXiv:2101.06887). ESTABLISHED and already verified in docs/aif/LITERATURE.md.

### Principles and their translation

| principle | biology / mathematics | translation to a sequence model | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Timing is a signed, biphasic eligibility kernel, not an instantaneous outer product. The same synapse depresses for forward CS->US pairing and potentiates for backward pairing, with a window of order seconds. | Tanimoto, Heisenberg & Gerber 2004 Nature 430:983 (event timing turns punishment to reward); Handler et al. 2019 Cell 178:60 (Dop1R1/cAMP depression vs Dop1R2/Rac potentiation on the same KC->MBON synapse); Galili et al. 2011 J Neurosci 31:17383 (trace conditioning across seconds-scale gaps). | Replace the DeltaNet/TTT instantaneous update dW_t = -eta_t k_t (v_hat_t - v_t)^T with a kernelised one: keep two exponential traces of the key, e^f_t = lam_f e^f_{t-1} + k_t and e^s_t = lam_s e^s_{t-1} + k_t, and write dW_t = -eta_t (a e^f_t - b e^s_t) (v_hat_t - v_t)^T. The difference of exponentials is a biphasic kernel with a learnable zero-crossing; a and b (or lam_f, lam_s) are learned. Cost: O(d) extra state, no extra matmul. This is exactly the degree of freedom that current test-time-learning models do not have, and it is the single most concrete, most testable fly-derived modification available. Falsifier: if a learned scalar gate on the instantaneous update matches it on delayed-credit tasks, the principle buys nothing (the phase-1 error-gate result warns this is possible). | established |
| Never overwrite: contradicting evidence writes an OPPOSING trace into a different compartment and the readout is a signed sum. Old memory survives, which is why extinguished memory spontaneously recovers. | Felsenberg et al. 2017 Nature 544:240 and 2018 Cell 175:709 (extinction is a new opposing memory, not erasure); Aso et al. 2014 eLife 3:e04577/e04580 (~20 compartments, each with its own DAN, MBON and valence). This repository's own MaleCNS extraction shows the anatomical substrate: MBON transmitters are 25 acetylcholine vs 10-11 GABA + 13 glutamate per hemisphere, i.e. the readout is roughly half excitatory and half inhibitory. | A fast-weight bank of C compartments M_1..M_C with distinct decay rates gamma_c, readout v = sum_c s_c M_c q with fixed signs s_c in {+1,-1} and a learned mixing. Updates are additive-only: a contradiction at test time is written with opposite sign into a compartment that was not the one that stored the original. Predicted behaviours an experiment can test directly: (i) no catastrophic overwrite on rebind-then-revert (the original key-value pair returns without re-training), (ii) graceful degradation instead of cliff-edge forgetting, (iii) spontaneous recovery, which a delta-rule memory cannot show. This gives a regime where a fly-aligned memory can genuinely beat Gated DeltaNet / Titans: re-binding and reversion, not raw MQAR accuracy. | established |
| Serial order in the fly comes from a ranked competition among simultaneously active programs plus slow internal-state switching, NOT from a learned transition model. | Seeds et al. 2014 eLife 3:e02951 (grooming suppression hierarchy: all programs activated in parallel, order set by fixed inhibitory ranks and falling drive); Calhoun, Pillow & Murthy 2019 Nat Neurosci 22:2040 (courtship = ~3 persistent hidden states, each a different sensorimotor GLM, switching on a tens-of-seconds timescale); Coen et al. 2014 Nature 507:233 (song mode chosen from instantaneous feedback). | A sticky discrete latent z_t (S ~ 4-16 states, p(stay) ~ 0.99) that GATES the output head and the memory write policy, inferred online by a forward filter over the model's own prediction errors. Concretely: logits_t = W_{z_t} h_t + b_{z_t} with a low-rank per-state modulation, and z_t updated by a hard-EM / streaming forward step. This is the only 'syntax-like' mechanism biology offers here, and it is architecturally cheap. It predicts gains exactly where the fly's version helps: long documents with topic/register switches, and continual streams, not on i.i.d. perplexity. NOTE: the failed AIF-H3 arousal idea was a scalar version of this; the state version is discrete, sticky and gates the head, which is a different and stronger hypothesis. | promising |
| Durable storage requires temporally SPACED recurrence of the same error, plus an offline phase. One big surprise is not enough; the same surprise on separated occasions is. | Tully et al. 1994 Cell 79:35 (10 cycles x ~15 min rest -> 7-day protein-synthesis-dependent LTM; the same 10 cycles massed -> only ARM); Placais et al. 2012 Nat Neurosci 15:592 (spaced training induces sustained DAN oscillations that gate LTM); Donlea et al. 2011 Science 332:1571 and Dag et al. 2019 eLife 8:e42786 (sleep and post-learning reactivation consolidate memory). | A consolidation gate from fast weights to slow weights with a spacing requirement. Per memory slot i keep a slow 'CREB' counter c_i <- c_i * exp(-dt/tau_c) + 1[surprise_i > theta], and consolidate slot i into the slow weights only when c_i >= R AND the increments were separated by >= Delta tokens. Run consolidation in an offline pass (replay the stored sparse keys, one or a few gradient steps on the slow weights), between documents or between episodes. This turns the memory branch into a CONTINUAL-LEARNING claim, which is a genuinely open frontier with no strong incumbent, unlike perplexity-per-FLOP. The spacing test itself is the novelty: nobody in the test-time-learning literature gates consolidation on the temporal separation of repeated errors. | promising |
| Learning rate is a state variable set by neuromodulators and by internal state, and forgetting is an actively controlled rate, not decay. | Krashes et al. 2009 Cell 139:416 (hunger gates appetitive memory expression); Shuai et al. 2010 Cell 140:579 (Rac1 sets forgetting rate); Berry et al. 2012 Neuron 74:530 (ongoing dopamine actively erases via DAMB); Rajagopalan et al. 2023 PNAS 120:e2221415120 (DAN reward responses scale down with expectation). | eta_t and gamma_t are both outputs of a small controller driven by running statistics of prediction error: eta_t = eta_0 * sigmoid(alpha * log(recent squared error / long-run squared error) + beta), gamma_t likewise. This is the ONE surviving piece of active inference (inferred precision = learning rate), and it is the piece phase 2 tested least (AIF-H2 was never run to completion). It must be tested against a learned input-dependent gate with the same inputs, because phase 1 already showed a learned gate beats a hand-specified error gate when the world is stationary. The honest hypothesis is narrower: the inferred-precision rule wins only under NON-STATIONARITY, where the learned gate has no training signal for the regime it now faces. | promising |
| Two addressing schemes, deliberately different: discrete items live in a sparse random hash with fan-in ~6 and ~5% coding; continuous quantities live on phasors and are combined by vector arithmetic. | Caron et al. 2013 Nature 497:113 and Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 Neuron 93:1153 (random fan-in 5-7 is near the dimension-maximising optimum K* ~6); Seelig & Jayaraman 2015 Nature 521:186, Kim et al. 2017 Science 356:849, Hulse et al. 2021 eLife 10:e66039, Lyu, Abbott & Maimon 2022 Nature 601:92 (sinusoidal heading code, vector addition and rotation by phase shift). Algorithmic confirmation: Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha 2017 Science 358:793; Liang et al. ICLR 2021 (FlyVec). | Address the fast memory by a sparse binary hash h_t = kWTA_k(R x_t) with R a fixed sparse random binary matrix of fan-in 6 and k ~ 0.05 n, and carry POSITION and continuous structure on phasors (rotary embeddings are already this). Memory cost becomes O(k d) per token with k << n, satisfying the sparsity axiom. The near-orthogonality of sparse random codes is what limits interference, and it is a theorem, not a hope. Warning from phase 1: the REAL measured PN->KC wiring is indistinguishable from random wiring of the same fan-in (H1d, -0.1 seed-SD), so use random wiring with the fly's statistics, and say so. | established |
| The fly's learning signal is a LOCAL, per-compartment reward-prediction error carried by dopamine, closed by MBON->DAN feedback. It is not a global error broadcast, and the evidence is that flies do not show cue-competition (blocking). | Rajagopalan et al. 2023 PNAS 120:e2221415120 (expectation reduces DAN response; operant matching); Hattori et al. 2017 Cell 169:956 (novelty); Eschbach et al. 2020 Nat Neurosci 23:544 (MBON->DAN recurrent architecture in the larva); Gkanias, McCurdy, Nitabach & Webb 2022 eLife 11:e75611 (an incentive-circuit model reproduces extinction, reversal, second-order and unpaired conditioning with only local information and CORRECTLY predicts the absence of blocking). Math link: Loewenstein & Seung 2006 PNAS 103:15224 show matching behaviour follows generically from covariance-based plasticity. This repository measured the anatomical substrate: 339 / 300 MBON->DAN feedback edges (2861 / 2605 contacts) per hemisphere. | Each memory compartment computes its own error against its own readout and gates its own write; there is no summed cross-cue error term. Engineering consequence: the write gate is per-slot, d_c = (v_c_target - M_c q), and slots do not compete for a shared error budget. Contrast with a Rescorla-Wagner / delta-rule bank where a shared prediction subtracts across slots. This is a concrete, falsifiable architectural difference and it predicts LESS cue competition and therefore less interference in multi-key binding. UNCERTAIN: the empirical absence of blocking in flies is contested; Brembs & Heisenberg 2001 found none in the flight simulator, but the literature is not unanimous. | promising |
| The mushroom body is a context/generalisation gate over a memory that other circuits can also read; the fly's competence is split into specialised systems rather than one general learner. | Wolf et al. 1998 Learn Mem 5:166 (visual/tactile/motor learning without MB); Liu, Wolf, Ernst & Heisenberg 1999 Nature 400:753 (MB required for CONTEXT GENERALISATION of visual memory); Ofstad, Zuker & Reiser 2011 Nature 474:204 (place learning needs ellipsoid body, not MB); Brembs & Plendl 2008 Curr Biol 18:1168 (operant self-learning is PKC-dependent, MB-independent). | Do not build one monolithic memory. Build (a) a content-addressed item memory (MB analogue), (b) a continuous state tracker with a learned sensory->state alignment (CX analogue; note the alignment itself is learned Hebbian in minutes, Fisher 2019 / Kim 2019), (c) a slow policy/habit component. Read the item memory through a context key so that the same key retrieves different values in different contexts - that is what the MB's context gating does. Practical form: concatenate the sticky state z_t into the hash input so the address is context-dependent. | promising |
| Second-order conditioning is the fly's only chaining operation: an item that predicts a predictor inherits its value, through a hierarchy of dopaminergic compartments. | Tabone & de Belle 2011 Learn Mem 18:641; Yamada, Bushey, Li, ... Hige & Aso 2023 eLife (hierarchical dopaminergic architecture enabling second-order conditioning; UNCERTAIN on exact article number). The architecture is: MBON of compartment A drives the DAN of compartment B, so B's write signal is A's readout. | Stack memory compartments so that the error signal writing compartment B is the READOUT of compartment A: d_B = f(M_A q). This gives one-step value propagation without backpropagation through time, i.e. a local TD(0)-like bootstrap implemented as feedforward compartment stacking. Depth-2 is demonstrated in flies; deeper chains are unverified. For a language model this is the minimal mechanism for 'a token that predicts a predictive token', which is the closest biological analogue of a short dependency chain. | promising |
| Prediction in the fly is a FORWARD MODEL of the consequences of its own actions, not iterative inference over a latent world state. | Kim, Fitzgerald & Maimon 2015 Nat Neurosci 18:1247; Kim, Fenk, Lyu & Maimon 2017 Cell 168:280; Fujiwara, Cruz, Bohnslav & Chiappe 2017 Nat Neurosci 20:72 (corollary discharge quantitatively matched to expected optic flow during self-generated turns). | This is a direct correction to the phase-2 premise. The biology supports: (i) predict the sensory consequence of your OWN emitted token and subtract it (a cheap self-consistency / efference-copy term on the decoder side), and (ii) reward-prediction-error learning. It does NOT support K-step free-energy descent on a latent state per token, which AIF-H1 measured at +0.7 seed-SD. If active inference re-enters the design, it should enter as an action/decoding principle for agentic tasks with an information-gathering payoff, not as a per-token inference loop. | established |
| There is no order memory and no multi-item working memory in the fly, so a fly-aligned architecture is intrinsically order-blind and must import sequence structure from elsewhere. | Neuser et al. 2008 Nature 453:1244 (~4 s, one remembered target, ellipsoid body); Seeds et al. 2014 eLife 3:e02951 (order without a sequence memory); absence of any counter-evidence across the behavioural literature. | Accept this and design around it: keep a conventional, strong sequence backbone (transformer or SSM) trained by backpropagation - which plays the role of evolution, per the repository's own axiom A1 - and put the fly content ENTIRELY in the test-time learning system. Do not attempt a fly-derived sequence mixer; phase 1 already measured that path (ring attractor -9.1 seed-SD vs an unstructured layer). Position and continuous structure come from phasors (rotary embeddings), which is the one place fly biology and current practice already agree. | established |

### Numbers an engineer can use

- Aversive olfactory conditioning, one cycle: ~12 electric shock pulses (60-90 V) over 60 s paired with odour; immediate performance index up to ~0.8-0.9; decays to near zero by ~24 h (Tully & Quinn 1985 J Comp Physiol A 157:263).
- Long-term memory requires 10 training cycles with ~15 min inter-trial rest; massed 10 cycles with no rest give only anaesthesia-resistant memory. Spaced LTM lasts ~7 days and is blocked by cycloheximide (Tully et al. 1994 Cell 79:35). The spacing interval (~15 min) is the single most useful number for a consolidation schedule.
- One-trial appetitive learning: a single ~2 min odour + sucrose pairing gives memory lasting hours (Krashes & Waddell 2008 J Neurosci 28:3103).
- Trace conditioning gap: associations form across CS-US gaps up to roughly 15-20 s, falling steeply with gap length (Galili et al. 2011 J Neurosci 31:17383). UNCERTAIN on the exact cutoff; treat as 'order 10 s, not order 100 s'.
- Plasticity sign flip: forward pairing (CS before US) -> aversive; backward pairing (US before CS, order 10-30 s) -> appetitive relief memory (Tanimoto et al. 2004 Nature 430:983; Handler et al. 2019 Cell 178:60). The zero-crossing of the eligibility kernel is near simultaneity; exact width UNCERTAIN without the Handler 2019 figures.
- Visual/spatial working memory: ~4 s for ONE remembered target position, lost with distraction (Neuser et al. 2008 Nature 453:1244). This is the fly's entire working-memory capacity as measured.
- Extinction: on the order of 2 unreinforced CS presentations suffice to extinguish, and the original memory persists underneath (Felsenberg et al. 2017 Nature 544:240). UNCERTAIN on the exact number of presentations.
- Mushroom body per hemisphere (MaleCNS v1.0, measured in this repository, docs/CIRCUITS.md): 2019 (L) / 2045 (R) Kenyon cells; ~120 projection neurons in ~60 PN types; KC fan-in 5.16 / 5.07 PN types (median 5); PN fan-out ~83 KCs; 1 APL neuron (GABA) contacting 100% / 99.95% of KCs; 48 / 49 MBONs across 32 compartment labels; 158 PAM and 8 PPL1 dopaminergic neurons; 339 / 300 MBON->DAN feedback edges carrying 2861 / 2605 contacts.
- MBON output sign balance (measured here): 25 cholinergic vs 10-11 GABAergic + 13 glutamatergic per hemisphere, i.e. ~51% excitatory / ~49% inhibitory readout - the anatomical basis for summed opposing memory traces.
- Coding level: ~5% of Kenyon cells active per odour, enforced by the single APL feedback neuron (standard figure in the MB literature; consistent with this repository's k-WTA setting of 5%).
- Optimal fan-in: K* ~ 6 maximises the dimension of the KC representation for the fly's parameters (Litwin-Kumar et al. 2017 Neuron 93:1153), matching the measured 5-7 (Caron et al. 2013 Nature 497:113). This is the one place where the connectome's number is also the mathematically optimal number.
- Central complex (measured here): 46 EPG, 20 PEN_a, 22 PEN_b, 18 PEG, 42 Delta7, 148 core neurons; EPG->EPG connection peaks at 0 deg offset, EPG->Delta7 peaks at 180 deg, effective EPG->Delta7->EPG inhibition is minimal at 0 deg (117) and maximal at 180 deg (1873) - a textbook ring attractor with global inhibition.
- Visual-scene-to-heading remapping is learned in MINUTES in a novel arena (Fisher et al. 2019 Nature 576:121; Kim et al. 2019 Nature 576:126). Order-of-magnitude: tens of bump-anchoring events, not thousands of gradient steps.
- Courtship internal states: ~3 hidden states, persisting tens of seconds, each with its own sensorimotor mapping (Calhoun et al. 2019 Nat Neurosci 22:2040). Use S ~ 3-16 for a sticky-state gate.
- Grooming: a fixed anterior-to-posterior order over roughly 6 body-part programs (eyes, antennae, head, abdomen, wings, thorax), produced by a suppression hierarchy (Seeds et al. 2014 eLife 3:e02951).
- Whole brain: 166,700 neurons and 10,520,431 edges >= 3 contacts (101,422,396 contacts), 30.8% inhibitory, measured density 0.038% (this repository, MaleCNS v1.0; note LIMITATIONS.md records a discrepancy with an externally quoted 0.52%). FlyWire female brain for comparison: ~139,255 neurons, ~54.5 M synapses (Dorkenwald et al. 2024 Nature; Schlegel et al. 2024 Nature). Larval brain: 3,016 neurons (Winding et al. 2023 Science 379:eadd9330).
- FlyVec (fly-circuit word embeddings, Liang et al. ICLR 2021, arXiv:2101.06887; numbers already verified in docs/aif/LITERATURE.md): at hash length k=4 it beats binarised GloVe/word2vec baselines (MEN 34.0 vs 16.9/35.5/23.6; WS353 43.2 vs 8.2/26.0/20.2), and binary codes use ~3% of the memory of float embeddings, at a training cost far below GloVe on the same corpus. Overall it is BELOW word2vec. This is the existence proof of a defensible regime-specific claim from fly circuitry, and also a caution about its size.
- FlyHash (Dasgupta, Stevens & Navlakha 2017 Science 358:793): sparse binary random projection + winner-take-all gives ~20x lower projection cost than classical LSH at equal or better nearest-neighbour quality.
- Prior null results in this project that bound expectations: real connectome mask vs degree-preserving shuffle 0 of 3 sizes beyond threshold; real PN->KC wiring vs shuffle -0.1 seed-SD; extracted ring attractor 0.2436 vs unstructured RNN 0.6688 accuracy (-9.1 seed-SD); MB memory 0.9814 vs Gated DeltaNet 0.9979 on MQAR (-5.6 seed-SD); K=3 free-energy steps vs K=0 only +0.026 bits/char (+0.7 seed-SD).

### Dead ends

- Expecting the fly to supply SYNTAX or compositionality. No fly behaviour requires more than a finite-state description. Grooming order is a priority list with mutual inhibition (Seeds et al. 2014), courtship is a ~3-state HMM (Calhoun et al. 2019), and the only chaining operation is depth-2 second-order conditioning. There is no recursion, no symbol reuse, no productive combination, and no evidence of hierarchical structure building anywhere in the repertoire. Any design that claims to get compositional language FROM fly biology is not supported by the behavioural record.
- Expecting a working-memory buffer or order memory from fly biology. The measured capacity is ONE spatial target for ~4 s (Neuser et al. 2008 Nature 453:1244). A fly-aligned memory is content-addressed and order-blind by construction; sequence position must be imported from phasor/rotary codes, not derived from the fly's memory system.
- Using the measured wiring as structure. Already falsified in this repository: whole-graph mask indistinguishable from degree-preserving shuffle at all three sizes; real PN->KC wiring vs shuffle -0.1 seed-SD; the 148-neuron central-complex ring 0.24 vs 0.67 for an unstructured layer of equal size (-9.1 seed-SD). Use the STATISTICS (fan-in 6, 5% coding, ~50/50 signed readout, ~20 compartments), never the adjacency matrix.
- Per-token iterative free-energy descent on a latent state as the core algorithm. Measured at +0.7 seed-SD vs K=0 and losing to a wider control (AIF-H1), and the biology does not ask for it: the fly's prediction machinery is an efference-copy forward model (Kim et al. 2015, 2017; Fujiwara et al. 2017) plus reward-prediction-error learning (Rajagopalan et al. 2023), not iterative inference. The amortisation gap closes with training; there is nothing left for the inner loop to recover.
- Hand-specified error gates in place of learned gates, in STATIONARY settings. Phase 1 measured a learned gate beating the error gate by -2.6 seed-SD on MQAR. A learned gate with the same inputs contains the fixed rule as a special case. Any fly-derived gate must be tested only where the learned gate has no training signal, i.e. under distribution shift at test time.
- A global Rescorla-Wagner style summed prediction error across cues. The fly's error signals are per-compartment and local, closed by MBON->DAN feedback, and the leading circuit model reproduces extinction, reversal, second-order and unpaired conditioning while CORRECTLY predicting the ABSENCE of blocking (Gkanias, McCurdy, Nitabach & Webb 2022 eLife 11:e75611). UNCERTAIN: the empirical status of blocking in Drosophila is contested, so treat this as a design hypothesis rather than settled.
- Spiking neural networks justified as 'fly-aligned'. Many fly central and peripheral neurons are graded/non-spiking rather than spiking, so 'the fly spikes' is not an accurate premise; the quality gap to dense models on language is large; and the energy argument requires neuromorphic hardware that this project does not have. The behavioural record gives no reason to prefer spikes - it gives reasons to prefer sparse codes, signed eligibility kernels and gated rates, all of which are implementable in dense arithmetic.
- Reservoir computing as the BACKBONE. Two independent negative results are already recorded in docs/aif/LITERATURE.md: a MaleCNS connectome reservoir for token-level tagging scored F1 0.2753 +/- 0.068 vs 0.2782 +/- 0.061 for a degree-preserving shuffle and 0.2727 for byte-only (paired difference -0.0029 +/- 0.028, a clean null), and a fly-circuit LM where MBON readout was actively damaging (5.5975 vs 6.1629 nats/token) and shuffled wiring was the best condition. The fly's expansion is an ADDRESS SPACE for a memory, not a dynamical backbone.
- Contrastive / local learning (forward-forward, predictive coding, equilibrium propagation) for the SLOW weights. The repository's own sweep records standard predictive coding at perplexity 523 vs backprop 120 on a masked-LM setup, with incremental PC reaching 106 only in the masked case and losing badly in the causal case (142.5 vs 113.3). Backpropagation plays the role of evolution in the axiom set; replacing it is a different research programme with a large measured gap.
- Claiming perplexity-per-FLOP wins against a well-tuned transformer or Gated DeltaNet. No biologically derived architecture has ever done this, the fly brain was not optimised for it, and both prior phases confirmed the pattern. Any state-of-the-art claim must be in a regime the fly actually excels at: one-shot binding, re-binding under non-stationarity, retention beyond the context window, continual adaptation without forgetting, and compute per token.
- Treating anatomical synaptic-contact counts as synaptic strengths, or treating the graph's neurotransmitter predictions as measured signs. Already recorded in LIMITATIONS.md; it remains a live trap for any new design that wants to initialise from the connectome.

### Open questions

- What is the exact shape and width of the CS-US eligibility kernel? Handler et al. 2019 Cell 178:60 has the data (Dop1R1 vs Dop1R2 time constants and the interval sweep), but the figures could not be retrieved when this note was written. The designer needs the zero-crossing and the two lobe widths to parameterise lam_f, lam_s in the biphasic write rule. Until then, treat the kernel parameters as learnable with a biphasic inductive bias.
- Do flies show BLOCKING and negative patterning (A+, B+, AB-)? The Webb-lab incentive-circuit model predicts no blocking and reproduces 92 interventions at r = 0.76, and Brembs & Heisenberg 2001 reported no blocking in the flight simulator, but the literature is not unanimous and honeybees do show configural learning. This determines whether the memory's error signal should be per-slot (no cue competition) or summed (cue competition). It is the single highest-leverage unresolved biological question for the write rule.
- How many independent odour-valence associations can a mushroom body hold at once? There is no direct behavioural measurement. Theory gives a perceptron-style bound (order 2 x 2000 patterns per MBON under idealised assumptions) but bounded, graded, compartment-shared weights make the real number far smaller. This matters because memory CAPACITY at fixed compute is one of the few axes where a defensible claim could live.
- How deep can second-order conditioning chain? Depth-2 is demonstrated; depth-3+ is not, to my knowledge. If chaining is strictly depth-2, the compartment-stacking bootstrap is a shallow mechanism, not a substitute for backpropagation through time.
- Are the mushroom-body compartment forgetting timescales logarithmically spaced, and what are their actual values in seconds/minutes/hours? The phase-2 model assumed decays of 4/16/64/256 without biological grounding. Aso & Rubin 2016 eLife 5:e16135 has compartment-specific decay data; the actual ratios should replace the guess.
- Is sleep reactivation CONTENT-SPECIFIC (replay of the trained pattern) or a global permissive state? Dag et al. 2019 eLife 8:e42786 suggests learning-related neurons specifically reactivate, which would justify selective replay of stored sparse keys rather than generic consolidation. If it is merely permissive, the consolidation design loses its selection rule.
- Does the fly's spacing requirement have a functional rationale beyond protein-synthesis kinetics, i.e. is spacing a good STATISTICAL test for 'this is a durable regularity rather than a coincidence'? If yes, the spacing gate is a principled replay-selection criterion rather than a biological quirk. This is an open theoretical question and a good target for a formal result (e.g. spacing as a sequential test of stationarity of the error process).
- Is there sensory preconditioning in Drosophila (learn A-B with no reinforcement, then B-US, and test A)? If yes, the fly learns stimulus-stimulus associations without reward, which is a stronger, more language-relevant capacity than value chaining. The status of this question could not be verified when this note was written.
- Can the central complex's phasor arithmetic bind more than one vector at a time (multiple goal vectors, multiple remembered sites)? Fan-shaped body vector memory is established for a small number of goals; the capacity limit is the relevant number for using phasors as a binding mechanism (vector-symbolic architectures assume many superposed bindings).
- CITATION VERIFICATION IS OUTSTANDING. Primary sources could not be reached when this note was written. Every citation above is from background knowledge with confidence marked inline; the ones I flag as UNCERTAIN on exact volume/article number are Ravbar/Zhang/Simpson 2021 eLife, Yamada et al. 2023 eLife article number, Kuntz et al. 2017 Cell Rep volume, and Brembs & Heisenberg 2001 venue. A verification pass with network access should precede any citation appearing in a paper.

### Relevance to language models

"The honest answer to the brief's central question is: the fly's behavioural repertoire contains almost nothing that is a precursor to language, and quite a lot that is a precursor to a good LIFETIME LEARNING SYSTEM. There is no recursion, no symbol reuse, no productive combination, and working memory is one item for four seconds. Serial order in fly behaviour is generated by a ranked suppression hierarchy (grooming, Seeds et al. 2014) and by slow switching among ~3 persistent internal states (courtship, Calhoun et al. 2019) - both finite-state, both feedback-driven, neither a learned transition model. The only genuine algebra in the brain is the central complex's phasor arithmetic (vector addition and rotation on sinusoidal codes), and the only chaining operation is depth-2 second-order conditioning. Together those are a minimal variable-binding apparatus in the vector-symbolic sense, but they are a long way from syntax, and no design should claim otherwise. The engineering consequence is sharp and should structure the whole phase-3 design: keep a conventional sequence backbone trained by backpropagation (which plays the role of evolution in this project's own axiom A1), carry position on phasors (rotary embeddings already are the CX's code), and put ALL of the fly content into the test-time learning system, where the biology is genuinely rich and current models are genuinely underspecified. Four specific things transfer with real content: a signed biphasic eligibility kernel over time (the forward/backward pairing sign flip, which no test-time-learning model has); additive parallel opposing traces across compartments with distinct decay rates and a signed summed readout (extinction is not erasure - this predicts no catastrophic overwrite and spontaneous recovery, which a delta rule cannot produce); a spacing-gated consolidation from fast weights to slow weights with an offline replay phase (the 10 cycles x 15 min result plus sleep reactivation); and an inferred, state-dependent learning AND forgetting rate. There is one existence proof that a fly-derived mechanism can win in a defined regime - FlyVec's short-hash word embeddings and FlyHash's nearest-neighbour search - and it is a modest, regime-bounded win, which is exactly the kind of claim that is available here."
